# Port architecture and parity

## Bulk posting edits and independent split checks

The bulk update follows the pinned Entry.bulk_update! split-date exception and independent manual-entry status. It uses the existing reviewed annotation selection, owner, revision and UTC review day. A reconciled timestamp is fixed when the review is generated and bound to approval. Date changes run through the ordinary statement-review triggers. Transfer details follow each selected leg, fee and leg dates must agree, and saved rate snapshots must remain eligible. Bill-payment links and savings goal evidence retain their original dates.

Migration 0071 adds a nullable split-line status and timestamp. Existing lines inherit the purchase status until explicitly checked. New manual and rule-created splits start unchecked. Split checks are independent of the existing statement workflow, which reconciles physical purchase totals. Financial JSON and ZIP exports use version 36. Readers accept versions 1 through 36.

## Reviewed bulk deletion (schema unchanged)

The pinned `Transactions::BulkDeletionsController` selects owned writable physical entries with `parent_entry_id: nil`; child entries are destroyed with a selected parent. `Transaction::Transferable` destroys the Transfer when a principal leg is destroyed, `Transfer` destroys fee transactions, and its `release_transactions` callback preserves an unselected counterpart. The port now implements that complete bulk scope, distinct from its existing explicit whole-transfer Delete operation. All accounts are manual and owner-scoped; no household permission model or provider is introduced.

`planBulkDeletion` accepts 1–20,000 unique owned root IDs, sorts the canonical selection and includes automatic transfer fees and every split child. Choosing one confirmed leg clears the surviving association and resets only the source's transfer kinds (`funds_movement`, `cc_payment`, `loan_payment`, `investment_contribution`) to standard, removing only a matching kind lock. Existing standard/one-time kinds and all pending kinds/locks remain. Reconciliation fields stay exact. Both selected legs are removed once; a fee already explicitly selected is not double counted. A fee selected alone removes its link and zeros the corresponding fee total while retaining principal legs, applied rate/amounts and original request fingerprint. Archived, excluded, zero and future roots are eligible. Deletion dates are independent from report valuation dates.

The pure proposed state explicitly models FK/trigger effects: split cascade, bill allocation unlinking while preserving conversion and settled occurrences, goal usage unlinking with applied conversion intact, saved pledge/price evidence without transaction links, receipt-association removal with file metadata retained, match-rejection cascades and affected completed-statement flags. It validates the complete proposed workspace. Accounts, all price/trade/holding/cash/total observations, contribution checkpoints, goal definitions/allocations and saved original source histories remain. Import claims on deleted rows cascade; financial restore does not recreate ownership. Files remain owner-private and downloadable, a deliberate port retention choice rather than an assertion of Rails blob-purge parity.

`ledgerReportingEffects` extracts the version-91 period/goal and detailed envelope comparison without changing annotation semantics. Both workflows include category spending, availability, shared availability and rollover even when period totals stay equal. Up to 20,000 derived effects are reviewed; oversized derived reports reject without writes. Current/chosen-date balances include every deleted or released account, retain missing/range issues rather than invented zeroes, distinguish investment cash/holdings, and show exact signed BigInt deleted-date sums per account. Those sums never stand in for a balance difference. Saved anchors may mask effects. Eight independent 25-row streams cover deletion roots, child lines, releases, transfer details/fee totals, links, balances, effects and statements; full CSV includes every stream, every removed fee link, all original objects and the canonical selection.

Authenticated same-origin POST `/api/bulk-deletion` uses strict body-only commands bounded to 4 MiB, enough for the full financial workspace's 160-character IDs. Owner/current revision/canonical selection/as-of date/UTC review date bind the SHA-256 receipt. Any navigation beyond an initial deletion page requires a complete stamp; provided stamps are checked even on page zero. Export/commit always require it. UI state immediately hides changed contexts and aborts stale requests. The complete UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe CSV streams one consistent read snapshot with cancellation and no financial writes. Whole-filter selection, full streams, a financial ZIP link, scope acknowledgement and literal `DELETE SELECTED TRANSACTIONS` precede approval.

Approval uses ordinary ledger epoch 28 and one guarded D1 batch: remove fee links, revise isolated fee totals, release surviving legs, restore their original reconciliation fields after ordinary kind-reset triggers, detach bill links, delete roots in 500-ID chunks and remove transfer details. Restoring unchanged reconciliation fields within the same batch preserves source entry evidence; the reserved investment-repair epoch 36 is not reused. Existing cascades and retention/review triggers run normally. A fault at final transfer-detail deletion restores all earlier records, links, manual locks, statement flags and actual CSV ownership. The revision barrier is atomic; pre-existing unconditional price-history revision triggers can add increments when their ledger link clears. No historical trigger/schema is rewritten or counter artificially compensated. Concurrent approvals have one winner; missing rows can cause 404 on replay. No permanent completion marker or dedicated undo is introduced.

All 2,399 behavioral tests, strict TypeScript, 119 focused Worker API calls and 1,318 related calls across thirteen suites pass (1,437 across fourteen). Full-limit evidence covers 19,999 transaction deletions in a 20,000-record graph, complete last page/full CSV and one explicit revision increment when no price history is detached. The main graph covers 500 saved converted payments and settled occurrences, converted goal use, savings matches, bill price history, 501 closed statements, 50 goals, category budgets/rollover, confirmed FX and pending survivors, receipt bytes and real staged-CSV claims. It verifies exact proposed-versus-persisted state, current first/final pages, complete read-snapshot/cancellation behavior, stale/tampered scope/date/receipt rejection, late rollback, races, fee-only edits and historical extra price revision tracking. Five exact owner graphs and every final pending review section/CSV are retained for fresh-Worker validation. The actual pinned Ruby release method matches 216 controlled transaction/entry/existence/association cases and 144 survivors; split-root and fee-cascade declarations are inspected, not full Rails/ActiveRecord execution.

All 71 SQLs, 63 snapshots, journal, Drizzle schema, hosting and package lock (138 tracked files) remain byte-identical to version 91; dependency declarations remain identical. Schema 70/69 expected user tables and backup 35 remain unchanged. No migration, financial backfill, provider, secret, schedule, bank/broker integration or household sharing is added. Build/package identity and complete fresh-Worker persistence gate publication. Hosted browser/sign-in/responsive/keyboard QA remain unverified. Bulk dates/reconciliation and broader personal-finance parity remain unfinished.

## Previous milestone: reviewed bulk annotations (schema unchanged)

The pinned `Transactions::BulkUpdatesController` selects owned annotatable entries excluding split parents. `Entry.bulk_update!` applies name/notes/category/merchant values and handles explicitly requested tags separately. The personal port now adapts this annotation subset through typed transaction/split-line selections of 1–100 unique entries. Parent selection rejects, rather than silently editing hidden children. Every selected identity and all category/merchant/tag references must belong to the owner. Archived, scheduled and excluded rows can be annotated without changing their financial status. A selection includes transfer legs independently; counterparts and applied transfer details stay intact.

`lib/bulk-annotations.ts` is a pure proposed-state planner. Omitted fields stay unchanged; enabled fields replace values. Tags replace rather than append. Explicit empty notes/tags and null category/merchant clearing are port extensions to the source's compact-blank behavior. Changed name/category/merchant/tag fields preserve existing locks and add only corresponding existing manual rule locks; notes have no supported rule action. Identity, amounts, accounts, dates, import keys, kind/activity/exclusion, split grouping, payments/goals, reconciliation and statement snapshots remain recorded. The entire proposed workspace is validated before a review is returned.

The review includes every selected before/after record, unchanged counts and a full CSV. Category changes use existing period/goal comparisons plus explicit category-row spending/availability/rollover comparisons, since a reassignment can leave overall period totals equal. Effects have independent 25-row pages, a 20,000-effect rejection bound and a complete streaming CSV; there is no silent truncation. Existing aggregate 20,000-record and validated field-length limits remain in force. Acknowledgement and a financial-backup link precede approval. Dates, reconciliation status, bulk deletion and complete source bulk-update parity remain unfinished; this milestone does not invent household permissions.

Authenticated same-origin POST `/api/bulk-annotations` uses a strict bounded JSON command, with no query options. Owner/current revision/canonical typed selection/patch/UTC review date bind the SHA-256 receipt. Nonzero pages require a complete stamp; supplied stamps are checked even on page zero. Export and commit always require it. The UI immediately hides stale contexts, cancels obsolete requests and guards return-to-first-page navigation. The complete CSV is a consistent in-memory read snapshot with UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula escaping. Review/export/cancellation do not write finance.

Approval writes only changed columns in one `commitMutation` batch using established ledger writer epoch 28, allowing current manual-lock triggers to apply. One revision advance covers all changes; a no-op runs an atomic revision guard without incrementing it. A fault at the final selected root rolls back earlier roots, the selected split and locks. Concurrent approvals have one success and one stale rejection. No dedicated undo or permanent idempotency marker is introduced: uncertain-response handling requires reload. Existing format-35 recovery retains facts and locks with remapped identities.

Validation: thirteen new behavioral cases, all 2,383 behavioral tests, 74 focused actual Worker API calls and 863 related calls across nine suites (937 across ten), plus strict TypeScript. A full 100-target graph contains one split child, independent sibling/parent, imported/excluded zero memo, 50 linked goals, category allocations and completed-statement evidence. Checks verify current last-page and full CSV consistency, stale scope/revision/date/receipts, exact proposed-versus-persisted objects/locks, final-update rollback, single-winner approval, no-op revision stability, explicit clears and independently annotated transfer legs. Two exact owner graphs and a full-limit pending preview/final effects page/CSV are retained for the complete fresh-Worker corpus. Source controller/model inspection establishes the adapted scope; the full Rails method/callbacks are not executed by these tests. One legacy classification regression uses a raw loopback HTTP client incompatible with the direct Worker harness; the passing investment-kind repair suite covers related transfer reporting without changing that legacy test.

All 71 SQL files, 63 snapshots, journal, Drizzle schema, hosting and package lock (138 tracked files) remain byte-identical to version 90, and dependency declarations are identical. Schema 70/69 expected tables and backup 35 remain unchanged. No migration, financial backfill, provider, secret, schedule, bank/broker integration or household sharing is added. Final build/package identity and fresh-Worker persistence are publication gates. Hosted browser/sign-in/responsive/keyboard QA remains unverified. Broader personal-finance parity continues.

## Previous milestone: complete reviewed holding/trade removal (schema unchanged)

The source’s manual holding deletion performs `account.entries.where(entryable: account.trades.where(security: security)).destroy_all`, destroys the selected holding and queues an account sync. The port now exposes this complete scope separately from its existing observation-only delete: one selected snapshot plus every same-account/security trade, regardless of date, activity, exclusion or import provenance. No other holding date or account/security scope is silently included. All accounts are manual in the user-confirmed no-bank port; archived investment/crypto accounts remain eligible. The port recalculates its derived reports synchronously from surviving records instead of using Rails’ materialization queue or persisted lot rows.

`/api/holding-removal` is an authenticated same-origin POST workflow with strict review/export/commit bodies. A current owner/revision/holding/as-of/review-date receipt guards navigation, complete export and typed approval. Each deletion stream, changed position, budget/goal effect and affected completed statement uses independent 25-row pages. The report lists the original selected observation, its basis locks/source/applied conversion, every trade with original notes/import key/conversion, exact cross-date cash/fee sums and the actual dated before/after valuation. Aggregate signed minor-unit sums use BigInt strings and are not treated as a balance difference or an extra fee deduction. Future trades are included even when a past valuation date is selected. Other dated holdings and cash/total anchors may keep positions or totals visible.

Complete CSV streams a consistent read snapshot in UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe rows, including every original object and conversion plus full valuation/effect/statement streams and notes. Only one CSV row is materialized at a time; the existing aggregate 20,000-record workspace bound and validated field lengths bound input. Review/export/cancellation never writes finance. Every proposed surviving workspace is validated before success. The UI offers financial backup download, an explicit scope acknowledgement and exact `REMOVE HOLDING AND TRADES` confirmation. It invalidates changed revision/holding/date context and guards every page, including returning to page zero.

Approval uses existing investment writer epoch 6 and one `commitMutation` batch: owner/account/security-scoped trade deletion, owner/ID-scoped selected snapshot deletion, completed-statement flags and one profile revision increment. A late holding-delete failure rolls back earlier trades and cascading claims. Concurrent approvals have one success; a later replay can return 404 because the selected holding is gone. No permanent operation marker or dedicated undo is introduced, and neither UI nor API claims retry success without evidence. Lost-response messaging requires account reload. Financial recovery can recreate records with fresh identities; saved import ownership is not recreated. Existing raw source history/completions are retained while FK claims on deleted CSV/native trades cascade away.

Surviving physical ledger transactions, prices/splits, other snapshots, cash/total observations, applied FX, files, goal definitions and contribution checkpoints stay intact. Position lots, cost, disposal gains, cash estimates and linked backing recalculate. Affected completed statements retain their recorded snapshots and are marked for review. This follows the port’s existing explicit-observation representation rather than pretending to execute the source’s full ActiveRecord callbacks, lot materializer or background jobs.

All 2,370 behavioral tests and 1,950 API calls across nineteen suites pass: thirteen new behavioral cases, 150 focused Worker checks and 1,800 related checks across eighteen suites. A valid full 20,000-record graph verifies 19,997-trade complete preview/final page/CSV/atomic removal; exact cross-date totals beyond safe integers, signed short positions, 500 completed statements and 50 goal effects are covered. Tests check owner/origin/type/body/page safety, all dates/excluded activities, original raw CSV and applied FX, snapshot/cancellation semantics, stale receipts/as-of/review dates, late rollback/races, retained later holdings, remapped recovery, real CSV/native source claims/originals, and converted/archived graphs. Nine exact owner graphs plus pending first/final trade/statement/effect reviews and source-byte evidence are prepared for fresh-Worker verification. Strict TypeScript and changed-module lint pass. The actual pinned `Holding#destroy_holding_and_entries!` body matches 200 controlled relation cases and 2,773 deleted trades, including one source sync; this is scope evidence, not full Rails execution.

Schema 70, all 71 SQL files, prior snapshots/journal/Drizzle schema/hosting, dependencies/lock, existing writer epochs, 69 expected user tables and financial JSON/ZIP 35 remain unchanged. No provider, financial backfill, secret, schedule, bank/broker integration or household sharing is introduced. Final build/package identity and complete fresh-Worker persistence are publication gates. Hosted browser/sign-in/keyboard/responsive QA remain unverified. The previous live Yahoo probe was rate-limited; this workflow makes no provider requests. Broader personal-finance parity remains unfinished.

## Previous milestone: reviewed Yahoo security-price ingestion (schema 70)

This adapts pinned `Provider::YahooFinance#fetch_security_prices` and `Security::Price::Importer#import_provider_prices` for owner-approved explicit full fetches (`clear_cache: true`). Fixed Yahoo chart URLs receive a validated normalized ticker and UTC date bounds. The source’s configured MIC suffixes, reported-exchange currency fallback, GBp/ZAc major-unit conversion, seven-day anchor lookback and 3,652-day maximum history window are retained. Exact numeric lexemes use the shared provider JSON reader; prices round half up to the source decimal(19,4) boundary. Null/invalid/zero/unsupported closes are diagnosed, with last-valid duplicate-date selection. Original numeric tokens, timestamps, metadata and body bytes remain downloadable. The quote series is `indicators.quote[0].close`; adjusted-close is not substituted. Provider historical corporate-action revisions and the port’s recorded splits require review together.

Full-clear gap filling uses provider observations and the preceding usable close, rather than preserving an intervening DB quote under replacement. An exact requested-start DB close can supply an anchor; a clamped start can use preceding same-currency saved history. Without an anchor, filling advances to the first usable provider observation. Earlier gaps remain honest. Keep-existing is an explicit port choice and can change later carry. Kept zero manual prices remain recorded facts but cannot seed invented carried zero prices. Invalid-only/empty sources propose no prices. Recent carried rows are marked provisional in the audit; no unattended provisional-refetch/cache-on-read lifecycle or global listing-date state is introduced. UTC review dates and strict supported currency/range/row diagnostics are port extensions. The source comparison exercises positive observations, with edge differences covered separately.

Yahoo chart requests are anonymous by default. Hard 401 or soft Unauthorized can perform the source’s one fresh anonymous cookie/crumb fallback through constant `fc.yahoo.com` and `query1.finance.yahoo.com` endpoints, within one bounded timeout. Ephemeral session values are never persisted, put in saved URLs or returned in errors. Redirects, host/agent rotation and automatic rate-limit retries are excluded. The actual public probe returned HTTP 429; successful live or hosted retrieval is unverified. Controlled real-Worker tests verify both session paths and all failure/size/identity checks. No financial data or broker credentials leave the site; manual prices and CSV are independent of provider availability.

`price_feed_imports` holds up to ten owner-private pending/ready/complete/deleting sources. Immutable request options bind the security ID, original ticker/MIC, normalized symbol and dates. A later security identity edit invalidates unapproved source review. Conditional R2 writes preserve the first saved response; interrupted ready updates recover it without another network call. Original responses and completed audit manifests are checksum verified. Approval binds owner/source/options/revision/review date/choice. Quote writes, completed-statement review flags and the completion marker share an atomic D1 batch using existing FX epoch 6. Completed retries remain idempotent after later quote or security metadata edits. Cleanup is resumable and retains financial rows. Source/audit history is outside financial JSON/ZIP 35; actual prices retain the existing recovery representation.

Independent 25-row reviews cover all quotes, issues, uncovered dates, current account valuations, changed positions, budget/goal effects and affected completed statements. The full UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe CSV includes those entire streams. The exact approved audit is retained after later edits. Preflight validates the complete resulting financial workspace and caps the immutable audit at 16 MiB. Other limits: 4 MiB HTTP JSON, 9 MiB source envelope, 10,000 requested days, provider history clamp and unchanged aggregate 20,000 financial records. Pricing affects every owned account using that security, including archived accounts; total-value anchors can change derived cash estimates. Foreign pricing still follows the existing opt-in mode and dated FX requirements. Recorded money, acquisitions, holdings, contributions, goal definitions and applied conversions are preserved.

Schema 70 appends one source-history table and its indexes/lifecycle/approval guards. All 70 prior SQL files, prior snapshots, complete journal prefix, package lock, dependencies and hosting bindings remain unchanged. The new complete Drizzle snapshot represents all 69 current declarations without additional generated DDL. The isolated real D1 upgrade passes 654 assertions and 32 lifecycle checks, preserving all 68 earlier table fingerprints/indexes/triggers across 206,549 synthetic rows; a final migration fault rolls back. No financial backfill or backup-version change occurs.

All 2,357 behavioral tests, strict TypeScript and changed-module lint pass. Focused Worker verification covers 158 API calls, complete 3,653-price/clamped-gap history, all 500 account/position/statement and 50 goal effects, original/audit corruption, cookie/crumb privacy, exact decimal rounding, empty no-ops, source recovery after a D1 fault, concurrent fetch/approval, late rollback, stale receipts/dates/identity, retained accounting and history-only removal. Twenty related suites pass 2,220 additional calls (2,378 total across twenty-one). The actual pinned importer matches 185 controlled cases and 2,704 exact dated prices through relation/provider/four-place storage adapters, not full Rails. Historical daily-price tests now assert both archive/date revision writes introduced at schema 61; movement CSV comparisons bind dates/cash/fees without depending on random restored IDs. No financial implementation changed for those two corrections. Final build/package and complete fresh-Worker evidence are publication gates. Hosted browser QA and successful live Yahoo retrieval remain unverified. The full personal-finance port is unfinished; bank/broker integrations and households are excluded by the user.

## Previous milestone: reviewed Frankfurter v2 ingestion (schema 69)

The personal-only port now adapts pinned `Provider::Frankfurter` and `ExchangeRate::Importer#import_provider_rates` for explicit, owner-approved full-range fetching (`clear_cache: true`). The constant public HTTPS endpoint receives only pair/date parameters. Five lookback days supply a start anchor. For requested dates, valid provider observations take precedence over exact existing daily quotes, then the preceding usable quote carries forward. Without an anchor the loop advances to the first valid observation; prior existing requested-date quotes remain available for optional inverse backfill. Empty/invalid-only provider sources propose no writes. Duplicate valid observations use the last valid row and emit diagnostics. Existing manual rate range/precision remains in force; unsupported source rows are diagnosed. Inverses use exact arithmetic and round to twelve decimals; unsupported/zero reciprocals are skipped with diagnostics.

Public fetching is an explicit foreground operation, with keep-existing (default) and replacement review choices and an inverse toggle. Keep-existing can deliberately change later carry and inverse results from the source overwrite behavior. Existing rate IDs and private notes remain intact. Exact numeric JSON tokens are expanded with string arithmetic rather than binary floating-point conversion. The same decimal expansion implementation now also serves the existing XLSX reader without changing its accepted numbers. Current UTC bounds, private owner scoping, bounded source/audit storage, review receipts and immutable audits are port extensions. There is no automatic cache lookup/fill job, global pair-listing metadata, custom provider URL, bank/broker connection, secret or schedule.

`fx_feed_imports` stores at most ten owner-private source lifecycles. A stable request reserves a pending row before HTTP; conditional private R2 writes retain the first saved response. Recovery reuses that exact response after a failed ready update instead of contacting the provider again. Ready source identity is sealed; explicit same-origin approval binds owner, source hash, options, workspace revision, replacement policy and inverse choice. Quote writes, foreign-market statement review flags and the completion receipt share one guarded D1 batch using existing FX epoch 6. Concurrent/lost-response approvals are idempotent; stale choices reject. A deterministic immutable audit manifest is saved before the financial batch, verified by checksum and referenced by its atomic completion marker. Its completed downloads remain unchanged after later manual quote edits. History cleanup uses a resumable deleting marker and bounded private prefix deletion; removing it retains every financial quote and record.

Original provider JSON is byte-preserved, including BOM and exact numeric tokens. Reviews and CSV exports include every proposed row, candidate/before/after rate, original quote ID, API/database observation date, source row, carried date, preserved-note policy, provider diagnostic and unfilled day. Separate 25-row pages require the same current receipt. Complete CSV uses UTF-8 BOM, CRLF and formula escaping. Provider observation dates are not asserted to be underlying central bank publication dates. Reference rates are not live execution quotes. Limits are 10,000 requested calendar days, 2 MiB HTTP JSON, 5 MiB saved envelopes, 8 MiB audit manifests and the unchanged aggregate 20,000-record financial workspace bound. Completed audits are retained in private R2; financial JSON/ZIP version 35 includes actual quote rows but excludes provider history.

Migration 69 adds only the source-history table, indexes and lifecycle/approval triggers. Every prior SQL migration, journal entry, snapshot, dependency, lock file and hosting binding is retained. The isolated real D1 upgrade preserves all 67 prior table fingerprints, indexes and triggers across 206,549 synthetic rows; a final injected migration failure rolls back. There are 644 upgrade assertions and 32 lifecycle guard checks. The actual pinned Ruby importer matches 180 controlled explicit-full-fetch cases and 5,400 exact dated quotes under replacement/inverse choices. These controlled relation/provider adapters exercise the source importer, not full Rails or a live provider dataset. The separate actual public HTTP response probe is parsed without sending finance data.

All 2,342 behavioral tests and 192 focused Worker API checks pass. Focused checks cover actual Worker numeric-token support, full 20,000-rate approval/audit, every last page, precise originals, altered object rejection, independent diagnostics/gaps, keep/replace/inverse behavior, stale receipts, late atomic rollback, concurrent source/approval retries, saved-object recovery after a D1 fault, no-op completion, private ownership, retained snapshots in five copied nonempty finance graphs, closed market statement flags, overflow rejection, source limits and history-only removal. Fourteen related suites pass 1,551 additional API checks (1,743 total across fifteen suites). Strict TypeScript and changed-module lint pass. Complete fresh-Worker persistence passes, including eight exact FX-feed owner graphs, private originals/audits, pending first/final diagnostic and gap pages and full review CSV. Packaging is verified before publication. Hosted sign-in/keyboard/responsive/visual QA and broader personal-finance parity remain unfinished; banks, brokers and households are excluded by the user.

## Previous milestone: complete paged rule reviews and streamed audit CSV

The earlier Rules interface displayed only the first 200 changes. It now offers 50-change pages with first/previous/next/last controls and a complete CSV before applying the same proposed run. This review interface is a port extension over the existing adapted `Rule::Runner` behavior, not a new financial action. Every change identifies its responsible rule, root transaction, existing/proposed split reference, date, native recorded amount and all changed fields. Display names and exact raw before/after JSON both appear in CSV; existing reference identities remain explicit. Event counts can exceed the number of affected transactions because later rules can add other fields or act on newly created split lines.

The evaluator now exposes an iterator. Default callers retain the exact prior result shape and complete changes; manual legacy previews/commits collect only their first 200 changes while retaining full totals, unique affected-root counts, complete proposed state and atomic writes. A complete review collects only its selected 50-change page, counts every field/CSV byte and validates the full resulting workspace including run-log constraints. Export preflights that whole graph, then replays the same owned snapshot through a cancellable row stream. It does not accumulate a complete CSV or all changed-event objects. New proposed split IDs are deterministic review-only placeholders, collision-checked against original root/split IDs. Approval still creates actual UUIDs; no future saved identity is promised by a preview. Existing persisted split IDs stay unchanged.

Authenticated same-origin POST `/api/rule-review` supports strict review/export options, owned rule/account/transaction scopes, valid date ranges and 50-change offsets through the existing operation bound. A first-page request without a receipt starts a fresh review. Nonzero pages and all exports require a current revision/receipt; supplied guards on page zero are checked too, and partial-page export rejects. The interface supplies guards for every navigation, including returning to the first page. The existing `/api/finance` `ruleRun` commit accepts that same byte-compatible receipt over owner/revision/canonical options. Scope, protected-field override, rule order or workspace changes stale the review. The interface cancels obsolete loads/downloads on owner/revision/rule/scope changes. Explicit Preview starts a fresh review. Demo controls remain disabled.

CSV streams complete UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe rows with scope, revision and receipt context. A report is limited to 100 MiB of UTF-8 CSV before a successful response is returned; larger scopes require narrowing. The existing two-million rule-operation and regex/workspace-program budgets remain in force. A shared unchanged 20,000-total-record constant now also checks split growth and reserved run-log space during evaluation, preventing an already-invalid oversized graph from being materialized before final validation. Locks, priority, additive tags, stopping rules, selected inactive rules, scopes and split/goal/payment eligibility remain unchanged. Review/export/cancellation never changes financial rows or run history. Explicit approval retains the established atomic transaction and race/rollback guards.

Schema 68, all 69 SQL migrations, journal, Drizzle schema and hosting manifest (72 files), all dependencies and the complete package lock remain byte-identical to version 86. Financial JSON/ZIP format 35 and writer epochs stay unchanged. No table, column, migration, financial backfill, provider, secret or schedule is added. Backup recovery retains the existing financial facts; these reports are derived from a current owned snapshot rather than persisted new import ownership or immutable historical knowledge.

Validation adds 23 behavioral cases (2,327 total) and 114 focused real-workerd/D1 API checks. A complete 19,000-transaction run produces 57,000 changed events and 95,000 field rows, with all rule boundaries/final pages and full CSV verified. Coverage includes strict authentication/origin/body/scope controls, unchanged reads, byte-exact BOM/CRLF/Unicode/formula-safe output, snapshot consistency across concurrent later edits, stale/tampered reviews, cancellation, late log-insert rollback and a single-winner approval race, retained recorded money/reconciliation facts, stable proposed versus actual split identities, ZIP remapping, 100 MiB output rejection, regex budget rejection and early split-growth rejection at the existing record limit. Fifteen related suites pass 1,637 API checks (1,751 across sixteen total), covering existing regex rules, direct/staged CSV/rule imports, mapping/creation/reversal, native recovery, category/merchant merging, workbook extraction, core finance and migration. The historical full API corpus was not all repeated.

The actual retained version-86 evaluator executes 600 controlled cases across USD/JPY/KWD. Default serialized results are byte-identical for 17,286 changed events, 29,747 matches and 2,262 created split lines; bounded windows retain identical final states, summaries and total counts. Inputs remain unchanged. This is a differential against the prior port implementation, not a claim of executing full Rails/PostgreSQL advisory locks, callbacks or provider behavior. New record-growth rejection concerns graphs that already exceeded existing workspace validation limits.

Final strict TypeScript/build/package and the complete fresh-Worker persistence corpus are publication gates. The new probe checks seven exact owner graphs, approved 19,000-root finance, current pending first/final pages, legacy receipt interoperability, byte-exact complete CSV and saved split/ZIP recovery. All financial fixtures are synthetic local data; native production verification reads publication/access/schema metadata only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. The broader personal-finance port remains active; bank/broker connections and household sharing remain excluded.

Reference: [we-promise/sure at d194118](https://github.com/we-promise/sure/tree/d194118a3b80cd3f88d111e73ff5f6255c42c22b).

This is a functional reimplementation for the Sites runtime, not a Rails deployment or a static copy of Sure's screens. This port remains in progress; full upstream parity has not been reached. Current foreground recurring generation/edit/activation hooks, recoverable history reconstruction and virtual-pruning cutoffs are implemented; cycle-aware cleanup, interleaved payday markers and a responsive calendar agenda and reviewed loan principal/interest replay are implemented; unattended jobs remain pending. The latest sections below supersede earlier update-specific pending lists. Current schema is 68 (67 expected user tables), and financial JSON/ZIP exports use format 35 with formats 1–34 still accepted.

Current requested scope is personal finances only. Bank/broker connections and shared-household permissions are excluded. Earlier household-pending notes describe historical milestones and are superseded by this user-confirmed scope.

## Implemented architecture

- `app/`: React interface and server-rendered sign-in entry page.
- `app/api/finance/route.ts`: authenticated, uncached state reads and same-origin JSON commands.
- `lib/repository.ts`: D1 persistence; every query binds the authenticated owner, never a submitted owner ID.
- `lib/finance.ts`: integer money, account balances, spending, and chart calculations.
- `lib/csv.ts`, `lib/csv-formats.ts`, `lib/csv-schema.ts`: CSV parsing, explicit column/date/number/sign mapping, validation and safe spreadsheet export.
- `db/schema.ts`, `drizzle/`: schema with composite tenant foreign keys, import uniqueness, budget uniqueness, amount constraints, and immutable migration history.
- `worker/index.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, `scripts/vite/`: supported Sites starter runtime integration, preserved from the supplied template.
- `.openai/hosting.json`: Sites project identity, D1 records binding and private R2 originals binding.

No app-owned password or OAuth system is introduced. Sites dispatch supplies identity; membership and visit access remain platform-controlled. Do not accept these identity headers from untrusted callers on an independently hosted Worker.

## Accounting conventions

Money is an integer number of minor currency units, with validation before insertion. USD/EUR use cents; JPY/KRW use whole units. Transaction and account inputs are limited to 1,000,000,000,000 minor units; budget aggregates may use the exact safe-integer range. No binary floating-point parsing is used to convert decimal input to minor units.

All account balances are signed net asset values. Debt is negative; a payment into a credit card/loan increases its balance toward zero. Each account has an opening balance and date. Transactions before that date are rejected. Opening balance is before transactions on that date; transactions on or after it affect the current balance. Editing an opening balance changes historical reports too.

A transfer has two principal entries linked atomically in one D1 batch with a shared transfer ID. Both can be newly recorded, two existing entries can be paired, or one missing counterpart can be created for an existing entry. Same-currency principal amounts are equal/opposite; different-currency principals retain an applied rational quote and their actual amounts. Optional source/destination fees are separate standard expenses saved and deleted with the complete group. Directly editing principal or linked-fee financial figures is prohibited; labels and fee splits remain editable. Credit-card payments and internal movements are excluded from income and budget analytics. Loan payments and investment contributions count as outgoing budget spending; the incoming leg is excluded. This follows the distinction in Sure's `Transaction::BUDGET_EXCLUDED_KINDS`.

One-time transactions affect balances but are excluded from monthly income/spending and budgets. Positive standard entries are money in (including refunds), negative entries are money out. Category budget spending is the net of signed entries in that category, clamped to zero, so refunds reduce category spending. As a result, gross money-out totals can differ from net category spending. Give income its own category for a meaningful category breakdown.

All dates and the boundary for scheduled entries use UTC. Entries after today are stored but not applied to balances or current reports until their date. Current net worth is always as of today; the six-month chart uses historical month-end balances through the selected month, capped at today. Archives preserve balances and history.

Native reporting separates totals by currency. The opt-in combined Overview converts with explicit dated manual rates; missing quotes and out-of-range figures remain unavailable. Goal conversions now follow the update below; budget reporting now follows the conversion update below; manual recurring payments now follow the update below; securities retain their existing currency scope. No live market prices are fetched. Investment/crypto accounts now combine uninvested cash and a manually maintained securities portfolio. Property and vehicle accounts remain valuation-based. Automatic market pricing remains pending.

## Import and export behavior

CSV supports date, name (or description), amount, optional category, merchant, tags, excluded, notes, kind, and currency. Tags use a JSON array of names within the CSV field, allowing commas in tag names; tags, merchants, and categories must already exist. Exporting a split purchase emits its matching child lines; full JSON preserves its grouping. Up to 500 rows per file and 1 MB file size. Date must be YYYY-MM-DD; amounts use a decimal point, no thousands separators, negative for outflows. Categories must already exist. Transfers are intentionally excluded from CSV import and filtered CSV export to avoid unbalanced ledger entries; the full JSON export includes both transfer legs.

Each file imports to one selected account. An optional `account` column must match that account's name, protecting against accidentally merging a multi-account export. Mixed-currency rows are rejected.

The server validates every row before the atomic insert batch. SHA-256 fingerprints of date, name, signed amount, notes, kind, and occurrence index within the file make repeat imports idempotent, including repeated identical purchases within the same file. Matching identical rows across overlapping files may be skipped; this is not bank-provider reconciliation. Category changes do not change an import fingerprint. Deleting an imported record allows it to be imported again.

CSV exports prefix formula-like text with an apostrophe to keep spreadsheets from executing it. JSON preserves original strings, transfer pairs, and integer amounts. Restoring remaps source IDs under the signed-in user. Sites backup restore and a Sure export v2 adapter are implemented below; direct PostgreSQL import remains pending.

## Remaining parity work

1. **Migration and recovery:** staged large-finance-file imports, native attachment-manifest migration, native-export account mapping/merge, direct database conversion, and expanded native-export parity. Backup restore, a v2 NDJSON adapter, preview and a representative migration corpus are implemented.
2. **Reporting and ledger depth:** PDF statement parsing, additional spreadsheet formats, investment trade/quantity reconciliation (reviewed holding plus all related-trade removal is implemented), advanced rule providers/PostgreSQL regex compatibility/transfer actions, and paginated server-side analytics for large histories.
3. **Currencies and investments:** unattended/cache-on-read exchange-rate ingestion (reviewed foreground Frankfurter fetching is implemented), unattended market-price ingestion (reviewed foreground Yahoo fetching is implemented; provider availability applies), further cross-currency reporting, price/FX providers, expanded security-movement editing, tax-lot selection, expanded security taxonomy, time-weighted performance and benchmarks. Reviewed cross-currency budget copying and money-weighted returns are implemented in the latest updates. Manual dated rates, direct/inverse lookup, combined Overview conversion, cross-currency transfers and fee expenses, plus manual securities, trades, average basis, lot views, stock splits and dated prices are implemented.
4. **Households — excluded:** the user requested personal finances only. Membership, invitations, per-account sharing and shared-household reports are outside the port’s scope. Every signed-in owner has an isolated workspace.
5. **External connections:** Bank integrations and automatic bank syncing are excluded at the user’s request. They are not a completion requirement for this port. Focus on manual entry, CSV/native data migration, reporting, and investment workflows; no bank credentials or bank-provider configuration are needed.
6. **Files and assistance:** PDF extraction, additional spreadsheet formats, assistant provider configuration, streaming responses, and background task lifecycle. No provider credentials are requested or needed yet.
7. **Operations:** browser interaction/accessibility/visual QA, deployment smoke tests with a real sign-in, large-history performance tests, and data export/deletion workflows.

Each provider must be assessed separately: Sites cannot run the original Ruby libraries, Redis/Sidekiq worker, Docker Compose services, raw TCP database client, or arbitrary long-running process.

## Validation in this milestone

- Strict TypeScript check and production Worker build.
- Node behavioral tests covering precise money, dates, scheduled balances, transfers, currency separation, budget refunds, CSV edge cases, and command validation.
- HTTP integration tests against the actual built Worker and local D1, covering authenticated reads/writes, 401/403/415 errors, create/edit/delete, category unlinking, budget upserts, atomic transfer deletion, currency guards, idempotent imports, all-or-nothing invalid import handling, and cross-tenant ownership checks.
- A separate persistence probe checks the saved state after restarting the Worker.
- No browser automation or visual QA has been performed yet.

## Recovery and Sure migration update

The Sites backup format is now version 14; restores also accept Sites versions 1–13. Settings downloads a fresh server snapshot rather than exporting a potentially stale browser view. Backups include category parent links, excluded transaction/account flags, and absolute balance history. Owner identities and source IDs are never imported as authorization: records receive fresh UUIDs and belong only to the current signed-in user.

Settings → **Import or restore a workspace** supports Sites JSON and `all.ndjson` extracted from a Sure export whose `version.txt` says `export_version: 2`. The adapter follows the pinned Sure exporter. Select the date that export was generated; its Account balances become explicit end-of-day snapshots on that date. Daily account-currency Balance records and explicit Valuation records preserve earlier history; inferred opening balances support dates before the first snapshot.

The preview lists imported counts, per-account balances, replaced counts, and migration notes. Previewing never writes. Committing requires typing RESTORE for a workspace without account/transaction/balance/budget records, or REPLACE for an existing workspace, and acknowledging native-format limitations. Existing categories and preferences are replaced in both modes. Download a backup before replacing records.

All supported records replace the current workspace in one D1 batch, using bound inserts below the 100-parameter limit. Validation rejects missing references, duplicate keys, category cycles, invalid dates or precision, and unbalanced same-currency pairs or inconsistent cross-currency snapshots. A database trigger checks the previewed workspace revision inside the same transaction. Every finance mutation increments that revision atomically. Database triggers also track unguarded writes, so edits from an older Worker version or an external database writer invalidate previews too. Concurrent changes or repeated commits invalidate the preview; one simultaneous restore can commit and the other receives 409. Old source identifiers cannot collide with another user's data.

Current native-format support:

- Every supported fiat currency exposed by the runtime's ISO currency list, including zero- and three-decimal currencies. Account balances remain separated by currency.
- Manual checking/savings, credit cards, investments, crypto, property, vehicles, loans, other assets and other liabilities.
- Transaction dates, names, notes, categories, exact amounts with inverted Sure signs, and report exclusions.
- Confirmed same- and cross-currency transfer pairs, including card/loan payment and investment-contribution distinctions. Transfers between investment/crypto accounts stay internal movements. Pending suggestions remain separate transactions.
- Category parent relationships; the UI can edit parents and rejects cycles.
- Absolute daily balances and end-of-day valuations, signed negatively for liabilities. Current snapshots do not replay historical transactions on top of an exported current balance.
- Household and personal budget copies, explicit custom periods, expected income, hierarchical allocations and rollover settings. Personal account scopes map from exported account ownership; unmapped owners receive an empty scope and a warning.
- Split grouping, child categories, merchants, tags, notes, exclusions, and exact amounts. The parent affects the account balance once; its child lines replace it in reports.
- Tags and merchants, including provider-merchant labels without activating a provider connection.
- Securities referenced by Trades/Holdings, exact fractional shares and unit prices, signed trade cash amounts, dated holding observations, and cost-basis source/lock flags. Sure export limitations for trade labels and fees are shown explicitly.
- Recurring templates, calendar rules, occurrence identities, snoozes, expected amounts, and confirmed/suggested payment allocations. Split-linked allocations and payment-only history retain their relationships.

The preview reports unsupported record types and omitted metadata. Unsupported rule features, attachments, household permissions, institution details, loan terms, and provider credentials are not activated by this migration. Recurrence matching configuration, holiday calendars, renewal/trial/cancellation metadata and automatic suggestions remain pending; price-change history is implemented in the update below and are described in the preview notes. Transaction currencies that differ from their own account, inconsistent transfer snapshots, invalid precision, or malformed references block the import instead of silently changing amounts. Confirmed cross-currency transfers between correctly denominated accounts are preserved as described below. The original export remains necessary for later parity work.

Restore files are currently limited to 8 MB, 20,000 total imported records, and a 10 MB encoded HTTP request. Large histories require a future staged import workflow; the current implementation does not claim unlimited migration or server-side ledger pagination.

Account cards now offer **Balance history** to create/edit/delete end-of-day balance records. The latest dated balance replaces that day's closing value, then only later-dated transactions change it. Property and vehicle accounts follow valuations rather than cash transaction flows, matching Sure's non-cash account behavior. Balance records never create income or expenses. Account report exclusions apply to net worth and spending; archived records remain available.

Validation added in this update: 26 pure finance/import tests; 54 core HTTP checks; 47 migration HTTP checks including concurrent/stale commits, tenant remapping, category cycles, valuations, failed-file no-op behavior, and a 1,100-row bulk restore. Schema changes and revision triggers were applied to the existing local schema. Exact integer arithmetic preserves small amounts even when large offsetting flows occur; restore validation checks dated balance and report ranges. Browser interaction and visual QA remain outstanding.

## User scope clarification

Bank and broker integrations are excluded at the user’s explicit request (November 14, 2026). Do not add bank connectors or treat missing bank sync as unfinished port work. Continue the manual-finance, migration, reporting, investment, and other applicable workflows.

## Manual ledger and recurring payments update

Tags and merchants now have editors, deletion/unlinking, transaction assignment, and filters. Split purchases support 2–100 individually categorized and tagged lines, including signed refunds. Saving rejects an unallocated remainder. Split metadata drives reports and filters while the parent remains the single account movement. Transfers cannot be split. CSV exports matching split lines rather than duplicating the purchase total.

Recurring schedules support weekly, monthly, and yearly patterns, interval repeats, last-day and ordinal-weekday rules, multiple patterns, end dates/counts, weekend handling, and fixed/average/last-paid estimates. Imported reminders start at the chosen export date while retaining stored history. Pausing preserves due history; resuming starts new reminders from today. Editing prices freezes already-due obligations. Missing exported transfer destinations pause the schedule until corrected.

Payments require an explicit action: create a transaction (or a paired transfer with dated conversion), link an existing transaction, or record a payment without another account movement. Partial payments, snoozes, skipping, missed cycles, reopening, and suggested-allocation confirmation are supported. Request IDs make retried payment submissions idempotent. Deleting a transaction unlinks its payment while preserving the payment record; deleting a payment record leaves its transaction in the ledger. Autopay is a reminder label and never initiates a bank payment. Notifications, automatic matching, price-change audit history, holiday calendars and renewal/trial lifecycle are still pending.

Validation: 46 pure tests, strict TypeScript checking, a production Worker build, 54 core API checks, 47 migration API checks, and 101 new ledger API checks. These cover calendar behavior, exact split totals and report/balance separation, metadata ownership, CSV labels, payment retries, partial and unlinked payments, history preservation, transfer pairs, native recurrence migration, and version-3 backup remapping. Additive schema migrations retain existing data. Browser interaction, visual QA, and a real hosted sign-in session remain unverified.

## Manual investments update

The Portfolio screen now supports securities, investment/crypto-account activity, holding snapshots, separate cash observations, dated prices and stock splits. Activity includes buys/sells, reinvestments, dividends, interest, fees, and security movements. Investment activity stays outside spending budgets. Its cash change and securities value contribute to net worth once. Existing total-balance observations calibrate cash by subtracting that date's holdings; cash observations explicitly record uninvested money and take priority on the same date. New accounts start with an opening cash balance before the opening date's entries. When adding existing holdings, record their actual uninvested cash too.

Share quantities use up to 18 decimal places and unit prices use up to 10. Strings and BigInt fixed-point arithmetic preserve those values; final currency values round half away from zero to minor units. Average cost includes acquisition fees. Sells reduce the pool and acquisition lots proportionally; full liquidation resets the pool. Sales subtract disposal fees from proceeds. Short positions, unclassified activities and inbound movements with unknown acquisition history do not invent a realized gain or cost basis. A known holding cost can restore the position's basis; explicitly locked zero basis is valid. Snapshot discrepancies form a reconciliation lot instead of fabricating a purchase.

A stock split takes effect before trades on its ex-date. It adjusts shares, cost per unit and carried quotes while preserving total cost. Future-dated data does not enter current balances. Quotes in another currency never substitute for a missing account-currency quote. Historical account balances, dashboard net worth and portfolio views use the same accounting engine.

CSV supports investment activity, holding snapshots and unit prices, up to 500 rows/1 MB per file. Create securities first; exchange disambiguates a ticker. Activity files use date,ticker,activity, signed qty,price, optional fee, cash_amount (or amount for cash-only activity), name and notes. Holding files use date,ticker,qty,price and optional average cost_basis. Locked/source flags are retained by the matching export. Price files use date,ticker,price. Optional account/currency columns must match the selection. Trade fingerprints preserve duplicate detection after JSON backup remapping; price/holding imports replace matching dated observations atomically. Portfolio CSV exports retain exact signed decimal quantities without spreadsheet formulas. JSON is the complete recovery format and preserves cash observations, stock splits and all relationships.

The Sure v2 exporter omits trade activity labels and explicit fees. Imported share activity stays unclassified until reviewed; fee estimates derive from the difference between its cash amount and share value. Cash-only exported activity retains its signed amount. Existing holding basis and lock flags are retained. The adapter preserves current and historical account-total snapshots without replaying trades on top of them. Additional security exchange/classification metadata and exported stock-split history remain pending. Monetary values with unsupported fractions of a currency minor unit still block import; quantities and unit prices retain their finer precision.

Account and investment currencies must currently match. Security movements do not yet carry basis automatically between two accounts. Live pricing, FX, time-weighted returns, selectable tax-lot methods, statement-file and individual trade reconciliation, and advanced portfolio reporting remain unfinished. No brokerage or bank credentials are used. Browser interaction and real hosted sign-in checks remain outstanding.

Validation: 75 pure tests, 54 core API checks, 47 migration API checks, 101 ledger API checks, and 74 portfolio API checks, plus strict typechecking and the production Worker build. This includes accounting and migration regressions, actual Worker/D1 checks, concurrent-change triggers for every new table, and restart persistence. Ordinary long histories use a conservative absolute bound to prove dated integer safety without replaying the ledger at every date; larger values fall back to exact event-boundary checks. A synthetic 1,000-trade import validation completed in 40 ms locally; this is not a hosted performance guarantee.


## Hierarchical budgets update

Budgets now have named books, explicit periods, separate spending and expected-income targets, and optional account scopes. Monthly periods can start on days 1–28; imported custom date ranges retain their boundaries. Period currency is fixed, while a book’s defaults apply to new periods only. Copying allocations requires an earlier initialized period in the same book and currency. A new period can remain uninitialized until targets are saved.

Parent allocations include funded descendants. A zero-allocation category shares its nearest funded ancestor’s reserve after separately funded descendants are reserved. Child edits preserve the parent’s direct reserve and adjust ancestors; deliberate parent underfunding remains visible as a warning. Allocation moves cannot consume reserves already assigned to descendants. Category reparenting and deletion preserve surviving direct reserves across every period.

Category spending nets signed descendant entries before clamping at zero, so refunds reduce their branch’s spending. Transfer exclusions match the manual ledger. Actual income remains gross positive receipts. Rollover carries positive direct surplus only, subtracting funded descendants’ allocations and spending to avoid duplicate carry. Changes to past activity or allocations recalculate later periods. Uninitialized periods and gaps are skipped; currency changes stop the chain. Rollover switches propagate forward. Imported incoming carry may seed the first initialized period; later periods recalculate their carry. Current-period carry is labeled projected until the period closes.

Reports show remaining funds, shared reserves, carry, unpaid scheduled obligations, daily allowance, actual versus expected income, and transaction drilldowns. Scheduled obligations retain their original period despite snoozing and deduct partial payments. Cash on hand includes checking and savings only. The Goals update also shows cash after live goal reservations, within the plan’s selected accounts. The budget FX update below now converts different-currency activity, current cash and unpaid estimates; unavailable quotes remain explicit and block affected availability/carry.

The additive database upgrade converts legacy flat budgets into inclusive hierarchy allocations while preserving each original direct reserve. Original rows remain an archive until the corresponding plan or period is removed. Older clients cannot write archived budgets or delete/reparent scoped records without the current guarded mutation path. Workspace revision checks cover the new tables. Version-5 backups include books, periods, envelopes, scopes and carry seeds; older backups upgrade during validation. Imported household/personal plans are private copies owned by the current Sites user, not imported sharing permissions.

Validation: 107 pure tests, 54 core API checks, 47 migration API checks, 101 ledger API checks, 74 portfolio API checks and 94 budget API checks; strict TypeScript checking and the production Worker build. A real D1 upgrade from the pre-budget schema verifies preserved allocations and older-client guards. Restart persistence checks cover the saved budget, ledger and portfolio state. Browser interaction, visual QA and real hosted sign-in verification remain outstanding.


## Savings goals and reserves update

The Goals screen supports named one-off savings goals and maintained reserves, optional deadlines, notes, colors, and links to checking, savings, investment or crypto accounts. A link reserves a fixed amount in the account’s currency, or its remaining balance after fixed earmarks. At most one live goal may claim that remainder. Fixed earmarks that exceed an account’s positive balance share the shortfall proportionally; integer largest remainders preserve the final minor unit without promising more money than exists. Reservations do not move account money.

Paused goals keep their earmarks. Completing a one-off goal freezes its current backing and completion date and releases its funds. Archiving releases funds; reopening or restoring rechecks conflicting remainder claims. Reopening a completed goal clears the frozen amount and resets usage for the new lifecycle while retaining history. Maintained reserves have no deadline, cannot be completed, and show a shortfall when their backing falls below target.

Recording usage shrinks the account earmark and adds the declared amount to the goal’s consumed total, so used money continues to count as progress. An optional ledger outflow can anchor the declaration once; it must have the same account/date and sufficient outgoing amount. Recording usage never creates another transaction. Deleting an anchored transaction preserves usage and clears its link. Editing an anchored outflow cannot invalidate its date, account or amount. Bare declarations remain explicit manual records and should only be entered once. Targets cannot fall below consumed amounts, and a used goal cannot change into a maintained reserve.

A reserve target can be a fixed amount or a number of months of median recorded expenses. The median follows Sure’s expense-active calendar months over available history, including the current partial month; missing months are not invented as zero observations. The goal FX update below converts dated expenses through workspace currency before deriving the goal-currency target. Save or Refresh reserve target recomputes it; missing history preserves the prior positive target. Automatic monthly refresh is pending. Deadline guidance uses the actual days remaining, avoiding a calendar-month cliff near the deadline.

Budget cash shows both checking/savings cash and cash after live goal reservations within the budget’s account scope. A goal in another currency still reserves its native account share; goal progress now converts the resulting share with the dated quote. Investment-linked goals now support contribution progress as detailed below. Automatic withdrawal suggestions, historical progress charts and projection/pace analysis remain pending. These limitations are displayed in the interface.

Version-6 Sites backups include goals, account links and usage records, and restore remaps every related account/goal/transaction identifier under the authenticated owner. The pinned Sure v2 exporter does not export goals, goal-account links or pledges. Its import preview now reports that these must be recreated from the original Sure workspace; an NDJSON file alone cannot recover omitted data. Additive D1 migrations create the new tables and revision triggers. Existing ledger records and budgets remain in place.

Verification includes 121 behavioral tests, 54 core API checks, 47 migration API checks, 101 ledger API checks, 74 portfolio API checks, 94 budget API checks, plus the goal API suite. It covers ownership isolation, proportional backing, lifecycle snapshots and reserve release, usage preservation, target derivation, concurrent remainder claims, scoped budget cash, backup remapping, revision triggers on every goal table, and restart persistence. Browser interaction, visual QA and a real hosted sign-in session remain outstanding.


## Transaction rules update

The Rules screen provides ordered, enabled/disabled rules with group labels, an optional effective date, an explicit currency scope, top-level AND conditions and one level of AND/OR groups. Matching supports description/notes text, absolute amount, income/expense/transfer type, account, exact category, merchant and independent tag membership. Text collapses whitespace; substring/prefix/suffix matches ignore case, equality preserves case, and wildcard characters are literal. Empty/not-empty and negative membership comparisons preserve missing-field semantics. The transfer type includes loan payments and investment contributions, following Sure’s transfer-kind definition.

Actions rename, set or clear category/merchant, add tags, include/exclude from reports, or split transactions. Top-to-bottom execution follows Sure’s Runner: each rule freezes its own matching set, later rules see earlier changes, the first rule claims each scalar field even when it was already set or protected, tags remain additive, and stop-processing blocks later rules. Applying one inactive rule includes enabled predecessors and stops after that rule. Groups currently organize labels; arrows determine exact execution order.

Rule splits contain fixed amounts plus percentages of the remaining amount. Percentages total exactly 100, round to the currency’s minor unit, and the last percentage row absorbs the remainder. Pure fixed configurations require a matching exact-amount condition. Transfers, existing split children, excluded purchases and purchases linked to recurring payments or goal usage are skipped. Invalid per-purchase amounts skip that split rather than create zero/negative portions. New split portions are protected from later enrichment. The purchase affects account balances once.

Historical application requires a server preview. The preview reports per-rule counts, protected/skipped actions and before/after values, showing up to 200 changes with an explicit omission count. Account/date scopes can narrow large runs. An owner-scoped digest and workspace revision bind the commit to its preview; changed options, rules or ledger records require a fresh preview. The commit is atomic. An evaluation budget limits very broad runs; narrow the account/date range when reached. Rules can change reporting metadata and splits, not account amounts, dates, balances or transfer pairs.

Enabled rules run atomically on newly imported transaction CSV rows. Rule failure rejects the complete import before any rows are written. Duplicate fingerprints use original CSV fields, so renaming through a rule does not break duplicate detection. Duplicate re-imports do not rerun rules against old rows. JSON/native workspace restore preserves imported history and does not execute rules. Automatic runs on manual entry, queued/nightly execution and investment CSV remain pending.

Field protections are stored on transaction totals and split portions. Manual creation protects explicitly supplied description/metadata; subsequent edits protect changed fields. Manual splitting protects its portion fields and protects the parent from automatic split creation. The transaction dialog offers a protection editor, including each split portion. Previews can explicitly override protections; applying one requested rule does not override its predecessors’ protections. Rule writes do not create manual locks, and backup restore preserves saved protections. Database triggers cover manual edits made through older clients too. Deleting a referenced account, category, merchant or tag disables affected rules, retains their first original configuration and clears operational references.

Version-7 Sites backups include rules, field protections and the latest 100 run records. Restore remaps references inside conditions, groups, actions and split configurations under the signed-in owner. Run history retains names/counts as snapshots; deleting a rule leaves its previous ledger changes and run history in place. The Sure v2 Rule/version-1 adapter resolves portable category/merchant/tag names and typed references, preserves run order and group labels, and retains unsupported configurations disabled for review. Its rule format omits currency: the import form offers an explicit source-rule currency for numeric conditions and fixed splits. Without that selection, those rules stay disabled. Imported numeric rules are scoped to the selected currency; no exchange parity is assumed. Source transaction attribute locks are absent from the pinned export, so they cannot be recovered from it.

Regex matching, provider/AI categorization, email actions, transfer-pairing actions, investment activity labels, automatic similar-merchant filling, richer group management and complete provider metadata remain pending. Unsupported native rules never silently become broad active rules. Their original configuration remains downloadable from the editor and in the full backup.

Verification: 142 behavioral tests and 581 HTTP checks across core (54), migration (47), ledger (101), portfolio (74), budgets (94), goals (103) and rules (108), plus strict typechecking, the production Worker build and post-restart persistence. These cover matching, first-field precedence, stops, inactive-rule execution, field protection/override, exact split rounding, scopes, portable native import, disabled unsupported actions, explicit currency selection, backup remapping, local D1 triggers, atomic CSV failure and revision guards. Browser interaction, visual QA and real hosted sign-in verification remain outstanding.


## Manual statement reconciliation update

The Reconcile screen stores typed statement titles, account currency, periods, opening/closing balances and notes. Opening means before the first day’s transactions; closing means after the last day’s transactions. Statements compare opening, closing and period movement with the existing account engine. Account-opening-day comparisons use the opening balance; later openings use the preceding day’s close. Asset valuations and investment cash/holdings retain their existing semantics. Signed debt amounts remain negative. Comparisons follow Sure’s 0.01 currency-unit tolerance, converted to whole minor units; every difference is displayed, including tolerated differences.

Transactions now carry unchecked, cleared or reconciled status. The ledger offers a cycling badge and status filter. Statement review lists purchase totals once, including report-excluded cash movements; split children do not duplicate the balance. Transfer legs are checked independently in their own account. A page can explicitly be marked cleared. Completion requires matching balances and no unchecked transactions in the period. A server preview and workspace revision bind the commit; concurrent completions have one winner. Completion records ledger snapshots and marks checked transactions reconciled without changing money. Transactions already linked to an overlapping statement keep that link.

Completed statement figures must be reopened before editing. Reopening returns the statement’s linked entries to cleared and preserves its balance adjustment. Later financial edits, backdated additions/deletions, changed opening balances and historical portfolio observations/quotes flag completed periods for review. Financial edits reset the affected transaction’s status. This includes changes hidden by an end-of-day balance anchor: a matching closing total alone does not erase the warning. Description/category/tag/report-label changes preserve transaction checks. Newer activity does not invalidate an older period, but newer unchecked transactions affect the account indicator. Database triggers also cover older clients and unguarded writes.

A closing mismatch can be resolved through an explicit previewed balance observation; it never fabricates a deposit/expense or duplicates transactions. Ordinary accounts and valuation assets save an end-of-day valuation. Investment and crypto accounts save derived uninvested cash (statement total minus recorded holdings), respecting the engine’s same-date cash-observation priority. The previous observation is retained for guarded undo, including its original name and kind. Repeated adjustments retain the first before-image. Undo refuses if the observation was edited separately, rather than overwriting newer work. Deleting a statement preserves its balance observation and returns its linked transaction flags to cleared. One statement cannot take ownership of another’s adjustment.

Version-8 Sites backups include statement figures, completion snapshots, review flags, adjustment before/after images and transaction reconciliation links. Recovery remaps every account, statement, valuation and cash-observation identifier, including references to removed historical observations. Restore deliberately suppresses change-review triggers while preserving the backed-up review state. Invalid cross-account/currency/date links, duplicate adjustment ownership and incompatible snapshots are rejected. Transaction CSV supports the optional `reconciled_status` column (unreconciled, cleared, reconciled); manual reconciled imports receive a timestamp and no statement authority. Duplicate fingerprints remain based on original transaction fields; re-importing an existing row does not update its check status. Future transactions cannot be marked cleared/reconciled through the manual/CSV workflows.

The pinned Sure exporter omits statement records/files and reconciliation flags. Its import preview states that transactions start unreconciled, while supported balance history is retained. The reconciliation milestone did not upload or parse statement files (uploads are now implemented below), extract PDF/XLSX transactions, match outstanding/pending statement lines, or verify individual investment trades/security quantities. Those remain parity work. The existing manual financial ledger treats dated entries as recorded account movements; users should review unexplained differences before recording an adjustment.

Verification includes 162 behavioral tests, 727 API checks (including 146 reconciliation checks), strict typechecking, the production build and restart persistence. Tests cover date boundaries, tolerance, signed liabilities, split/root counting, masked changes, investment cash priority, backup remapping, malformed recovery, guarded previews, atomic completion, adjustment/undo, separate edits, metadata versus financial changes, historical price/split warnings, transaction flags, CSV and ownership. Browser interaction, visual QA and hosted sign-in remain unverified.


## Private statement originals and recovery update

Files adds a private per-owner library for PDF, UTF-8 CSV and unencrypted standard XLSX originals, with 25 MB per-file and 500-record limits. Reconcile can attach new uploads to its statement; existing files can be renamed, linked/unlinked or deleted through Files. Uploads preserve originals and do not parse figures or create financial records. Filename/path/control validation, MIME/signature checks, XLSX package structure checks, bounded request reading and SHA-256 deduplication run on the server. XLSX containers reject encrypted/macro packages, unsafe paths, inconsistent local entries, ZIP64 and expansion declarations above 100 MB; uploaded packages are never decompressed or executed. No content parser or malware-scanning claim is made.

D1 holds public metadata, private immutable object references and operation state; R2 holds original bytes. An owner digest and random key identify each upload attempt. Download/delete require the signed-in owner, a matching private reference, object owner/checksum metadata and size. Download responses force attachment, disable caching and sniffing, and restrict cross-origin embedding. List/state/backup responses omit object references and attempt tokens. Concurrent duplicate uploads have one winner without deleting its object. Upload finalization accounts for D1 revision-trigger changes rather than assuming one changed row means one total database change.

Pending, available, failed, missing and deleting states make incomplete operations visible. Fresh uploads cannot be deleted for 15 minutes. Failed uploads retain keys for cleanup/retry, and failed deletion keeps a tombstone until cleanup succeeds. Missing objects become placeholders on download; re-uploading matching bytes restores the same record and any statement link. Deleting statement figures unlinks originals without deleting their bytes. Library edits and state transitions advance workspace revision and invalidate stale financial-restore previews.

Version-9 financial JSON includes file metadata, not original bytes. Existing library files survive financial restore. Same-owner hashes reconnect to remapped statements while retaining their original private references and filenames; foreign hashes never confer access and become missing placeholders. Preview reports retained/available/missing counts. It rejects inconsistent hash metadata and active pending/deleting operations. An option skips incoming file metadata when the combined library would exceed its limit.
