Statement replacement, link updates and new missing placeholders commit atomically with financial records; restoring never deletes R2 objects. Owner identities, object keys and backed-up availability statuses are never imported as authorization.

Settings also prepares a streaming ZIP containing `finance-data.json`, `manifest.json` and available originals, capped at 500 MB of original bytes. The manifest names unavailable originals explicitly. ZIP entries use UTF-8 names, CRC32 and original byte streams without loading the complete archive into Worker memory. A changed/missing object fails the download instead of silently producing a complete-looking archive. Recovery currently requires unzipping, restoring the financial JSON through the guarded preview, then re-uploading missing originals; direct ZIP import remains pending. Existing 8 MB/20,000-record JSON limits still apply. Sure's pinned v2 exporter omits statement files; those must be uploaded separately.

Verification: 180 behavioral tests and 829 HTTP checks (102 file checks plus the existing 727 finance checks), strict typechecking, production build, isolated legacy D1 upgrade and post-restart D1/R2 persistence. File tests cover signatures/types, stream limits, UTF-8, ZIP structure/CRC/cancellation, ownership isolation, concurrent duplicates, forced downloads, statement unlinking, private-key verification, metadata recovery/remapping, missing-file healing, failed/pending operations, capacity limits and stale restore previews. Browser interaction, visual QA and a real hosted sign-in session remain unverified. Statement extraction, pending-line matching, transaction receipt links and individual trade/quantity reconciliation remain parity work. Banks and brokers remain excluded.


## Pending-savings pledges and matching update

Goals now record pending savings through GoalPledge semantics: seven-day intent windows, seven-day extension, open/matched/cancelled/expired history, and no financial mutation from creating or matching a pledge. The account must be linked to the goal and active; foreign native amounts now use the saved entry-date conversion described below. Checking/savings accounts can match a recorded inflow or a positive balance increase; investment/crypto accounts require a transaction inflow. Matching checks the five-calendar-day lookback and expiry window, ignores future dates/outflows/excluded entries, and applies the original absolute 0.50-unit or relative 1% tolerance using exact integer arithmetic. FIFO creation order and ID ties choose the claimant deterministically. Paused goals can receive savings; completed/archived goals cancel open intent.

A post-save hook checks newly created or financially changed ledger entries after manual transaction entry, transfer creation, CSV import, recurring inflow payments and manual/statement balance reconciliation. It never runs during workspace restore. The capture snapshot is revision-bound before the financial write, and matching requires its expected one-revision successor. If another operation interleaves, it refuses to infer a potentially incorrect delta. Its own claim write is atomic and guarded by the resulting revision. Following upstream’s import-pipeline failure isolation, a matching error preserves the successful financial response and appends a warning; the UI can then preview recorded deposits without repeating a money entry.

Manual balance matches use the increase, not the new account total. An edit of an existing valuation uses its pre-save amount as the freshest baseline. A new dated observation compares with the prior recorded ledger at that date. Negative/unchanged totals and investment cash/holding/price changes cannot match a manual-save pledge. Separate increases to the same valuation use separate matching event IDs, while one event closes only one pledge. Transaction identity remains exclusive across all pledges. Matching never changes a fixed earmark or adds to account backing, so planned money cannot count twice as goal progress.

Historical checks offer a server preview, signed-in-owner and scope digest, workspace revision, matching-evidence binding and explicit confirmation. The digest also changes when clock expiry changes eligibility without a database write. A goal-wide check can be narrowed to one pledge from its history row; a 200,000-comparison budget bounds large queues. Preview shows the server snapshot’s goal/account names and currency, independently of stale browser state. Only actual transaction inflows are checked historically. Prior valuation edit deltas cannot reliably be reconstructed after overwrite, so historical checks do not guess them; future positive balance edits use the automatic capture path.

Expiry appears immediately from the clock in the interface and is materialized by matching/check requests, rather than a background schedule. Open pledges can be extended/cancelled before expiry. Goal closure and account unlinking cancel open pledges; database triggers cover older clients too. Match evidence keeps original date, amount, name, and balance-before/after figures. Later changes/deletion unlink the pointer while preserving the snapshot and showing a source-change warning. Goal deletion removes its pledge history without changing money. Account deletion is blocked while goal/pledge history depends on it.

Version-10 financial JSON and ZIP backups include pledge history. Restore remaps goals, accounts, source transactions/valuations and event IDs to the current owner, retains matched evidence, and never rematches history. Validation rejects malformed evidence, duplicate source claims, invalid windows, incompatible open accounts and missing references. Composite foreign keys, unique transaction/event claims, revision triggers and a 5,000-record per-owner limit reinforce persistence. The pinned Sure v2 exporter omits goals and pledges, so it cannot recover those records.

Verification: 199 behavioral tests and 981 API checks, including 152 pledge checks, plus strict typechecking, production build, isolated legacy D1 upgrade and post-restart D1/R2 persistence. New coverage includes FIFO matching, exact tolerance/date boundaries, no fictional goal progress, CSV duplicate stability, receiving transfer legs, repeat valuation edits, market exclusions, ownership, guarded previews, clock-only expiry invalidation, concurrent creation/commits, database capacity, failed matcher isolation, retained evidence and owner-safe backup remapping. Browser interaction, visual QA and real hosted sign-in verification remain outstanding. Richer pace/projections, FX, statement extraction and the other parity work above remain incomplete. Banks and brokers stay excluded.


## Contribution-based investment goals update

Goals now persist `progress_basis` as balance or contributions. New goals with an investment/crypto link select contributions automatically unless an explicit basis is submitted. A cash goal newly linked to a market account also adopts contributions when no explicit basis is supplied. Existing goals retain their basis; the additive column defaults to balance and does not rewrite historical completion figures or money. The editor offers both bases, and closed goals must be reopened before changing their interpretation.

The engine follows Sure’s `current account value − cumulative net_market_flows`, floored at zero. Its daily market-flow definition is holdings at day end minus holdings at day start minus inverse cash of nonzero-share trades. Telescoping the holdings differences gives `current cash + cumulative inverse trade cash`, calculated with BigInt and exact minor units. A dated checkpoint restarts the identity at its declared net contribution total, adding subsequent changes in cash and inverse trade cash only after that end-of-day boundary. This avoids daily replay while matching the upstream flow definition. Buy/sell fees stay in the market component; cash dividends/interest and standalone fees affect the net contribution figure. It is neither tax cost basis nor a pure external-deposit total.

The contribution-history panel is available from account Balance history and goal Funding & history. It records signed, dated checkpoints without changing balances, shares, transactions, prices, fees or cost basis. The newest eligible checkpoint supplies the starting figure; same-date saves update that observation. Earlier financial corrections do not rewrite the declared checkpoint. Later dated observations/cash movements continue to affect progress. Checkpoints retain their account; an account with checkpoint history cannot change currency/type to reinterpret amounts or move its opening date past the history. Archived accounts require restoration before adding/editing checkpoints. Deletion restores earlier contribution calculation, preserving all money records.

A deterministic share-event replay identifies holding-quantity corrections and security movements without reliable contribution values. A checkpoint covers earlier uncertainty, while a new unexplained event after it raises a new warning. Matching snapshots, split-adjusted shares, ordinary trades and cash-only income do not introduce false uncertainty. Missing history excludes the affected account from a contribution goal’s displayed subtotal, names it explicitly, and blocks completion or usage attributed to that account until resolved. Market-value goals remain usable, and complete recorded histories can calculate progress without a checkpoint. A bounded memo per immutable workspace reuses account/date calculations across goal cards.

Fixed and remainder earmarks are allocated in native account currency against the selected basis, using the existing integer pro-rata pool. The separate market-backing pool continues to cap actual reservations. Contribution progress can exceed market backing after a loss; cards and funding rows show both rather than presenting contributed principal as available cash. Goal usage follows the selected basis and completion freezes its selected amount. Reopening releases the frozen figure and resumes live calculation. Non-investment accounts retain their balance semantics; missing FX remains explicit and never assumes equal currency values.

Version-11 backups include basis and contribution checkpoint metadata. Restore remaps checkpoint/account IDs under the signed-in owner, validates dates/types/duplicates and preserves declared amounts. The Sure v2 adapter now retains complete exported account-currency daily market-flow history as cumulative contribution checkpoints (`balance − cumulative net_market_flows`). It rejects malformed/unsafe figures and warns when rows are absent, rather than interpreting missing market flows as zero. Source opening-cash inference and incomplete-history caveats still apply. Extra checkpoint rows count toward the existing 20,000-record/8 MB restore limits; staged large-history import remains pending.

Additive migration 0023 adds the checkpoint table, constant balance default, composite account ownership, unique account/date index, amount/source constraints and revision tracking. Raw checkpoint writes invalidate financial-restore previews; basis enum triggers protect unguarded edits. The API rejects foreign IDs and incompatible changes. Checkpoint writes and goal changes validate and commit atomically behind workspace revision guards. The file UI also handles an oversized HTTP 413 response with an empty body by showing its size-limit message.

Verification: 220 behavioral tests and 1,092 HTTP checks, including 111 new contribution checks, plus strict typechecking, production build, legacy upgrade and post-restart D1/R2/checkpoint persistence. Coverage includes an independent daily-flow replay, gains/losses, fees, sales and withdrawals, negative principal floors, fractional shares, multi-precision currencies, checkpoints and future boundaries, missing holdings/security-movement history, shared earmarks, actual reservation limits, goal defaults/basis selection, frozen completion, purpose spending after losses, ownership, additive preservation of existing goals, native market-flow import, malformed recovery, revision invalidation and concurrent upserts. Browser interaction, visual QA and real hosted sign-in remain unverified. Remaining parity above stays active; bank/broker integrations remain excluded.

## Dated FX and transfer update

`lib/fx.ts` uses exact BigInt ratios and currency minor-unit precision with half-away-from-zero rounding. Manual library rates are bounded positive plain decimals (12 integer and 18 fractional digits). `ExchangeRate::Provided` supplies the five-calendar-day prior-date convention; inverse lookup is an exact rational extension. The freshest eligible direct/inverse quote wins, with direct preferred on date ties. Lookup never uses a future quote, triangulates a path or substitutes 1 for a missing foreign quote.

Exchange rates supports create/edit/delete, CSV export, and previewed imports of up to 1,000 rows/1 MB with skip/replace policy. Duplicate pair/date rows within one CSV are rejected; manual saves without an ID upsert that pair/date. Preview receipts bind owner, revision, file and policy. Workspace recovery supports up to 20,000 rate observations within its overall 8 MB/20,000-record limit. Table revision triggers also invalidate previews after external D1 edits.

The combined Overview converts balance observations at today's quote, flows at their entry dates and each historical point at its capped month-end date. It respects account/entry report exclusions and existing transaction-kind semantics. Missing quotes and unsafe individual conversions are omitted only from explicitly marked known subtotals; unsafe aggregate totals are unavailable. Historical gaps are never rendered as zero or interpolated. Balance and cash-flow completeness are tracked separately. Native-currency Overview remains the default.

The manual transfer service adapts Sure's `TransferCreator`: principal moves between accounts, while optional additional source/destination fees are ordinary expense entries. Amounts and the applied exact quote are immutable together; changing/deleting the rate library does not change posted money. The complete group writes/deletes in one guarded D1 batch. Individual fee monetary edits/deletion are blocked, and split totals must preserve the fee. Goal-use snapshots and recurring payment evidence survive group deletion through the existing unlinking behavior. Retry identities bind normalized inputs, not mutable rate-library contents; conflicting reuse is rejected. JSON recovery clears retry keys while preserving recorded amounts and associations.

The Sure v2 adapter now preserves confirmed cross-currency principals, including different posting dates, and derives an exact rational quote from the actual source/destination minor-unit amounts. The pinned native exporter omits global ExchangeRate rows and transfer-fee associations. Imported fees therefore remain independent expenses; the adapter does not guess links from names/dates. The preview reports both omissions. Version-12 Sites JSON and ZIP financial exports preserve the complete new collections and remap their references on recovery. Versions 1–11 remain accepted.

The subsequent goal FX update below adds goal conversion. Budget conversion is implemented in the subsequent update below; manual recurring payments are implemented below; cross-currency securities and automatic rate/price providers remain pending. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded by the user. Validation includes exact rounding/staleness/inverse/report tests, real Worker/D1 rate/import/transfer/ownership/recovery/concurrency checks, all existing regression suites, and restart persistence. Hosted sign-in, browser interaction and visual/accessibility QA remain outstanding.

## Cross-currency goals update

`Goal::CurrencyConverter`, `Goal#current_balance`, `Goal#market_value_money`, `Goal#consume!` and `GoalPledge#matches?` supply the conversion semantics. The existing native allocation pool runs before goal-currency conversion, keeping fixed shares and largest-remainder shortfalls independent of sibling goal currencies. Current backing uses today's eligible manual direct/inverse quote (five-day lookback). Contribution progress converts separately from actual market backing; missing history stays incomplete. Budget cash reservations use native account shares directly and do not pass through goal currency. Budget-wide foreign-account analytics are implemented in the budget FX update below.

Reads expose known subtotals for missing/unsafe individual conversions and unavailable totals when converted aggregates overflow. Monthly-needed figures are unavailable while histories/conversions are incomplete. Completion refuses incomplete progress or market conversion, then freezes a goal-currency amount. Live market-reference overflow cannot overwrite a valid frozen completion amount.

Usage is entered in goal currency and converted back with the forward account-to-goal quote on the usage date. Native releases use ceiling rounding; whole-account claims settle to the remaining target using floor rounding. This is the integer-money adaptation of Sure's fractional allocation arithmetic and can leave a remainder below one native minor unit's goal-currency value. The UI shows the release before saving. Each foreign usage saves immutable native/goal amounts, currencies, date, rational quote, source and quote date. Linked transaction checks use native amounts in both application validation and the D1 trigger, including older-client writes and split-root updates. Source deletion unlinks the pointer and preserves the snapshot. Account currencies and usage dates remain protected after goal unlinking.

Foreign pledges retain goal-currency intent while matching native transactions or positive manual balance deltas at their entry dates. FIFO claims and goal-currency tolerances are unchanged. Match evidence retains native totals/delta and an exact forward-rounded conversion snapshot. Missing/out-of-range/zero-rounded conversions leave intent open and report skipped conversions; the original successful financial write remains successful. A later historical rate plus a guarded recorded-deposit check can match transactions. Overwritten historical balance deltas are deliberately not reconstructed. Renaming or updating an account without changing its native currency no longer cancels an otherwise compatible foreign pledge.

Months-based reserve targets follow the workspace/family currency boundary: convert each eligible expense to workspace currency at its transaction date, take the median across expense-active months, multiply, then convert to goal currency at today's quote. The former same-currency-only derivation is replaced. No missing FX leg is substituted with 1 and no incomplete subtotal becomes a derived target. Saving retains the supplied fallback/previous target; Refresh refuses a missing/range-limited calculation. Refresh remains explicit.

Version-13 recovery adds nullable JSON conversion snapshots to existing goal usage and pledge tables. Versions 1–12 restore with null same-currency snapshots. Cross-currency evidence requires validated currencies, amounts, rate dates and rounding; restoring remaps links under the signed-in owner. Existing D1 revision triggers cover snapshot edits. The additive migration leaves previous amounts, identifiers and revisions unchanged, replaces the usage transaction guard with native-unit comparison, and fixes the account-compatibility trigger for foreign pledges. No bank/broker connectors or provider credentials are added. Hosted browser sign-in, visual/accessibility QA, goal projections/withdrawal suggestions, household sharing, receipt extraction and assistant workflows remain outstanding.

## Cross-currency budget reporting update

Budget transaction conversion follows `IncomeStatement::ScopedTransactionsQuery`'s entry-date rate boundary. Current liquidity and native goal reservations adapt `Budget#available_cash`, `Budget#earmarked_for_goals` and `Goal#backing_within`. Foreign quotes use the port's exact direct/inverse lookup with the five-day prior-date rule. A missing quote is never parity. Loan/investment kinds normalize to outgoing budget spending, matching Sure's explicit expense classification regardless of raw signed entry direction.

`lib/budget-fx.ts` retains each reporting entry's raw native amount, normalized budget direction and applied quote. Eligible split siblings share a single rounded conversion of their signed total. Mathematical floors plus largest remainders preserve that total with mixed-sign refunds; original split position breaks ties so restored IDs cannot change which category receives the last minor unit. Scope/exclusion/kind filters apply before the eligible split group is converted. Native ledger entries and allocation records remain unchanged.

The report tracks category spending, incoming carry, displayed descendant carry and availability completeness separately. Missing actuals are shown only as known subtotals. Affected shared pools and category daily limits are unavailable. An unknown category cannot emit carry into later initialized periods; sibling envelopes with complete evidence retain their own carry. Turning rollover off and period-currency breaks stop that uncertain path with known zero. Resolving/deleting dated quotes recomputes the complete chain. Aggregate FX overflow produces an unavailable calculation, never a rounded/clamped budget figure. Native-only strict workspace validation preserves existing structural plan/amount checks independently of report conversion availability.

Cash conversion uses the requested as-of date for active checking/savings accounts inside the plan scope. Each account's native shared-pool reservation total converts directly to budget currency once, preserving full earmarking after rounding. Investment cash, archived/excluded accounts and out-of-scope accounts do not inflate liquidity. Missing positive or negative foreign balances remain outside an explicitly incomplete known subtotal; raw account balances and reservations remain visible in a cash breakdown. Per-account or aggregate range errors are explicit.

Unpaid bills convert their remaining native occurrence amount at the as-of quote as a forecast. Confirmed partial payments reduce it; snoozing retains the original period; missing quotes create known reservation subtotals and per-category missing counts. The financial recurring record, payment history and currency remain unchanged. The interface exposes requested/quote dates, direction, carry age and native/converted drilldown amounts. No future quote is invented for an upcoming bill.

No new database fields, table or backup version are required: existing version-13 recovery retains all inputs and recomputes reports. Native Sure imports lack the global rate library. Until the required quote is entered, the corresponding imported expense/carry stays incomplete instead of producing excess carry. Manual recurring payment conversion is implemented below. Currency-changing allocation copy, automatic FX feeds, household permissions, receipt extraction and assistant features remain pending. Bank/broker integrations remain excluded. Hosted sign-in, browser interaction, visual/accessibility QA and large-history performance remain unverified.

## Cross-currency recurring payment update

The source `RecurringTransaction::Allocator#resolve_amounts` and native source-capacity guard define the bill/entry currency boundary. Schedules and occurrences retain bill currency while assigned and destination accounts may differ. New transaction creation converts on the payment date; linking converts on the entry date even when `paid_on` differs. Amounts remain exact integer minor units. Reference-rate source consumption rounds upward so it never understates capacity used; an explicit actual native amount stores the exact implied posted ratio. Record-only payments remain bill-currency declarations with no source transaction or conversion. Accountless series can select a one-payment source account.

New allocations retain JSON conversion snapshots with source account/date, both amounts/currencies, exact ratio, quote date/source and rounding. Saved/inverse quotes follow the five-day lookback; actual/imported pairs use a rational implied quote. Retry fingerprints bind the original normalized request, including mode, selected source/split, native override, dates and bill amount. Successful retries return existing history before mutable quote resolution. New financial entries, occurrence settlement, conversion/transfer details and the allocation commit in one guarded D1 batch. A post-save savings-pledge check uses the existing separate guarded hook and cannot undo or duplicate a successful payment.

Source-capacity checks use native amounts across confirmed active-schedule claims. Root/split claims cannot mix, and split selection checks its signed direction. Ended schedules stop reserving an entry's capacity while preserving history. Suggestions do not consume capacity until confirmation; confirmation retains an exported measured native amount or resolves an unknown amount from a dated quote/explicit actual amount. A legacy unmeasurable confirmed foreign claim remains readable but cannot share its source with another claim. Confirmation is idempotent. Full recovery checks native capacities and conversion/account/date consistency.

Recurring transfers can use separate bill/source/destination currencies. Source-to-bill conversion determines the account principal; source-to-destination conversion produces the receiving principal and immutable transfer details. These two entries and the bill payment settle atomically. No receiving rate is guessed and no fees are created by this path; transfers needing actual receiving quotes or fees can be recorded through the existing Transfer form and linked by their outgoing entry.

Deleting a transaction or removing/replacing a linked split line unlinks the source pointer and preserves payment history. It does not silently rebind a refunded or changed split to a different root capacity. Account-currency/opening-date/delete guards and transaction/split monetary guards preserve saved source evidence, including older-client D1 writes. Deleting a payment allocation retains the actual ledger entries. Existing schedule history prevents changing its bill currency.

The additive migration adds nullable `conversion` and `request_fingerprint` fields to recurring allocations and the relevant evidence guards. Prior values, IDs, occurrence records and workspace revisions are retained. Version-14 JSON/ZIP exports preserve snapshots; restores accept versions 1–13, remap native account/transaction/split references and reset request fingerprints. Native Sure export imports retain `source_amount`/`source_currency` but report absent applied quotes. No provider credentials or bank/broker connectors are added. Automatic matching setup, price-change audit flows, household permissions, securities FX, receipt extraction, assistant features and hosted/browser QA remain outstanding.


## Transaction receipts update

Sure's `Transaction` attachments and `TransactionAttachmentsController` allow up to 10 receipts per transaction, 10 MB per attachment, and JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP or PDF types. The Sites adaptation reuses its private file library with an owner-scoped many-to-many `transaction_receipts` junction. One original can serve multiple transactions or a compatible statement. Receipt links belong to the root transaction, including its split lines; individual transfer legs can carry receipts. No bank or broker connections are added.

The additive migration creates the receipt table and a composite owner/file index. Composite foreign keys prevent cross-owner references and remove links when a transaction or original is deleted. Original files remain when a financial transaction or receipt link is removed. Database triggers enforce 10 links per transaction, 5,000 per workspace and eligible file type/size. New uploads reserve pending metadata and their receipt link in one revision-guarded batch before R2 storage, preventing concurrent uploads from exceeding capacity or leaving an eleventh orphan. Failed uploads retain retry/cleanup information. Same-owner SHA-256 duplicates attach the existing original; ordinary library duplicate uploads retain their previous conflict behavior.

Files accepts images up to 10 MB; existing PDF/CSV/XLSX statement limits remain 25 MB. Image uploads check MIME/extension agreement, container signatures/bounds and dimensions without server-side decoding, including PNG CRC checks. Image dimensions are limited to 16,384 per side and 40 megapixels. Authentication, owner-scoped storage references and integrity metadata protect downloads and image previews; private no-store, nosniff, same-origin and sandbox response headers apply. PDFs remain downloads. Images cannot be linked as statement originals. Uploads do not perform OCR or extraction.

Transaction details offer contextual receipt uploads, an existing-original picker, private 150px image previews, downloads, separate unlinking and explicit deletion of the shared original. The full library shows receipt link counts. Missing originals can be re-uploaded through Files even when a transaction's ten slots are full. Pending/deleting originals cannot change links during their operation.

Version-15 JSON and ZIP financial backups include receipt links. Versions 1–14 default to an empty receipt collection. Restore remaps transaction and file IDs, then resolves same-owner checksums back to retained originals before validating and inserting receipt links. Other sign-ins receive missing placeholders without access to any original bytes; re-uploading matching bytes heals all links. ZIPs include each original once. Excluding incoming file metadata also excludes its receipt links. The pinned Sure exporter puts attachment metadata in a separate `attachments.json` manifest without binary payloads; current `all.ndjson` migration does not import that manifest. Keep those originals and attach them manually after financial import.

Validation includes 325 behavioral tests and 119 receipt API requests covering every allowed type, invalid/truncated inputs, private headers/previews, ownership, deduplication, unlinking/deletion, max-capacity/concurrent slots, database guards, failed/missing recovery, JSON/ZIP restore and original byte equality. Full regression and restart persistence results are recorded in the workspace README after publication. Household permissions, receipt extraction, automatic feeds, securities FX, assistant features and hosted/browser QA remain outstanding.


## Dated foreign investment entry update

The upstream boundary comes from `Trade::CreateForm`, `Holding::TradeCalculatorHelpers#converted_trade_price`, `#effective_trade_price` and `Trade#calculate_realized_gain_loss`: quoted security prices/fees can differ from the account currency, acquisition basis uses trade-day FX and disposal proceeds must be expressed in basis currency. The port now supports this boundary for manual/CSV trades and holding observations. Dynamic foreign market quote valuation is a subsequent part of the full securities port; current portfolio market observations continue to be native account-currency prices.

An additive migration adds nullable JSON `conversion` columns to trades and holding snapshots. Existing rows, IDs, figures and workspace revisions remain unchanged. Snapshots retain original quoted price, fee/cash or holding value/basis, source/target currencies, quantity/date and exact rate numerator/denominator with quote date/source. Manual quotes use the existing positive 18-place rate contract. Saved/inverse quotes use the existing five-day lookup. Source/native figures, signs, dates, precision and supported integer ranges are verified during save and recovery. No unknown FX leg is replaced by parity.

Foreign trade cash is converted once from its signed source net cash amount. The native fee is independently retained for display; it is not subtracted from native cash a second time. Acquisition cost uses that converted net cash, including the fee. This avoids double rounding in zero-decimal currencies and lets exact foreign posted cash differ from rounded native per-share price times quantity plus the separately rounded native fee. Disposal proceeds use converted signed cash while average basis stays in native account units. Cash-only dividends/interest/fees and share-only movements retain their previous behavior. Converted holding snapshots retain their declared native total and converted total basis, including when a tiny per-share basis rounds to zero; pure stock splits preserve the observed total.

Original financial fields and applied quotes survive rate edits/deletion. Descriptive-only edits reuse the saved quote. Financial edits resolve the dated/explicit quote again. New foreign trade request fingerprints bind normalized source inputs and explicit rate; retries return the existing entry before mutable rate/account-archive resolution. A changed retry conflicts; editing an existing activity invalidates its create-request fingerprint. The form retains both recording order and request ID across a retry. Native APIs refuse accidental overwrite of converted entries. Database guards reject older clients updating/deleting converted records unless using a current guarded mutation epoch, and protect account currency/type/opening boundaries. Current investment commands use epoch 4; complete financial recovery uses epoch 5. A trigger-only migration limits statement-review suppression to restore epochs 3 and 5, so ordinary modern investment edits still mark completed statements for review.

CSV supports `quote_currency` and optional `exchange_rate` while `currency` remains the account's native currency. Native exports append exact conversion JSON plus activity import keys. Export/import retains rational inverse quotes and original values without resolving current rates; import keys continue to prevent duplicates after ownership/security remapping. Foreign source CSV duplicates skip before mutable quote resolution. Conflicting exported keys fail rather than silently changing an existing activity. Holding CSVs include their observed amount for exact recovery. All row validation and native range checks precede the guarded batch, including atomic optional native cash observations.

Version-16 JSON/ZIP financial recovery preserves investment snapshots; versions 1–15 default to null. Recovery validates source-to-native consistency, remaps accounts/securities and resets new-trade retry fingerprints. Original source fields contain no owner-scoped storage references. The pinned Sure v2 exporter does not include `Trade#exchange_rate`/extra, the global FX library or activity labels; foreign account-mismatched NDJSON records therefore require reviewed manual/CSV conversion instead of guessing a quote. Applying the new migration to a seeded prior SQLite schema verifies row/revision preservation, old-client protection and current guarded edits.

Validation includes 346 behavioral tests and 127 investment FX API checks for native and three-decimal currency precision, fee rounding, historical basis/proceeds, stale/future/inverse/manual rates, immutable evidence, retries/concurrency, owner isolation, account guards, atomic CSV, native repricing, holding recovery and cross-owner backup remapping. The broader finance regressions, final build and restart results are recorded in the workspace README after publication. Bank/broker integrations remain excluded. Dynamic foreign quote valuation, native foreign investment import, extraction, household permissions, assistant features and hosted/browser QA remain outstanding.


## Sure native foreign investment recovery update

The pinned v2 `Family::DataExporter` emits original trade quantities, prices and signed entry amounts, plus holding quantities, rounded prices, independent reported amounts and optional average basis. It does not export `Trade#exchange_rate`, activity labels or the global rate library. Native foreign entries previously failed account-currency validation; the migration now supports an explicitly selected current-owner rate context. The default remains off. Nothing is fetched from a bank/broker/provider.

`useInvestmentRates` keeps the signed-in workspace's complete current rate library and passes it to the native adapter. Owner IDs are removed, rate IDs remap during replacement, and applied investment snapshots contain no rate-row/owner references. The request cannot inject a different rate context. Source trade cash and inferred fees use their original currency, then the existing exact native conversion path. Foreign holdings convert their exported reported value and supplied per-share basis; their source total is retained even when it differs from rounded quantity × price. A discrepancy note is attached to the source line. Original execution-rate absence and unclassified activities remain explicit migration warnings.

Each conversion review records its source ID/line, account/security, date, original/native amount and unit price, inferred source/native trade fee or supplied average basis, and the complete rate snapshot. Reviews page through 50 records with a bounded offset. Paging changes only the projection; the receipt binds source bytes, export date, selected conversion/rule/file options and current workspace revision. A rate/context/financial change invalidates approval. A changed receipt clears UI acknowledgment/confirmation. Preview failures clear obsolete details. Reports identify the page being downloaded. Missing/future/stale quotes fail before any financial replacement.

Account-currency daily/current anchors remain authoritative after conversion; original cash changes are not replayed over them. Source share quantities retain 18-place precision, fees/native values use integer minor units and holding basis uses the existing exact converted total. Share trades stay unclassified until review; a library quote is not represented as Sure's original execution rate. Reported holding totals are validated for sign/range and conversion consistency rather than forced to equal rounded unit price times quantity. Manual creation still requires that equation; edits of an unchanged imported quote retain the original total, and stale calculated rewrites fail. Exact exported holding CSV/JSON recovery accepts the reported original total without resolving current rates.

This update requires no D1 schema change. Financial JSON/ZIP version 17 documents the observed-source-value semantics; versions 1–16 remain accepted. The Sure-only option is rejected for Sites recovery, which retains its own snapshots. Validation includes 360 behavioral tests and 63 native import API checks covering explicit consent, missing quotes, retained unused rates, inverse/JPY conversion, source attribution, paging, stale receipts, owner isolation, observed totals, anchors, rate deletion, stale-client protection and CSV/JSON recovery. Broader regression/build/persistence results are recorded in the workspace README after publication. Dynamic foreign price valuation, household/assistant/extraction workflows and hosted/browser QA remain outstanding. Bank/broker integrations remain excluded.


## Dated foreign market valuation update

Upstream `Holding::PortfolioCache#get_price` converts a selected price at its recorded date, and `ForwardCalculator#build_holdings` produces account-currency holding values for that date. The Sites adaptation adds an explicit `profiles.market_fx` setting, defaulting to zero, so publication does not silently revalue existing data. An activation preview/commit is bound to owner, revision, pricing choice and report date. It checks all relevant historical/future price points before committing and pages current/proposed account cash/holdings/totals in groups of 25. View offsets do not change the approval receipt. Missing and range issues are bounded to the first 100, with a remaining count.

`market-fx.ts` sweeps trade/snapshot/split quantities per account/security and examines same-date independent price choices. Native currency wins ties; otherwise currency code determines foreign selection independently of storage order or restored UUIDs. Empty/closed positions and pre-position quotes do not need irrelevant FX. Nonzero selected foreign prices require the existing positive dated direct/inverse lookup with five-day lookback. Zero prices are known zero with null FX ratio metadata, never an invented parity rate. Enabled workspace validation rejects missing/stale/future-required quotes and unsafe converted values. Consequently deleting/editing a required rate or adding an unconvertible used price cannot leave an enabled workspace partially valued.

Portfolio valuation uses quantity × source-price × exact FX ratio, including later split factors, and rounds once at the account's minor-unit precision. Converted display unit prices may round to zero while the complete position value remains meaningful. The conservative portfolio range bound includes effective foreign unit-price ceilings and retains exact dated fallback checks. Quotes convert on their own point date; later FX observations do not leak backwards. A later native trade/holding observation can supersede a prior foreign point. Original trade execution snapshots, native cash, lots, basis and disposal amounts remain independent of market repricing. The position's derived conversion detail retains source price ID/currency/value/date and applied ratio/source/date.

Actual native cash anchors remain authoritative. Total-balance anchors subtract holdings at their own date to infer cash; changing market pricing can therefore change inferred cash while preserving an anchored total. Activation preview exposes both figures. Configuration changes identify affected accounts/first price dates; effective FX changes compare full rational converted prices. Completed affected statements are marked for review atomically. Unused or mathematically equivalent rate changes do not add review flags. Configuration/price/rate actions do not enter the pending-savings matching hook.

Price management now lists all quote currencies. CSV import accepts per-row quote currency and detects duplicates by security/date/currency; exports can preserve mixed currencies. Native trade/holding CSV account-currency boundaries remain intact.

The additive migration adds `market_fx` with default 0 and guards input changes while enabled. New investment/rate mutations and configuration use epoch 6; complete financial recovery uses epoch 7. Older writers cannot alter relevant prices/rates/trades/holdings/splits or flip pricing mode while enabled. Statement-review suppression covers only complete-restore epochs 3, 5 and 7; ordinary epoch-6 edits continue to flag statements. All preexisting table columns, records and revisions are verified unchanged after upgrade. Version-18 financial JSON/ZIP preserves the setting and raw inputs; older backups default to native-only pricing when the setting is absent. Sure migration retains the current workspace setting while importing its supported financial history; the source exporter does not supply an independent price library.

Validation includes 381 behavioral tests, a seeded schema upgrade proof and the full local Worker API regression/persistence results recorded in the workspace README after publication. The new cases cover direct/inverse/stale quotes, date isolation, zero/fractional/short holdings, native tie precedence, splits, native total/cash anchors, saved basis, statement review, strict mutation guards, mixed CSV, pagination and cross-owner recovery. Automatic price/FX feeds, remaining upstream workflows and hosted/browser QA remain outstanding. Bank/broker integrations remain excluded.


## Keyboard navigation audit

A source audit found the mobile sidebar was translated off-screen while its controls remained in the keyboard tab order. Mobile navigation now uses a native modal dialog: closed contents are hidden, the browser contains focus while it is open and restores focus on close, Escape and backdrop clicks dismiss it, and resizing to desktop closes it. Background scrolling is locked only while the drawer is open. Opening an account form first closes the drawer. Desktop navigation remains a normal sidebar. A skip-to-content link, named navigation landmark, current-page indication and expanded-state menu trigger were added.

TypeScript and the production Worker build verify this change. Hosted sign-in, keyboard interaction, focus restoration, responsive appearance and assistive-technology behavior still require browser QA: the computer-use tool returned “The browser access host is unavailable.” No production finance records were created or changed for this audit. Bank and broker connections remain excluded.


## Loan contract and amortization update

`accounts.loan_terms` is nullable JSON. Old rows default to null; terms are independent of signed ledger balances. The dedicated revision-bound command saves/removes terms on an active loan account. Existing account forms leave them intact; type/currency changes require deliberate removal first. Guards reject term edits, non-null insertions and deletion of accounts with terms from write generations below 8. Current financial restore uses generation 9; statement review/reset triggers suppress restored snapshots for generations 3, 5, 7 and 9, while ordinary account changes still request statement review. A term-only edit increments the workspace revision without flagging a ledger statement, since the schedule is separate.

The exact rational schedule adapts upstream `Loan::AmortizationMath`, `Loan::Simulator`, `Loan::AmortizationSchedule`, `Loan::RateResolver` and `Loan::Insurance`. It retains original principal/date, subtype, term, base annual percentage, variable rate vocabulary and dated changes, day-count convention, down payment, insurance and the source principal-only flag. Three-place interest and four-place insurance percentages are represented as decimal strings. Payment sizing uses the nominal monthly annuity, re-sized only when the effective numeric rate changes; straddling periods carry the actual opening-rate interest into sizing. Interest is rounded once per monthly period. Zero interest, short/long months, leap and cross-year actual/actual fractions, early rounding payoff, zero rounded installments and final residue are explicit. Monthly insurance is outside principal/interest. Values outside the supported money/date ranges fail without changing records.

The account editor opens terms with a complete saved schedule when available, supports partial metadata, previews candidate terms, pages 24 rows and exports all rows as CSV. Changing input invalidates the old preview. Native v2 export recovery reads the accountable loan fields, rounds four-place source money to account precision, preserves original contractual principal independently of current balance snapshots, and warns when first tracked principal/date must be used as fallback. Malformed fields reject the import. Financial format 19 preserves terms across JSON/ZIP backups; formats 1–18 remain accepted. Owner/account IDs remap on restore. The import preview includes all incoming loan terms and rate changes in its downloadable review, with an on-screen review paged ten loans at a time.

Validation includes the behavioral suite, seeded additive-upgrade proof, real Worker/D1 API checks, restart persistence and an optional original-source comparison. The latter loads the pinned Ruby simulator directly and compares full schedule rows; precise results are recorded in the workspace README after publication. Automatic ledger principal/interest replay remains parity work. Current-balance payoff projections and extra repayment scenarios are implemented in the update below. The source principal-only flag does not enable replay. No bank/broker integration is added. Hosted sign-in, browser interaction and visual/accessibility QA remain unverified.

Loan milestone results: 402 behavioral tests, 75 new loan HTTP/API checks and 2,246 checks across the full API regression set passed. The original Ruby comparison matched 8,974 rows across 33 cases. TypeScript, production build, seeded additive-schema upgrade and isolated legacy budget upgrade passed. Local Wrangler returned intermittent “worker restarted mid-request” proxy responses during early rejected requests; affected suites were rerun with fresh synthetic owners and passed without relaxing assertions. No production financial data was used for testing. Restart proof is recorded in the workspace README after publication.


## Recorded-balance payoff and extra repayment update

`loan-projection.ts` adapts upstream `Loan::PayoffProjection` without changing stored balances. It starts at the account’s signed recorded debt on a selected historical/current date, holds the contract’s remaining payment rows and original maturity, and uses the contractual period opening for the first interest charge. A mid-period rate change therefore cannot re-rate interest already accruing under the prior opening rate. All three saved day counts retain their own basis. It never re-amortizes a lower balance back onto the original term and never settles an underfunded final installment artificially.

Monthly extras ride each installment. Weekly/fortnightly extras begin the day after as-of and count inclusively through each due date. They are combined within the monthly accrual model, not daily interest. Applied extras are capped at the required settlement; insurance and fees stay outside projection totals. Results distinguish missing terms, settled debt, a contract with no remaining dates, a contracted payment rounded to zero, converged payoff and a maturity balloon. Interest/total-cost/months-sooner comparisons between base and extra scenarios exist only when both clear the balance. Negative principal portions and growing debt remain visible if the contracted installment does not cover the period’s interest. Unsafe money/date inputs fail without writes.

`GET /api/loan-projection` reads one owner-scoped consistent workspace snapshot, validates account/date/amount/frequency/page parameters, and returns bounded 24-row pages with full summary totals. Unknown or repeated options are rejected. Dates stay within recorded account history through today; the projection cannot start before origination. Optional revision binding prevents paging/downloading different inputs after a financial change. The private uncached CSV contains all rows of both scenarios, native currency, contract/extra breakdown, convergence and remaining maturity debt. Missing-payment reports cannot be exported as misleading empty CSVs. There is no write endpoint and no schema change.

The loan editor uses saved terms and requires saving edited contract fields before projection. Candidate input changes clear previous results; aborted/late requests cannot overwrite a newer result. Demo calculations use only in-memory sample data, while signed-in reports use the private endpoint. CSV download preserves its UTF-8 marker. Complete scenarios are exported; the view switches between contracted and extra repayments and pages their rows. Financial backup format remains 19; recovered ledger/contract inputs reproduce the same models under remapped owner/account IDs.

Validation: 426 behavioral tests and 96 new real Worker API checks cover ownership, no-write behavior, dated balances, revisions, all status boundaries, calendar extras, native precision, 1,200-period paging, complete CSV bytes and cross-owner recovery. The original Ruby payoff class matched 9,036 rows and complete summary totals across 324 cases; the prior contractual simulator still matched 8,974 rows across 33 cases. Core (54), loan-editing/recovery (75) and migration (47) regressions, strict TypeScript and the production build are verified separately. Restart evidence is recorded in the workspace README after publication. Hosted browser/sign-in/visual QA and automatic loan ledger replay remain unfinished. Banks/brokers stay excluded.


## Recurring price observation and recovery update

The additive `recurring_price_changes` table stores immutable previous/new native minor amounts, their own currency, an effective date, detected/user source, series reference and optional source transaction. A series/effective-date unique key follows upstream. Monetary snapshots survive source deletion: a transaction-delete trigger clears only the link before its foreign key is checked. History cascades with explicit schedule deletion. Insert/delete guards require write generation 10; current full financial restore uses generation 11. Older restores and schedule deletions cannot silently drop new history. Statement review/reset trigger suppression now includes restore generations 3, 5, 7, 9 and 11; ordinary generation-10 ledger changes still request review. Row revision triggers invalidate stale approvals. Capacity is enforced at 5,000 records per owner.

The detector adapts `RecurringTransaction::PriceChangeDetector`: active outgoing schedules, the latest two paid occurrences by due date, exactly one confirmed settlement each, amounts within 0.01 major units, and a difference from the declared price larger than that epsilon. The later occurrence supplies the new amount/effective date/source pointer. Suggested payments and multiple partials do not count. Recorded future paid-on values cannot influence an earlier as-of review; an already paid advance occurrence can reveal a known future effective price. Snooze labels do not rewrite observation dates. Existing effective dates and an identical latest known price suppress duplicates. Currency-specific integer thresholds and exact ten-percent materiality avoid floating-point decisions. An older history row in a different currency cannot mask the current native observation.

Review/commit binds owner, workspace revision, series scope and as-of date; paging through 50 candidates does not change its receipt. The metadata-only commit leaves schedule amounts, obligations and ledger/budget values unchanged. No-op reviews do not increment the revision. All ported schedules currently retain declared prices; the detector does not auto-reprice a potentially manually declared or imported series. Manual price records and deletions also require the current revision. Historical currencies are retained independently of a schedule’s current currency, with no invented conversion.

The new collection defaults empty for older finance files. Native v2 price records are supported, including split-source transaction remapping, rather than warning that the record type was omitted. Financial format 20 exports the history in JSON/ZIP; formats 1–19 remain accepted. Restore validates monetary ranges, source types, references and duplicate dates, then remaps owners, series and linked roots. The import count and replaced count make this metadata visible before replacement. The interface supports scope selection, both evidence rows, price history paging, manual records, explicit deletion and navigation back to the schedule editor. Demo mode never fetches a real owner’s review.

The original Ruby detector comparison covers 1,080 cases across whole/two/three-decimal currencies, epsilon boundaries, active state, single/multiple settlements and duplicate history. The seeded upgrade proof preserves every prior row/revision and verifies immutable prices, generation guards, safe source unlinking and ordinary/restore statement review behavior. New behavioral and Worker API results, broader finance regressions and clean-restart persistence are recorded in the workspace README after publication. Background pipelines, matching, lifecycle metadata and the other outstanding workflows remain incomplete. Banks and brokers remain excluded; hosted browser/sign-in/visual QA remain unverified.

Price-history milestone results: 447 behavioral tests, 93 new price-history HTTP/API checks and all 2,342 prior API regression checks passed (2,435 API checks total), plus strict TypeScript, the production Worker build, original-detector parity and isolated legacy budget upgrade. The seeded additive upgrade verifies unchanged old rows/revisions and guarded immutable history. The FX negative-case fixture was made deterministic: it now corrupts an explicitly cross-currency/request-bearing transfer rather than relying on random UUID ordering. Financial validation was unchanged. Clean-restart D1/R2 proof and publication provenance are recorded in the workspace README after deployment.

## Recurring transaction matching and rejected-match recovery update

The port now adapts `RecurringTransaction::Matcher` and the relevant `Allocator` behavior through an explicit, opt-in review. Each schedule stores nullable matching settings: early/late days, base amount tolerance, original detected name, aliases, learned tolerance and preserved source hints. Existing schedules remain unchanged and matching is off by default. Native imports retain settings and opaque hints with matching disabled until reviewed. A dedicated revision-bound settings command avoids losing them during ordinary schedule edits; renaming preserves the original name.

The matcher uses strict complete-name/alias or merchant identity, account scope, direction, native currency and transfer destination. It scores identity, amount, date and account as integer basis points, with exact currency-aware epsilon and rational amount-band arithmetic. Windows respect snoozes, cadence half-cycles and overdue extension. Sources are posted through the selected as-of date; split purchases contribute eligible children instead of their hidden root. Nearest open occurrences avoid same-series backlog ambiguity, while competing enabled schedules remain in cross-series ambiguity checks even under a single-series write scope. Exact unambiguous matches become confirmed allocations; high/ambiguous results become suggestions, except income and exact-only backfill reviews. No matching action creates ledger transactions or changes balances.

Each run assigns a source and occurrence at most once. Confirmed sources are excluded globally. Suggested and confirmed allocations both reserve native source capacity; ended schedules release it. Root/split claims cannot mix and unmeasurable foreign history cannot share a source. SQL guards enforce those limits and prevent overclaiming on reactivation, independently of application validation. Confirmation excludes its own reservation from the capacity check. Foreign manual payments retain the existing applied-quote workflow, while this matcher only selects sources in the bill currency. Slight underpayments settle within the enabled schedule’s base tolerance; the frozen estimate is retained and displayed separately from the recorded total. Learned tolerance widens matching rather than settlement. Manual attachments learn names from partial payments and widen tolerance only for a single close-worthy payment within 25% deviation; manual tracking without enabled matching retains strict settlement.

Reviews show applied/source amounts, frozen settlement estimate, score components, dates, ambiguity and tier, paged 50 at a time. Preview is read-only. Commit binds owner, workspace revision and complete options and applies all pages atomically; stale/concurrent saves fail. No-op commits leave revision unchanged. More than 300 proposed writes, 20,000 open/candidate rows or one million comparisons fail explicitly with a narrowing instruction rather than truncating or partially saving. The full resulting workspace is validated before commit.

Rejections are immutable series/root/optional-split pairs. Dismissing a saved linked suggestion or rejecting a proposed match records sticky memory across all occurrences of that schedule. Users can explicitly clear a rejection; the rejection list and saved suggestions are paged. Confirmed payment removal remains an unlink action. Transaction/split/schedule deletion legitimately cascades matching-pair memory. Imported rejections resolve split children to their parent root and own part; orphan source records are reported rather than guessed. Allocation confidence and bounded JSON score evidence survive confirmation, JSON/ZIP backup and native v2 recovery.

Migration 33 is additive: nullable settings/evidence columns and the `recurring_match_rejections` table. It preserves previous rows and revisions. Modern ordinary writes use generation 12; complete restore uses 13. Old destructive restores are rejected before they can erase any new settings, score evidence or rejection-only metadata. Older editors cannot discard matching configuration or score fields. Manual field-protection triggers recognize ordinary generation 12, while complete restore preserves original protections and statement-review flags. Financial format 21 preserves and remaps all matcher references; formats 1–20 remain accepted with empty rejection memory where absent. Settings and evidence enforce bounded finite JSON and explicit size/depth limits.

Validation includes 25 new behavioral cases, 4,032 original Ruby `Matcher#explain` comparisons (1,368 eligible results), 239 new real Worker/D1 API checks and a seeded schema upgrade proof. A previous recurring-FX test now verifies that unknown suggestions cannot share a measured source before isolating the unknown claim for manual confirmation, reflecting reserved suggestion capacity. Broad regression exposed a manual-rule-lock generation regression; migration 33 now preserves manual locks for generation 12 and a seeded test covers both manual writes and generation-13 recovery. Final full-suite/restart/publication results are recorded in the workspace README. Automatic background recurring pipelines, repost/orphan repair, renewal/trial/cancellation lifecycle, loan ledger replay and remaining household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain unfinished. No bank or broker integration is included. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified because browser access is unavailable.

Matcher milestone results: 472 behavioral tests and all 2,675 API checks passed, including 239 matcher checks and the 198 recurring-FX checks reflecting reserved suggestions. TypeScript, the production build, original Ruby score/window comparison, seeded additive upgrade and isolated legacy budget upgrade passed. One local Wrangler migration-suite attempt returned its known “worker restarted mid-request” proxy response; a fresh-owner rerun passed without changing status assertions. Clean-restart D1/R2 verification includes matcher settings, evidence, rejection memory and the same future review plan, alongside all previous report/original-byte snapshots. Publication provenance is recorded in the workspace README.

## Recurring dates, status, notices and commitments update

The pinned Sure model stores `renews_on`, `trial_ends_on` and `cancelled_on` independently. A cancellation date deliberately does not end a schedule: the original bill drawer warns when a cancelled record remains active and offers Pause. The port retains that behavior with dedicated revision-bound date edits and pause/resume/end actions. The shared cadence-change path preserves paid/closed/payment-bearing rows, freezes already-due obligations, removes only regeneratable future rows and resumes new reminders from today. Ending retains payment, occurrence, price and rejection history and releases source capacity; resuming validates all existing source claims before saving. None of these actions initiates a payment or cancels a service with its provider.

The recurring screen displays trial, renewal and cancellation labels alongside installment progress (paid occurrence count / declared count). It exposes an active-cancellation warning and direct pause action. Lifecycle controls edit saved schedules independently from the amount/cadence form, and ordinary/older-shaped schedule edits preserve all saved dates. Blank dates explicitly clear metadata. Invalid real calendar dates, timestamps and unsupported ranges are rejected rather than silently clearing an imported value. Stale or concurrent metadata updates cannot overwrite another edit. No-op dates/status changes leave the revision unchanged.

Notices adapt the original `BillsController#collect_notices` and `Notice`: active outgoing payable schedules, including debt-account transfer payments, trials/renewals today through 14 days, urgent trials through day three, price records since 30 days ago and known future effective prices. Material absolute price shifts of at least ten percent are urgent; signed percentage labels round halves away from zero and zero prior prices have no relative shift. Urgent items sort first, then distance from the selected date, then original collection kind and stable identity. Income, savings/investment transfers, suggestions and parked/ended schedules do not appear in payable notices. A price record keeps its own historical currency even when the bill uses another currency; amounts are never added across those currencies.

The view pages notices ten at a time and provides cadence-normalized subscription commitments in native currency buckets. Weekly/yearly/monthly intervals and multiple patterns use exact rational rates with rounding only at the final native total. The figures describe the active stated rate, not a count of charges due this month or an annual charge prediction for finite plans. Trial/cancellation metadata leaves a subscription in its active rate until paused/ended. Overflow is reported, not rounded into an unsafe total. An uncached, private GET report pages 50 notices and optionally binds the workspace revision. Its `asOf` changes the notice window against currently saved metadata/status; it does not reconstruct historical schedule state. Unknown/repeated options, invalid dates/currencies/pages and stale revisions fail explicitly.

Migration 34 adds three nullable date columns and preserves every prior row/revision. Reads omit absent dates to retain legacy shapes. Guarded date edits and explicit deletion use write generation 14; ordinary schedule changes retain generation 12. Full financial restore uses 15 and preserves manual field protections and statement review flags. Older destructive restores are rejected at guard insertion before deleting any date metadata. Older writers cannot clear dates or delete a dated schedule. Application and database checks validate real dates. Native Sure v2 imports retain lifecycle dates without changing the exported status, including active cancellations. Financial JSON/ZIP format 22 preserves dates across remapped schedule identities; versions 1–21 remain supported.

Validation includes 18 new behavioral tests, 118 new real Worker/D1 API checks, a seeded additive upgrade proof and 2,520 comparisons (2,688 notices) against the actual pinned Ruby BillsController and RecurringPriceChange. Coverage includes inclusivity/urgency/order, source currencies, date validation, report purity/paging, ownership, stale/concurrent edits, pause/end/resume capacity, schedule/payment preservation and native/legacy/JSON/ZIP recovery. The earlier matcher API balance helper was corrected to call `accountBalance` with a FinanceState and now asserts the actual 90,000-minor-unit balance instead of relying on a malformed argument shape; matcher checks still pass. Final regression/restart/publication results are recorded in the workspace README. Background recurring pipelines, repost/orphan repair, paycheck/pay-run planning and other household/assistant/extraction/investment/loan replay parity remain in progress. Bank/broker integrations remain excluded. Hosted sign-in, responsive appearance and keyboard/visual interaction remain unverified because browser access is unavailable.

Lifecycle milestone results: all 490 behavioral tests and 2,793 HTTP/API checks passed across 24 suites, including the 118 new lifecycle checks. TypeScript, production Worker build, original-source notice comparisons, seeded additive upgrade and isolated legacy budget upgrade passed. Clean-restart D1/R2 verification reproduced lifecycle metadata, notices and commitment totals plus every prior saved report, matching plan and original file byte snapshot. No production financial records were created for tests. Publication provenance and live-schema/access checks are recorded in the workspace README after deployment.

## Declared-income paycheck planning update

The port adapts the original `RecurringTransaction::PaycheckPlanner`: active declared income defines payday boundaries, same-day income sources merge, and an initial gap before payday is a cash-funded bridge. Detected inflows and schedules without a retained manual/declaration flag never establish paydays implicitly. Each payable obligation uses its remaining confirmed-payment amount and effective snoozed due date; overdue bills belong whole to the leading window. Income uses its full resolved expected amount until the occurrence closes, following the original rule. Payable transfers include credit/loan destinations, while savings/investment moves and non-active schedules are excluded.

Bills claim their own period’s income in chronological order. Only overflow reserves money from earlier positive-income periods, nearest first. The earliest paycheck absorbs an unavoidable residual shortfall, while the bridge never reserves for future bills. Each bill’s shares reassemble its full converted remaining amount exactly. Period income minus due and reserved amounts equals remaining; the bridge’s assessment instead compares its obligations with eligible recorded cash. Cash is not counted again as payday income, and no plan action creates transactions, allocations or actual reserve claims. This is an income/bill plan, not a running ledger cash simulation or a replacement for everyday spending and savings-goal reserves.

Income occurrences are generated virtually until sufficient boundaries exist for one to six requested periods, capped by supported calendar/finite-plan ends. This extends sparse annual schedules without requiring a mutating generation job or silently shortening an otherwise knowable plan to a ninety-day database window. Existing closed/payment-bearing history still controls occurrence identity. Income boundaries follow raw due dates, as upstream; bill snoozes control payable placement. The optional as-of selects forecast windows and recorded account balance dates against current schedule/payment statuses, not historical status replay. Twenty thousand open occurrences and safe integer total bounds fail explicitly rather than partially reporting a plan.

Forecast amounts use the existing exact direct/inverse manual quote lookup at the plan date, with five-day lookback. Original native amounts and the applied ratio/date/source are retained per arrival and bill. A missing bill or income conversion leaves known arithmetic visible but suppresses headroom/shortfall claims. A missing cash conversion or unreviewed account makes the bridge balance unknown rather than a misleading partial sum; independent paycheck windows remain measurable. Diagnostics retain full counts and the first hundred details. Zero amounts require no fictional exchange quote.

`recurring_entries.manual` and `accounts.cash_planning_excluded` are nullable metadata. New hand-entered schedules are declared, while new ordinary checking/savings accounts have known cash eligibility. Native Sure v2 restores the exported manual/detected flag and excludes Depository HSAs using the original subtype. Older flags remain unknown: income requires explicit declaration, and cash restrictions require explicit inclusion review before a bridge balance can be asserted. Archived, report-hidden, not-yet-open and explicitly excluded cash accounts are left out. Revision-bound preference commands change only planning metadata and reject wrong owners/types, stale or concurrent edits. Ordinary schedule/account editors preserve the preferences.

The recurring page opens an Income plan with date/currency/period controls, per-window income/due/reserved/shortfall figures, twenty-five-row bill pages, native quote details, declaration/cash review and navigation to cadence/status controls. CSV includes every period and bill with decimal currency amounts and formula-safe names. The private uncached GET endpoint validates and revision-binds options and complete CSV downloads. Stale/aborted client reads cannot replace newer results; demo calculations use in-memory data without server writes. No positive remainder is presented as universally safe spending; unconfirmed expense schedules and untracked obligations are disclosed.

Migration 35 preserves all old rows and revisions, adds only nullable metadata, protects values from older edits/deletes and blocks obsolete full restores before destructive writes. Current ordinary account/ledger/preference generation is 16, with manual field-protection triggers recognizing it; complete financial recovery uses 17 and retains restored field protections and statement flags. Financial JSON/ZIP format 23 preserves classification and cash choices with remapped identities; versions 1–22 remain supported for explicit review of missing metadata. Existing price and loan guard tests now verify that obsolete restores fail earlier, while the loan test still separately exercises an older ordinary writer’s term-deletion guard.

Validation includes 26 new behavioral cases, 99 new real Worker/D1 API checks, a seeded additive migration proof, and 3,600 comparisons against the actual pinned Ruby planner (10,200 full periods) covering boundary/cash/overdue/snooze/reserve/shortfall/share arithmetic. New cases also cover stale/inverse/whole/three-decimal quotes, unknown cash/income, finite/annual horizons, unsafe totals/backlogs, diagnostics, read-only CSV/paging, declared/detected/HSA/legacy recovery, ownership and concurrent preference changes. Final full regression, restart and publication evidence is recorded in the workspace README. Background recurring detection/generation/matching pipelines, repost/orphan repair, full bills overview/calendar parity, loan ledger replay and remaining household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain incomplete. Bank and broker integrations stay excluded. Hosted sign-in, visual/responsive appearance and keyboard interaction remain unverified because browser access is unavailable.

Paycheck milestone results: all 516 behavioral tests and 2,892 HTTP/API checks passed across 25 suites, including 99 income-plan checks. TypeScript, production Worker build, the original Ruby comparison, seeded additive upgrade and isolated legacy budget upgrade passed. Receipt negative-upload checks twice encountered the known local Wrangler “worker restarted mid-request” proxy response; a clean worker/fresh-owner rerun passed all 119 checks without relaxing assertions. Clean-restart D1/R2 verification reproduced planning preferences, full period/bill/quote reports and complete CSV bytes, alongside every prior saved report and original file snapshot. Published provenance, live schema and unchanged owner-only access are recorded in the workspace README after deployment. No production financial records were created for testing.

## Bills overview, calendar and schedule workspace update

Recurring now opens a bills workspace with Overview, Calendar and All schedules. The overview follows `BillsController#index` and `compute_kpis`: active outgoing bills and credit/loan transfers, separate past-due and parked obligations, remaining current-month balances, settled bills by their effective due month, and an inclusive seven-day window crossing month end. The Later section keeps the earliest occurrence per series through the raw ninety-day horizon; Next up excludes dates already past even during a grace period. Suggested payments open their saved evidence, imported suggested schedules remain reviewable, and existing price, lifecycle and income-plan controls remain available.

Payment progress is independent of date state. A partially paid bill can still be overdue; its remaining balance stays in Past due and its matcher window still extends through the reporting date. The older `occurrenceDetails.state` partial label remains compatible with existing callers, while `occurrenceScheduleState` supplies the raw derived date state. Parked unpaid history is visible without entering active totals or generating new reminders. The port accepts both paused and inactive history; the original overview query includes inactive only even though its management filter recognizes both.

The Sunday-first calendar uses raw scheduled due dates, while snoozes control effective state, export dates and overview ordering. Closed paid/skipped/missed history remains visible. Expected month totals include every occurrence; confirmed payment totals retain actual amounts and changed payments use the strict 0.01-major-unit threshold across currency precisions. Navigation caps at thirteen months beyond the reporting month, clips supported year boundaries, and generates clickable virtual occurrences without mutating D1. Desktop cells show three chips and full counts; selecting a day opens a paged agenda, which also serves narrow screens.

All schedules supports literal name/merchant search, type, current-occurrence payment-state filters, paused/inactive and ended/dismissed filters, native amount/name/status sorting and twenty-five-row pages. Ended schedules stay hidden by default with an explicit empty-state explanation. Current occurrence means the earliest open raw date, falling back to the latest closed one; sparse annual schedules and settled distant cycles retain their next occurrence. Subscription filters show commitment rates for the complete filtered result in separate native-currency buckets. Individual amounts always retain their currency.

Forecast conversion uses the reporting date and exact existing five-day direct/inverse quotes. Missing/range-limited conversions leave a known subtotal and suppress the complete total; no foreign bill silently disappears due to the global currency selector. Aggregate integer overflow is also unavailable. Empty lists display known zero, unlike upstream's nil-money placeholder. Reports describe the current stored statuses at the chosen forecast date; they do not reconstruct historical payment state. The read-only private `/api/bills-workspace` validates view-specific options, owner identity, duplicate parameters, revision and page bounds. There are no schema or backup-format changes; financial format 23 remains current. Occurrence walks reject more than 20,000 rows instead of truncating totals.

Calendar download produces a private `.ics` snapshot of active scheduled outgoing occurrences whose raw dates fall between the report date and ninety days later, including savings transfers as the upstream feed does. Events use effective snoozed dates and stable series/original-date identities, so virtual materialization does not change their UID. Output uses CRLF, escaped text and 75-byte UTF-8 folding. It includes obligations and native expected amounts only, with no account names or balances. It is a snapshot download, not a sessionless subscription: owner-private Sites dispatch requires ChatGPT sign-in.
