The original signed live-feed/revocation workflow remains pending, along with interleaved payday markers and the full per-series cost/history drawer. Existing payment and price history remain accessible.

Validation adds 28 behavioral cases and 87 real local Worker/D1 API checks, including end-to-end late-partial matching beyond its configured late window. The actual pinned Ruby `BillsController#index`, `compute_kpis` and `RecurringOccurrence` methods match 192 overview cases across date/grace boundaries, partial/overpayments, paid due-month attribution, snoozing, parked history, cross-month seven-day sums and later/next-up grouping. API checks cover owner isolation, no-write/revision protection, dated and missing FX, query limits, native import, complete pagination and calendar download. Restart verification adds exact overview/calendar/management/export snapshots. Full regression and publication results are recorded in the workspace README. Automatic recurring pipelines, repost/orphan repair, remaining bill-detail/feed workflows, loan principal/interest replay, household/assistant/extraction and other broad parity remain incomplete. Hosted browser interaction, responsive appearance, keyboard and real sign-in QA remain unverified because browser access is unavailable. Banks and brokers remain excluded.

## Per-schedule bill detail and cost-history update

A bill now opens a detail view from the overview, calendar or management list, including schedules without a current cycle. Its selected cycle, payment website, latest settled payment and existing cadence/lifecycle/matcher/price editors are directly accessible. The occurrence payment form links back to the complete bill story. Details use a private lazy read with revision binding and aborted/stale-response protection; demo calculations never fetch real workspace data. Closing the dialog retains the bills page’s tab, calendar day and filters.

The detail report adapts `BillsController#paid_analytics`, `load_deep_extras` and `ytd_paid_total`. Settled-cycle averages/minimum/maximum use the summed confirmed actual allocations of paid occurrences, never their frozen expected amounts or suggestions. The twelve-month chart and yearly totals instead include all confirmed allocations, including open partials and skipped/reopened cycles, grouped by the raw scheduled due date. Yearly average payment divides by allocation count, unlike the settled-cycle average. Money averages and cadence-normalized monthly/annual rates use exact BigInt arithmetic with rounding at native minor-unit precision. Unsafe totals are unavailable even when their independently computed average remains representable. Bills retain a fixed currency once history exists; original account amounts and immutable applied quotes remain separate evidence.

The original year-to-date query includes confirmed records whose payment date is on or after January 1, without an upper date bound. To preserve that rule without claiming a historical cash-flow total, the interface calls it Recorded since January 1 and discloses future-dated inclusions and undated exclusions. Yearly metrics may include recorded future cycles, as upstream. The report date selects date-state and chart windows against current saved statuses, rather than replaying prior edits. Latest dated linked accounts are listed together on ties; undated links are disclosed separately because the port does not retain the original allocation creation timestamps needed to establish their chronological position.

Coming up previews the next three distinct weekend-adjusted schedule dates within 400 days, independent of payment/snooze overrides, as the original drawer does. Parked/ended schedules label these explicitly as cadence previews and create no new reminders. The separate Open obligations list uses stored/virtual occurrences and effective snoozed dates, retaining distant stored records beyond the preview horizon. Current cycle still prioritizes the earliest raw open date, then latest closed history. Explicit cycle selection must belong to the requested series, including legitimate active virtual dates. A wrong owner, unknown series or invalid cycle cannot expose another bill’s history.

Closed cycles, open obligations, payment records and immutable price changes are independently paged in groups of twelve; complete totals never depend on the selected page. Unlike the upstream twelve-row history/four-year display cap, older cycles and complete yearly metrics remain reachable. Payment rows expose native conversion snapshots after a source transaction is unlinked, resolve split-line names and support navigation to the occurrence or existing account entry. Price records keep their own historical currencies. A private uncached CSV exports every payment record and state, with native/bill amounts, account entry/payment/quote dates and the saved rational conversion. Names and account labels are formula-safe, amounts remain decimal strings, and the UTF-8 BOM is preserved by the browser download. No database migration or financial-format change is required; version 23 remains current.

Reviewing large histories exposed repeated full-array identity scans in recurring occurrence stand-ins. The same membership and closest-date behavior now uses indexed identities and a binary search of distinct due dates, preserving earlier-date and stable original-date tie ordering. The regression includes more than 15,000 closed weekly cycles without recreating settled reminders. Report history/allocations/open lists fail explicitly above 20,000 records rather than silently truncating cost totals.

Validation adds 21 behavioral cases and 80 local Worker/D1 API checks. The actual pinned Ruby detail controller and recurrence-rate method match 324 cost cases across currency precision, single/multiple payments, partial/skipped/suggested records, date attribution, snoozes, future/undated payments and cadence rates. The existing 192 original overview cases and 4,032 original matcher explanations still match after recurrence indexing. API evidence includes no-write ownership/revision guards, all history pages, legitimate virtual selection, native/JSON recovery and original conversion evidence after library-rate edits and source unlinking. Clean-restart verification adds all four private report sections and complete CSV snapshots. Full regression and publication provenance are recorded in the workspace README. Hosted sign-in, responsive/visual and keyboard checks remain unverified because browser access is unavailable. Automatic recurring pipelines, repost/orphan repair, interleaved payday markers, live calendar subscription/revocation, loan ledger replay and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain incomplete. Bank and broker integrations stay excluded.

## Recurring-pattern identification and candidate review update

Find recurring patterns now adapts Sure’s `Identifier` and rule-based `Classifier` over manual transactions and imported CSV history. Identification uses three calendar months of native-currency observations, grouped by merchant or exact original name, account and currency. Sorted amounts join a cluster only within 7.5% of its running mean, evaluated with exact rational integers. A qualifying cluster needs three observations, a latest date within forty-five days, and every day-of-month within two circular days of the circular median. The most recent actual amount remains the proposed price; rounded native positive min/max/mean values describe its variance. Several observations on one date can qualify under the original rule, so the interface describes these as guesses rather than proving monthly recurrence.

The Add-bill mode offers unclaimed two-observation outflows with an exact mean of at least one native currency unit. Income-source candidates instead use two deposits within ninety days without imposing amount clustering or day consistency; any existing same-source income schedule claims that source regardless of amount and can be account-unassigned. Candidate drafts open the ordinary schedule editor for amount/type/cadence review. They seed the next due date from the latest entry’s day and default income to biweekly, following the original prefill behavior. Saving a draft creates an explicitly declared manual schedule. Candidate eligibility uses the unrounded mean, so fractional minor-unit averaging cannot promote a sub-unit cluster across the threshold.

Classifier precedence, keyword/stem boundaries, installment markers, explicit autopay wording and the flat modest credit-card fallback follow the source. Currency-specific precision applies the 150-major-unit ceiling correctly. Classification is only an initial guess: existing amounts, names, types, nonblank categories and lifecycle statuses remain intact. Exact identity plus amount-nearness claims existing detected, manual and ended records; known manual and ended rows cannot be recreated by a matching pattern. Missing legacy manual flags are conservatively claimed without automatic cadence edits. Unpinned detected monthly days may refresh; hand-edited cadences carry the original `schedule_pinned_at` hint, and renames retain their detected identity. Detected deletion now dismisses as an ended tombstone with history retained, matching the original controller; manually declared deletions retain their existing explicit removal behavior.

The manual variance pass follows the source’s separate six-month query, stable seed-amount half-to-double band and true cadence/weekend/end membership. It can recognize matching observations before the declaration’s anchor without creating prior debt. Manual amounts/cadences/statuses stay fixed, variance stats can refresh, and only a blank non-income category can fill from owned source categories. A changed average freezes already-due null estimates at their previous value; payment-bearing/closed history remains. Detected cadence shifts discard only regeneratable future unallocated reminders, with existing nearest-cycle stand-ins preventing duplicate old cycles. Repeated unchanged reviews, including overlapping clusters claiming one series, save no net change.

As in the actual collector, future-dated recorded entries, reporting-excluded rows and split parents/children remain eligible observations; the review discloses these facts and shows source evidence. Discovery excludes investment/crypto accounts and transfer kinds. The manual variance pass uses its own broader source rules, as upstream. The report date selects windows against current statuses and classifications, not historical lifecycle replay. A preview and its complete proposal set are bound to owner, workspace revision, options and resulting schedule content. Pages contain twenty-five proposals and twenty-five source observations per row; aggregate counts cover all records. Approval saves all reviewed suggestions/observation updates atomically, with concurrent or stale commits rejected and no ledger or account-balance writes. New discoveries remain suggested and matching remains opt-in; users confirm/dismiss or edit them. Income requires explicit declaration before defining paydays.

Provenance lives in the existing bounded matching hints under `sites_detection` (version, first/latest observation, count and exact mean ratio), with the original raw hints retained. Existing schema, protected writer generation 16 and financial JSON/ZIP format 23 preserve this metadata; no schema migration is required. Preview/saves are limited to 20,000 transaction/split records, one million manual-variance comparisons and 300 changed schedules. Limits fail explicitly before partial writes. Native transactions can seed a paged discovery; existing native manual/detected and pin hints and current JSON recovery retain identity/tombstone behavior.

Validation adds 24 behavioral cases and 82 real local Worker/D1 API checks. The actual pinned Ruby code matches 540 identifier collector/bill-candidate/income-source cases, 306 classifier cases and 756 `Schedule#matches_day?` cases. Cases include native precision, exact bounds, word boundaries/stems, phase before/after anchors, weekend shifts and finite ends. API checks cover preview/no-op invariance, all-page approval, concurrent/stale receipts, manual/ended claims, draft declarations, preserved estimates, pinned renames, logical deletion, native/JSON recovery and unchanged account money. Clean-restart verification adds metadata and repeat discovery plans. Full regression and publication provenance are recorded in the workspace README.

The remaining pipeline stages include automatic occurrence materialization, activation/history backfill, orphan/repost repair, coordinated matching/price stages, cycle-aware cleanup and background scheduling. Existing explicit matching/price reviews and virtual recurring views remain available. Interleaved payday markers, live calendar subscription/revocation, loan principal/interest replay and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain incomplete. Hosted browser, keyboard, responsive and actual sign-in QA remain unverified because browser access is unavailable. No bank or broker integration is included.


## Recurring generation, historical reconstruction and live refresh

Bills → History & refresh now provides three previewed operations. Materialize stores each active schedule’s current cycle and at least ninety days of upcoming occurrences, extending to the next sparse annual occurrence. Finite installment plans extend to their complete count within the supported 1900–2199 calendar, including when weekend skipping makes the original average horizon too short. Identity uses series/original date; re-running is idempotent. Existing dates, overrides, snoozes, closed states and payment-bearing records remain intact. Nearest-cycle stand-ins prevent a retained old date from duplicating a shifted cadence.

Reconstruct history additionally generates six calendar months of past cycles, including cadence phases before a declaration, and links only unambiguous exact-tier recorded payments. It then removes uncovered scheduled reminders before the bounded current cycle. Closed records and records with any allocation stay in place. Newly generated unmatched reminders before a declared or resumed start are discarded; real earlier payments can still be reconstructed. A finished finite plan without a next cycle discloses that uncovered history is retained for review. Historical matching includes future-dated recorded entries within generated windows and explicitly counts them.

Refresh runs live matching through the review date, followed by two-settlement price observations. Only schedules explicitly known to be detected (`manual=0`) adopt the observed new price. Declared and unclassified legacy schedules keep their stated amounts. Before a detected amount changes, scheduled null estimates already due through the actual save date are frozen at their prior resolved amount. Historical review dates cannot reprice later already-due debt. Saved overrides and closed cycles remain intact; future null estimates inherit the new amount.

The selected schedule limits generation and pruning. Matching and price observations remain family-wide, preserving cross-series ambiguity checks. Matching honors the existing opt-in preferences. A clearly labeled one-review option includes all active schedules without changing those preferences. Preview pages contain twenty-five changes per section; full counts and one owner/revision/options/content-bound receipt cover the complete result. Atomic bulk D1 writes apply occurrence inserts/updates/deletes, allocations, price history and detected amounts together. Concurrent, stale or changed reviews fail without partial writes, and a no-op does not bump the workspace revision. These actions never create account entries or change account balances.

Financial format 23, schema migration 35 and protected writer generation 16 remain current. Native Sure and current JSON recovery retain generated/closed history, payment evidence and detected/manual provenance. Reviews reject excessive occurrence, matching or price-history counts before any writes; calendar limits are explicit. Automatic activation hooks, the combined discovery/orphan-repair/cleanup pipeline and background jobs remain pending. Banks and brokers are excluded. Hosted sign-in, responsive layout and keyboard/browser interaction remain unverified.

Validation adds 23 behavioral cases and 73 real local Worker/D1 API checks, including atomic approval of a complete 600-installment plan across review pages, concurrency, idempotence, history pruning and protection, family-wide exact matching, immutable quote evidence, detected-only repricing, actual-clock estimate protection and native/JSON recovery. The actual pinned Ruby `Schedule` and `OccurrenceGenerator` match 1,296 phase/cycle/next/start/materialization/stand-in cases. Fifty-four finite weekend-skipping horizons are deliberately extended beyond the source estimate to include their complete count. The existing 4,032 original matcher explanations, 1,080 original price-detector cases, 540 discovery groups, 306 classifications and 756 cadence membership cases still match. Restart verification retains generated finite plans, pruned histories, allocations, prices and repeat maintenance plans alongside the existing D1/R2 snapshots. Full regression and publication provenance are recorded in the workspace README.


## Combined recurring workflow and deleted-entry repair

Bills → Recurring review now opens the complete workflow: discover/update patterns, materialize upcoming cycles, repair deleted-entry links, match live payments, then observe repeated settled prices. Optional six-month history reconstruction runs last, following the original `Pipeline#run!` ordering. New detections remain suggestions. The pattern-only candidate dialog also links to this complete review, and the three individual maintenance operations remain available.

The discovery update now uses one shared pure application routine for the standalone detector and the pipeline. It preserves manual/pinned/ended intent, freezes already-due average estimates before observation changes, and discards only regeneratable future unallocated dates after a detected cadence moves. Generation, repaired links, new matches, price events, detected amount changes and history pruning are computed together and committed in one guarded D1 batch. A single owner/revision/options/content-bound receipt covers every page and stage; concurrent or stale approval cannot partially write. Repeating a settled review saves nothing and leaves the revision unchanged.

Deleted-entry repair considers allocations whose ledger source disappeared and whose original source was automatic matching or manual confirmation. Record-only `user_created` payments remain independent. It requires the saved date, native currency and exact source/bill magnitude, the schedule’s assigned account when present, and related identity: matching known merchants, a normalized name/prefix/alias, or the outgoing transfer’s paired destination. Any entry already referenced by another allocation is unavailable for repair. The original first-related-candidate behavior is retained and competing candidate counts are disclosed before approval. Repairs preserve allocation IDs, signed ledger values, original payment amounts, states, closure records, confidence and score signals. Only the source pointer changes.

Saved conversions additionally bind the original account and account-posting date. When the user declared another paid date, repair uses that immutable original posting date and clearly identifies the difference. Quotes are retained rather than recalculated; a native claim must fit the replacement entry, unknown foreign native amounts stay unresolved, and saved root rejections remain sticky. Repair applies to eligible historical allocations even when matching is disabled or their schedule is parked; the one-review matching override continues to control new matches only. New matches retain existing native source-capacity rules and ended-schedule capacity semantics. Repairs do not import provider data, initiate payments, create ledger entries or change account balances.

Review sections now include new suggestions, changed schedules and repaired payments alongside occurrence, matching and price changes. Each section has twenty-five rows with complete counts. Repair rows show the selected replacement, signed amount/date, number of alternatives and retained conversion. Schedule and repricing counts can overlap. Schema migration 35, protected writer generation 16 and financial format 23 are unchanged. Native/JSON recovery and clean-restart checks retain repaired links and evidence.

Validation adds 24 behavioral cases and 61 real local Worker/D1 API checks. The original Ruby `Matcher#repair_orphans!` matches 1,296 cases across allocation sources, native currency precision, incoming/outgoing signs, identity and exact date/amount/account/claim gates. Both actual source pipeline orders also match. API cases include combined discovery/cadence/generation/repair/match/price/history approval, first-page and last-page receipts, all thirty-one repair rows, concurrent/stale/no-op/owner guards, parked history, unchanged ledger balances and retained foreign quotes when original posting and declared payment dates differ.

Automatic activation-triggered generation/backfill, cycle-aware cleanup and unattended background scheduling remain pending. Interleaved payday markers, live calendar subscriptions, loan principal/interest ledger replay and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity also remain incomplete. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual browser QA are unverified because browser access is unavailable. Banks and brokers remain excluded.


## Automatic schedule lifecycle and recoverable activation history

Creating an active schedule now stores its current cycle and upcoming window immediately, including the next sparse occurrence and all remaining finite installments that fit the supported calendar. Suggested and parked creations create no reminders. Calendar, start-date, currency and status edits rebuild scheduled allocation-free reminders from today onward, following the original generator’s inclusive boundary. Closed cycles and any cycle carrying a confirmed or suggested allocation survive unchanged. Paid and shifted old cycles remain nearest-cycle stand-ins. A regeneratable future override can be replaced by a cadence edit; earlier unpaid history is retained at its previous estimate.

Amount-only changes keep future identities/overrides and freeze already-due scheduled null estimates at their previous resolved amount. Closed records remain unchanged. A combined amount/cadence edit also protects a regenerated obligation due today. Currency may change while all existing reminders are unpaid, unallocated and strictly future; they rebuild in the new currency. Any payment, closed cycle or already-due history still locks the currency. No historical conversion is inferred. Metadata-only edits retain future overrides and do not rebuild the stream.

A suggested-to-active transition first generates upcoming rows and reconstructs six calendar months of history, then regenerates the future, preserving any payment/closure found by reconstruction. This applies both to the ordinary editor and the status action, including detected suggestions. Existing matching opt-ins remain authoritative; the exact-tier pass remains family-wide even though generation and pruning are scoped to the activated schedule. This callback does not run discovery, repair or price observation. Recorded future entries can qualify within the generated window, as in the source backfiller; the save response counts these separately and states that they do not prove a posted payment. Ordinary resumes start new reminders from today and do not add an unpaid paused gap.

A shared D1 write planner commits schedule state, generated/rebuilt occurrences, historical allocations and recovery metadata in one owner/revision-guarded batch. Concurrent and stale status writes cannot half-activate or duplicate settlements. If history planning exceeds a supported limit or cannot validate safely, activation and its ordinary reminder generation can still save with a durable pending marker and no partial matches. Manage status shows the pending reason and a guarded Retry history reconstruction action. A later active save also retries. Successful explicit history review clears pending work only for the covered scope/date. Pausing/ending cancels pending activation reconstruction; resuming does not revive that cancelled work. Unattended job retries remain future work.

History pruning now records a monotonic unpaid-history cutoff in the existing matching hints. Virtual views and later edits honor it, so an uncovered old reminder that was pruned cannot silently reappear as virtual debt. Actual stored closed/payment-bearing records remain visible below the cutoff. An explicitly reviewed stored older row remains visible too. A historical review cannot lower an established cutoff; finite plans without a bounded current cycle do not acquire a new cutoff. The metadata readers validate recognized version/date/string shapes and ignore malformed or unknown records without rewriting unrelated raw hints.

Pending/completed/cancelled work and reconstruction cutoffs live under bounded `sites_activation_backfill` and `sites_reconstructed_history` hints. Schema 35, protected writer generation 16 and financial JSON/ZIP format 23 remain unchanged. Current recovery retains those records and remaps their surrounding financial links. Native imports continue to restore their supplied rows without automatically re-running lifecycle callbacks.

Validation adds 26 behavioral cases and 58 real local Worker/D1 API checks. They cover creation, sparse/finite plans, today’s regeneration boundary, clipped large repeat/count intervals, future overrides and payment-bearing protection, paused gaps, detected activation and family-wide opt-ins, future-date disclosure, concurrent/stale/no-op/owner guards, currency locks, a greater-than-300-match activation overload with durable retry, current JSON recovery and virtual-pruning persistence. The actual pinned Ruby `OccurrenceGenerator#regenerate_future!` matches 576 callback outcomes across cadence, interval, weekend/end, manual/detected and active/paused cases at the port’s complete finite horizon. Existing original comparisons also pass for 1,296 generation cases, 4,032 matcher explanations, 1,296 orphan repairs and both pipeline orders, plus 192 bills overview cases. Legacy integration assertions now distinguish automatic stored reminders from on-demand generation and verify past versus future estimates independently.

Cycle-aware cleanup and unattended generation/detection/retry jobs remain pending, as do interleaved payday markers, live calendar subscriptions, loan principal/interest ledger replay and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity. Hosted sign-in, responsive/visual and keyboard browser QA remain unverified because browser access is unavailable. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

## Recurring staleness, observation facts and inactive retention

The foreground cleanup ports `RecurringTransaction::Cleaner`, `staleness_threshold_date`, `should_be_inactive?` and the model's recent-activity filters. The threshold is the earlier of the six-calendar-month manual/two-month automatic floor and `ceil(2 * 365.25 / occurrences_per_year)` days ago. Reference equality stays active; a missing reference never proves inactivity. Recent activity has no upper date bound and does not consult report exclusions or saved allocations. It requires native currency, assigned account, signed exact amount or a genuine observed variance band, exact merchant or original detected name, the original circular SQL day-of-month gate and real schedule phase/end/weekend membership. Transfers use the outgoing source/destination pair. Identity indexing avoids unrelated cross-products; more than one million qualified comparisons fails before writes.

Retirement uses the current save-date callback protection boundary, preserves closed/payment-bearing history and cancels pending activation backfill. Expired-history removal is explicit and separate: only originally inactive, known automatic records with a known `updatedAt` strictly earlier than six calendar months qualify. The frozen UTC millisecond clock retains month-end clamping and exact boundary semantics. Newly retired records cannot be removed in the same combined pass. The preview includes every affected dependent-history count; `DELETE HISTORY` is required when any recurring graph will be deleted. The ordinary shared recurring writer refuses parent deletion unless the cleanup path opts in after receipt/revision/confirmation validation. Ledger entries/accounts are unchanged.

Migration 36 adds nullable typed `last_occurrence_on`, `occurrence_count` and `updated_at`; it preserves old IDs, money and revisions. It backfills only valid `sites_detection` observations already present, never inferring dates from allocation history or guessing legacy edit age. DB guards validate supported dates, nonnegative integer counts and canonical UTC millisecond timestamps. Semantic edits touch timestamps; no-op writes do not. Current ordinary writer 18 continues manual transaction/split protections, while recovery 19 retains imported reconciliation/report protections and edit timestamps. Older recurring writers and restores are rejected before data loss. Native Sure dates/counts/offset timestamps are retained; source negative income bounds normalize to increasing magnitudes. Financial format 24 JSON/ZIP recovery retains all facts; formats 1–23 remain accepted.

`node --experimental-strip-types tests/recurring-cleanup-upstream.mjs /path/to/pinned-sure` executes the original Cleaner, model methods and Schedule against 1,050 cadence/provenance/reference/activity/retention cases. `npm run test:recurring-cleanup-upgrade` exercises an isolated schema-35 → 36 migration, valid/invalid legacy hints, unchanged IDs/money/revision, unknown clocks, old/current write guards, timestamp validation/touch/no-op/recovery and manual field protections. The cleanup API suite covers complete graph removal, protected history, exact cutoff, confirmation/receipt/revision/owner/clock validation, concurrent approval, page-independent removal, no-op saves and native/current JSON recovery.

Unattended generation/detection/retry, interleaved payday markers, live calendar subscription/revocation, loan principal/interest ledger replay and wider household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain incomplete. Hosted sign-in, responsive/visual and keyboard browser QA remain unverified. Banks and brokers remain excluded.

Verification for the cleanup update: all 692 behavioral tests and 3,398 HTTP/API checks across 32 suites pass, including 64 cleanup API checks. The isolated schema-35 → 36 upgrade passes 27 checks; 1,050 original Cleaner/model comparisons, 540 detection clusters, 306 classifier cases, 756 cadence matches, 576 regeneration callbacks, 1,296 repairs and both pipeline orders match the pinned Sure source. Strict TypeScript, the production build and clean-restart D1/R2 snapshots/reports/original bytes pass. The regression run caught a missing manual-field protection allowance for writer 18; the migration was corrected and the complete suite then passed. Hosted browser/sign-in/visual QA remains outstanding.

## Interleaved payday windows and responsive month agenda

The overview now ports `BillsController#month_pay_periods` and `BillsHelper#bills_pay_period_markers`. The original planner supplies up to six payday windows plus a leading gap. Only windows overlapping today through month end are considered, and markers are omitted unless more than one window overlaps. Active, manually declared income defines boundaries from its next open due occurrences; same-day sources merge and income snoozes do not move that boundary. A zero-known-value foreign arrival remains an arrival with unknown valuation, rather than being mislabeled as a missing paycheck.

Markers attach to the first chronological monthly row inside each period. They use effective snoozed bill dates and sum full expected amounts from current-month paid cycles plus every active non-overdue upcoming occurrence, including repeats and next-month dates. They are separate from the remaining-amount shares/reserves in the income planner. Missing quotes suppress a complete total while retaining the known subtotal, improving on the Source helper’s silent omission. A window beyond the existing ninety-day occurrence horizon discloses its counted-through date. Pagination repeats context with a continuation label and retains complete period counts/totals. A bounded planner arithmetic failure leaves the existing bills overview available and reports why the optional payday context is unavailable.

The calendar keeps Source’s bill-only scope, raw scheduled placement and descending native expected amount within each day. The responsive phone view is now an agenda instead of a compressed grid, with twenty-five-row pages, whole-month/day counts and continuation headings. `/api/bills-workspace?view=agenda` is private, uncached, read-only and revision-bound; calendar month clamping, date validation and view-specific option guards apply. Page totals exclude adjacent-month grid spillovers. Existing desktop selected-day details and outgoing-only signed-in `.ics` snapshots remain available.

`tests/bill-pay-periods-upstream.mjs` executes the original PaycheckPlanner, controller and helper against 384 weekly/monthly/declaration/closed-income/snooze/paid/native-FX cases. Focused behavioral tests cover remaining-versus-full amounts, month-end counts, mixed-currency uncertainty, sparse horizons, overflow isolation, deterministic no-write projections, continuing markers and agenda ordering/pagination. The API suite additionally checks actual owner isolation, stale revisions, strict agenda options, manual quote updates, native/current-JSON recovery and unchanged balances/history. Schema 36 and financial format 24 are unchanged.

Unattended generation/detection/retry, live calendar subscriptions, loan principal/interest ledger replay and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain incomplete. Private Sites require ChatGPT identity or platform service access before app routes run, so an external calendar client cannot use an app-only token to reach a subscription feed; a supported narrow access path is still needed without changing the Site’s audience. No access policy or service credential is exposed or changed by this update. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive rendering and visual browser QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

Verification for payday markers and the month agenda: all 718 behavioral tests and 3,452 HTTP/API checks across 33 suites pass, including 54 new payday/agenda checks. All 384 original marker/controller comparisons pass. Strict TypeScript, the production build and clean-restart D1/R2 snapshots, derived reports, original bytes and authenticated payday/agenda API pages pass. Browser interaction, actual sign-in, keyboard and responsive/visual rendering remain unverified.

## Loan principal/interest ledger replay

The port now executes the original `Loan::PrincipalPayments` model as an integrated derived accounting path. It is explicitly reviewed per loan, matching the original preview gate’s requirement for opting into recalculated balances. Complete terms and an opening debt/date exactly matching genuine origination are required. Existing and native-imported accounts have an unknown preference and keep raw recorded-movement accounting; no migration infers that a provider’s backdated current anchor is a full origination history. A review shows both balances, dates, all daily changes and totals before enabling/disabling; owner/revision/UTC review date/complete-content receipts and `APPLY ACCOUNTING` bind the write. No-op preferences leave revisions unchanged.

The engine walks native root transactions, valuation dates and contracted due dates in one ordered stream. Each scheduled period charges interest once on its opening principal, under the saved fixed/variable rate and 30/360, Actual/365 or Actual/actual convention. Mid-period valuation resets use the original US 30/360 stub rule. Payments cover carried interest, then a charged current period, then the selected instalment’s remaining premium, then principal. A nil deferred period differs from a real zero charge. Missed charges carry without capitalization. Five-day grace payments reduce the new period’s opening principal; outside that window the period keeps its opening figure. Refunds/drawdowns add principal, and overpayments leave credit rather than being clipped. New charges stop once contracted periods run out; late fees, penalties and unrecorded extensions are not invented.

Linked transfers default to full payments. The editable principal-only contract flag instead applies those native loan legs wholly to principal and counts carried/current interest as separately paid, as Source does. The report exposes that separate-interest assumption explicitly; it neither creates nor proves the paying-account expense. Transfer linkage uses the actual retained pair, including foreign native receiving amounts; later reporting quote edits cannot rewrite principal. Reporting exclusions and split children do not affect root replay. All end-of-day valuation kinds retain the existing port’s anchor precedence; a same-day payment remains in replay history even when the valuation resets that day’s balance. The port’s opening balance remains before transactions on its opening date, as elsewhere in this ledger.

`balance`/`accountBalance`, net worth, reconciliation and payoff projections share the selected principal calculation. Saved payment, rate, insurance and principal-only edits recalculate on the next read. The ordinary terms/opening forms preserve the preference and reject loss of required origination facts until replay is disabled. Completed statements are marked for review when mode or enabled terms change; reconciled transaction amounts/flags are not rewritten by the mode change. Current reconciliation adjustment/undo still writes/restores an explicit end-of-day anchor and resets replay consistently. The per-isolate contracted-schedule cache holds at most four objects, with content checks for mutated terms, rather than retaining every long loan schedule in a large workspace.

`/api/loan-replay` supplies private uncached, revision-bound historical pages and complete CSV. The CSV uses exact decimal strings, formula-safe labels and a preserved UTF-8 BOM, including negative/credit principal balances, covered premiums, separate-interest assumptions and original transaction/reset IDs. Pages contain twenty-five payment/reset days while totals/approval cover every day. Calculations end at the last recorded entry through the reporting date; they do not estimate the current day’s otherwise unrecorded accrued interest.

Migration 37 adds only nullable `accounts.loan_replay` and guards; old IDs, balances, transaction/valuation rows, closed statement snapshots and revisions remain unchanged. New ordinary generation 20, automatic generation 22 and recovery 21 preserve manual field protections and imported reconciliation/report flags. Old recurring/loan restores and older writers cannot remove reviewed preferences or mutate active replay’s loan/paired entry and valuation inputs. SQL validates binary loan-only preferences and complete matching origination anchors; full workspace validation also rejects malformed enabled recovery. Financial format 25 JSON/ZIP retains choices and remapped source graphs. Formats 1–24 remain accepted without guessing eligibility. Native Sure imports retain contract flags and original observations and require explicit replay review.

Source verification executes the actual Ruby `PrincipalPayments`, Simulator, RateResolver, AmortizationSchedule and Insurance against 1,944 cases / 7,344 payment/reset days, covering USD/JPY/KWD precision, both full and principal-only links, fixed/variable rates, all day counts, insurance, partial/missed/early/late/grace patterns, refunds, same-day/mid-period resets and credit overpayment. Focused unit/API checks cover integrated historical/current balances, net worth and projections, no-write/revision/owner/confirmation/concurrency guards, automatic CSV and same-/cross-currency transfers, library quote invariance, saved-rate changes, statement review/adjustment/undo, complete pages/CSV and native/current/legacy recovery. The isolated schema-36 → 37 test covers real D1 guards, preserved rows/revisions and restored field/statement flags.

Unattended generation/detection/retry, live calendar subscriptions and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain incomplete. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive rendering and visual browser QA remain unverified. No bank or broker integration is included.

Verification for loan principal replay: all 748 behavioral tests and 3,568 HTTP/API checks across 34 suites pass, including 116 new replay API checks. The 1,944 original Ruby comparisons cover 7,344 payment/reset days, and the isolated schema-36 → 37 upgrade passes 27 checks. Strict TypeScript, the production build and clean-restart D1/R2 state, original bytes, private replay JSON/CSV and integrated payoff endpoints pass. The unchanged 119-check receipt suite passed on a fresh worker after a local Undici upload EPIPE; the final 116-check replay suite also passed after bounding the schedule cache. No production financial data was created. Interactive browser, actual sign-in, keyboard and responsive/visual QA remain unverified.


## Reviewed matching of existing manual and CSV transfers

The port now adapts `TransferMatchesController`, `Transfer::Matcher`, `Transaction::Transferable`, `Family::AutoTransferMatchable` and confirmed `Transfer#confirm!` / release behavior from the pinned source. Open a saved transaction to choose an existing opposite movement in a different active account, within the source's inclusive 30-day manual window. Exact same-currency amounts are mandatory; a fee must be a separate actual expense. Same-category counterparts sort ahead of other categories within the suggestion tier, then exact currency and nearest date. Currency suggestions compare exact rational amounts within 10%, using the port's existing five-day direct/inverse quote lookup or a bridge through the profile currency. These are review aids, never automatic writes. The source controller permits explicit cross-currency confirmation independently of that suggestion band; the port exposes those pairs as manual currency review, even without a quote.

Approval links the existing rows rather than recreating them. IDs, both posting dates, signed native amounts, CSV duplicate keys, descriptions, notes, categories, merchant/tag labels, field protections, reconciliation flags and receipt links survive. The destination determines outgoing reporting kind; the incoming leg becomes funds movement. Existing categories are preserved rather than creating an inferred investment category. A transfer detail snapshot retains the two actual amounts and their exact implied rate, so later quote edits cannot rewrite recorded cash. Preview is read-only and shows both entries, before/after kinds, derived account balances through its UTC review date, principal-only loan assumptions, fee retention and the count of completed statements that will require review. Owner identity, complete review contents, revision and UTC date bind the receipt. D1's existing guarded batch commits both legs and snapshot atomically; stale/concurrent approvals fail before writes.

Unlink is distinct from deleting the transfer. Both surviving legs return to standard reporting. Their IDs, receipts, CSV keys and actual native amounts remain; the conversion association is removed. In an intentional adaptation, separate fee transactions are retained as standalone expenses with all original receipts rather than being deleted by the source transfer's dependent fee destruction. The existing delete-transfer operation remains available for deliberate removal of both legs and fees. Unlink can release an imported excluded transfer without clearing its exclusion. Archived accounts require restoration. Split roots/children, fees belonging to another transfer, recurring-payment backing and goal-usage backing cannot be newly reclassified. Recurring payment pointers must first be explicitly unlinked, retaining their independent history. Entries backing goal usage remain standalone purchases; pairing never silently removes saved conversion evidence or usage.

Matching can change principal-only loan replay even when the native payments do not change. The review uses the same integrated balance engine as net worth, statements and payoff planning. Approval explicitly flags completed statements from the affected entry dates, including the case where the reporting kind was already correct. Unlink reverses the classification effect without synthesizing interest expenses or changing payment amounts.

This uses schema 37 and the existing writer 20 / automatic 22 / recovery 21 and financial format 25. No migration or backfill is needed. JSON/ZIP recovery already retains the linked graph, receipt references and exact rate snapshots with fresh remapped IDs. Persistent rejected-pair hints, pending automatic transfer suggestions and one-sided counterpart creation remain pending. No bank or broker connection is included. Unattended recurring jobs, external calendar access, broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity and hosted browser/sign-in/visual checks also remain incomplete.

Verification for existing-entry transfer matching: all 781 behavioral tests and 3,659 HTTP/API checks across 35 suites pass, including 33 new behavioral cases and 91 new real Worker/D1 checks. The new checks cover CSV identity/deduplication, read-only review, concurrent/stale/owner/origin guards, native cross-currency snapshots without a library rate, eligibility, fee retention, principal-only loan balance changes, completed-statement review and JSON/ZIP recovery. Clean-restart D1/R2 snapshots, original receipt bytes and deterministic transfer reviews pass. The unchanged 102-check file suite passed on a fresh worker after a local Undici upload EPIPE; the original failure log is retained. Strict TypeScript and the production build pass. No production financial records were used for testing. Hosted browser, actual sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified.


## Reviewed creation of a missing transfer counterpart

The port now adapts `Transfer::Matcher#match_to_account!` and `TransferMatchesController#create`'s new-entry path, with the guarded idempotency approach used by `Transfer::Creator`. The existing transaction stays in place and exactly one opposite entry is added in another owned, active manual account. The source defaults to the same posting date and copies entry currency; the native-account port defaults to the same date, permits an explicit posting date within the source's 30-day confirmed window, and requires the actual target-native amount when currencies differ. It never silently treats a USD amount as JPY or guesses a foreign native posting. Same-currency amounts remain equal and opposite. A user-supplied name/notes apply only to the created entry; the original description, amount, date, notes, category, merchant/tags, locks, CSV duplicate key, reconciliation flags and receipt associations are preserved. Generated default names are bounded to the existing 160-character limit.

The missing entry is clearly labeled in the shared review and starts unreconciled with no copied receipts, category or CSV import key. Destination-derived reporting kinds match the existing transfer behavior. The exact native amounts and both dates form the immutable rational conversion snapshot. Account balances are calculated against the original state before adding the new row, so the target's actual debit/credit effect remains visible. Principal-only loan linkage can reclassify the original payment's interest/principal treatment; preview shows this separately from the new target movement. Completed statements from either affected posting date are explicitly marked for review, including a classification link with an unchanged outgoing kind.

Possible existing counterparts are same signed native amounts in the chosen target account within 30 days of the original. The review includes eligible, already-linked, excluded and other ineligible records; only eligible rows can switch to normal existing-entry matching. Complete duplicate counts and all-page receipts cover more than 25 rows. Approval requires explicit duplicate acknowledgment when any such record exists; this still permits genuinely separate same-amount transfers. A generated manual entry does not predict a future CSV's description or duplicate key, so a later import can contain the same movement and must be reviewed.

A form-scoped UUID and canonical normalized creation fingerprint use the existing transfer detail request fields. Preview binds owner, complete creation/duplicate review, options, revision and UTC date. Both new entry and original classification plus the applied snapshot and statement flags commit atomically under writer 20. Concurrent claims cannot leave two counterpart rows or a half-pair. Retrying a successful identical request returns its stored pair without changing revision, even after another workspace edit; reusing its request ID with different details fails. Unlink removes the transfer request association, preserves both standalone entries and existing fees/receipts, and a stale cached approval cannot recreate it without a fresh review. Current recovery remaps both entries and clears request identities, as for other transfers.

Recurring-payment backing, goal usage, split roots/children, existing linked fee entries, report-excluded entries and archived source/target accounts retain the existing manual-match safeguards. The existing recurring payment conversion/history is never silently removed. The standard post-save pending-savings hook now accepts committed counterpart creation only. A genuinely new receiving entry can match an eligible pledge; preview, repeated successful submissions and an old receiving entry whose missing debit was added cannot invent a new inflow event.

Schema 37, ordinary writer 20, automatic writer 22, recovery 21 and financial JSON/ZIP format 25 remain unchanged. No migration or automatic historical money write is required. Persistent rejected-pair hints and pending automatic transfer suggestions remain unfinished, along with unattended recurring jobs, external calendar access and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity. Bank and broker connections remain excluded. Hosted browser, actual sign-in, keyboard, responsive rendering and visual QA remain unverified.

Verification for missing counterpart creation: all 827 behavioral tests and 3,809 HTTP/API checks across 36 suites pass, including 46 new behavioral cases and 150 new real Worker/D1 checks. These cover debit- and credit-led creation, exact USD/JPY/KWD amounts, separate dates, original IDs/CSV keys/receipt bytes, no-write previews, concurrent and idempotent requests, changed-key rejection, paged duplicate approval, owner/origin/stale guards, payment-link retention, loan principal-only and statement effects, automatic new-inflow pledge matching and JSON/ZIP receipt healing. Clean-restart D1/R2 snapshots, private preview reports, exact receipt bytes and an actual stale successful-creation retry reproduce without another entry or revision. Strict TypeScript and the production build pass. Local proxy restarts interrupted the file and migration regressions: the unchanged 102-check file suite passed on a fresh worker; the migration suite kept all 47 assertions and now uses separate Node HTTP sockets with explicit body lengths, without retries, after the race recurred on a fresh worker. Initial failures and successful verification logs are retained. No production financial records were used for testing. Hosted sign-in, responsive rendering, keyboard and visual interaction remain unverified.


## Transfer hints and persistent rejection memory

The port now adapts the original `Family::AutoTransferMatchable#transfer_hint_candidates`, `dismiss_transfer_hint!`, `RejectedTransfer` and `Transaction::Transferable#likeliest_first`. The transaction list marks likely pairs without changing either ledger row. A hint requires an available opposite movement in another active owned account, equal same-currency amounts or an exact rational foreign amount within 3%, and dates no more than two days apart. Categories must match, including two uncategorized entries. The port's existing five-day direct/inverse/profile-currency bridge is used for available manual quotes. Transfer kinds do not originate a hint. Split roots/children, report exclusions, linked fees and protected recurring-payment/goal usage backing retain the manual-match safety gates. Ledger page hints are memoized for thirty visible rows; transaction details calculate the current entry's pairs.

A user can dismiss all current hints for one transaction or reject a selected manual counterpart. The complete current revision binds the displayed decision; stale or concurrent requests fail before writes. One guarded batch writes the entire set using groups of sixteen rows within D1's parameter limit. Repeating an existing decision or dismissing an empty hint set leaves revisions unchanged. Decisions store ordered outflow/inflow root IDs and known creation/update timestamps; unknown imported timestamps remain null. They do not alter amounts, dates, classifications, receipts, imports, field locks, reconciliation flags or closed-statement review. Clearing one decision permits the pair to be suggested again. Existing decisions survive ordinary financial edits; transaction deletion cascades only the now-invalid pair memory.

Dismissed counterparts sort after all other manual candidates, while remaining explicitly matchable. As in Sure, confirming a manually chosen pair does not erase the prior decision; unlink restores standalone entries and the saved hint rejection remains. Hint lookup uses the ordered pair, while manual ordering recognizes an earlier rejection in either orientation, matching the original concern. No pending transfer record or money entry is created by a hint or dismissal.

Migration 38 adds `transfer_match_rejections` with composite owner/transaction foreign keys, ordered-pair uniqueness, cascade cleanup, canonical optional UTC clocks, revision tracking and guarded insertion/update/deletion. It changes no existing IDs, money, flags, timestamps or revisions. Known legacy restore generations 3–21 cannot destructively replace a workspace containing the new memory. Recovery 23 clears/rebuilds the complete graph and is added to all 26 existing statement/reconciliation recovery exemptions; imported review flags and manual locks remain exact. Ordinary writer 20 and automatic writer 22 remain compatible. Financial JSON/ZIP format 26 includes the new collection and fresh remapped references; formats 1–25 remain accepted without inventing historical decisions. Native Sure `RejectedTransfer` records now migrate with original root transaction references and normalized original timestamps instead of being reported as unsupported.

Automatic pending-transfer matching, unattended recurring jobs, external calendar access and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain unfinished. Bank and broker connections remain excluded. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive rendering and visual/browser QA remain unverified.

Verification for transfer hint decisions: all 861 behavioral tests and 3,895 HTTP/API checks across 37 suites pass, including 34 new behavioral cases and 86 real Worker/D1 checks. The actual pinned Ruby `Transaction::Transferable#likeliest_first` matches 600 generated ordering cases, and its hint date/tolerance constants match. The isolated schema-37 → 38 D1 upgrade passes 24 checks for unchanged old records/flags/revisions, restore guards, owner/pair/timestamp constraints, exact recovery exemptions, manual/automatic field protections and deletion cleanup. Strict TypeScript, the production build and clean-restart D1/R2 state, saved hint/candidate results, request identities and original receipt bytes pass. The file suite passed unchanged on a fresh worker after a local proxy restart during oversized-body rejection; the receipt suite passed after updating its archive assertion to the new financial format 26. Initial failures and successful verification logs are retained. No production financial records were used for testing. Hosted browser, actual sign-in, keyboard, responsive rendering and visual QA remain unverified.


## Reviewed investment-transfer classification

Open an eligible transfer into an investment or crypto account and choose whether it counts as an investment contribution. The source account must be a different account type. Review both native entries, unchanged balances, category and reporting protection, monthly spending, budget rollover, completed source statements and expense-derived goal target proposals before applying the choice. All effect pages belong to one approval; stale or concurrent changes require a fresh review.

Enabling an uncategorized contribution creates or reuses **Investment Contributions**. Disabling removes any of Sure’s sixteen supported localized default category names; custom categories remain. Only the outgoing entry’s reporting kind is protected. Existing category, merchant, tag and other protections remain exact. Automatic category rules can still run when the category was not explicitly protected. Unlinking releases reporting-kind protection and keeps other protections.

The choice changes budget spending and separately calculated expense-derived goal target proposals. It does not change saved goal targets, actual investment contribution backing, progress, money, dates, fees, FX evidence, import duplicate keys or receipts. Completed source statements need review after a reporting-kind change; category-only changes do not add that flag. Archived accounts must first be restored. Native Sure exports retain an explicit contribution flag as a protected choice; older data without a flag is not assigned an invented preference.

Schema 39 adds guarded reporting-kind protection without new tables, backfill or changes to existing records/revisions. Ordinary writers use 24, other automatic writers remain 22, this reviewed classification uses 26, and complete recovery uses 25. Older writers/restores cannot replace a protected choice. Financial JSON/ZIP format 27 preserves the protection; formats 1–26 remain accepted. Split lines retain the original six metadata protections and do not accept reporting-kind protection.

Verification: 904 behavioral tests and 4,013 HTTP/API checks across 38 suites pass, including 43 new behavioral cases and 118 classification checks. An isolated schema-38 → 39 upgrade passes 22 checks. Six hundred cases executed against the actual pinned Ruby `Transfer#update_investment_contribution!` and all sixteen supported category names match. Strict TypeScript, the production build and clean-restart D1/R2 snapshots, original receipt bytes and classification reports pass. Three legacy clients now use separate Node HTTP sockets with explicit body lengths after local proxy restart errors; their assertions are unchanged and no retry was added. Failed and successful logs are retained. No production financial records were created. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Automatic pending-transfer matching and broader parity remain in progress; bank and broker integrations remain excluded.


## Pending transfer matches and reviewed resolution

In **Transactions → Pending transfer matches**, choose all active accounts or one account, then **Review new matches**. The scan adapts Sure’s `Family::AutoTransferMatchable#auto_match_transfers!`: entries have opposite amounts in different active owned accounts, a four-day inclusive date window, and no saved ordered rejection. Same-currency amounts must be exactly equal and opposite. Cross-currency guesses between these manual accounts require a previously confirmed transfer in the same account direction and an eligible dated manual quote within 10%. Exact same-currency pairs come first, followed by nearest dates. Deterministic ID ordering resolves source ties. Each entry can be claimed once; pending associations do not establish a confirmed foreign route. Categories need not agree on this automatic path. The port retains its existing five-day direct/inverse/profile-currency quote lookup; no bank or broker provider is involved.

Review shows both original native entries, reporting kinds, category, applied ratio, all affected current balances, principal-only loan assumptions and completed statements. Approval saves pending associations for the complete proposal set, including other review pages. No payment, fee, CSV duplicate identity or receipt is created. The original kinds and categories remain until confirmation, so pending standard entries remain regular transactions and retain their income/expense and budget reporting. They remain eligible for filtered CSV export. A pending association can still affect principal-only loan replay, matching Sure’s link-based source accounting. Completed statements are explicitly marked for review after link/resolution changes.

Select one or many pending matches, including across pages, to **confirm**, **reject** or **release** after review. Confirmation derives outgoing reporting from the destination account, sets the incoming entry to funds movement, and supplies the Investment Contributions category only when needed. It preserves custom categories and existing protections without adding manual category or kind locks. Rejection retains both original standalone entries and saves the ordered rejection decision. Release retains the entries without adding rejection memory. Pending imported transfer kinds and their protections survive rejection/release; legacy standalone funds/payment kinds are accepted in financial recovery, while creation commands and CSV import continue to require valid paired movements. Filtered CSV excludes kinds its importer cannot accept. Confirmed transfers keep the existing unlink behavior.

Pending matches appear on both transaction rows and in their details. Confirm or release a pending match before adding a new recurring-payment claim; automatic recurring matching also skips pending sources. Existing imported payment history remains recoverable. Deleting one pending entry releases its association and retains the other original entry, import key and receipts. Confirmed transfer deletion still removes the complete group and its linked fees.

Schema 40 adds nullable status and original creation/update timestamps to transfer details, without new tables, backfill or changes to old money/revisions. Null status keeps existing transfers confirmed. Native Sure pending records and known original clocks now migrate instead of being skipped. Financial JSON/ZIP format 28 retains status, timestamps, both entries and receipt graph with fresh identities; formats 1–27 remain accepted. Older restores cannot discard new transfer metadata, and older writers cannot resolve, delete or financially alter pending entries. Ordinary writers use 28, other automatic workflows remain 22, reviewed contribution choice uses 26, pending matching/resolution uses 32 and complete recovery uses 29. Recovery preserves imported statement flags and existing manual protections exactly.

The bounded scan rejects more than one million candidate comparisons or 100,000 possible pairs before any write. Narrow to one account when needed. This foreground workflow uses explicit all-page review; the source’s global automatic-matching enable/disable preference and pending cleanup, unattended scan triggers and configurable operator FX tolerance remain outstanding. No cloud schedule is configured. Broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity and external calendar access remain in progress. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

Verification: 943 behavioral tests and 4,119 HTTP/API checks across 39 suites pass, including 39 new behavioral cases and 106 pending-transfer checks. The isolated schema-39 → 40 upgrade passes 31 checks for unchanged old records/revisions, guarded metadata and dates, old-writer/restore rejection, manual versus automatic protections and recovery exemptions. Six hundred cases execute the actual pinned Ruby greedy matcher and `Transfer#confirm!`, alongside independently constructed exact-amount/date predicates and asserted original automatic scope flags; foreign routes and tolerances are covered separately by behavioral/API checks. Strict TypeScript, the production build and clean-restart D1/R2 records, native clocks, original receipt bytes, pending/confirmation reports and IDs pass. The two file suites passed their unchanged assertions on a fresh local Worker after development-proxy restart errors. Failure and successful logs are retained. No production financial records were used. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified.


## Automatic transfer matching preference

The pending-matches section now provides a persistent, owner-scoped matching preference. **Review turning matching off** previews releasing every pending pair whose two entries still have standard reporting kinds. Existing preclassified pending pairs, confirmed transfers, all original entries, native amounts/dates, categories, protections, CSV keys, receipt bytes/links and prior rejection decisions remain. Cleanup adds no rejection memory. It includes standard pairs in archived accounts, as in Sure’s family-wide cleanup. Review shows all affected balances, principal-only loan effects and completed statements before approval.

**Review turning matching on** proposes an immediate scan across all accounts. Approving the complete review saves the enabled preference and pending associations atomically; reporting still waits for confirmation. Repeating the current on/off setting is an exact no-op. In particular, repeating off does not remove standard pending records imported while already off. Off blocks new automatic scans, but preserves manual matching, hints and confirmation/rejection/release of existing pending records. Matching here runs through explicit foreground review; unattended scan triggers and configurable operator FX tolerance remain pending.

The port adapts the actual `AutoMatchesController#update_settings` and `cleanup_pending_auto_matches!` predicates. The source queues a scan when re-enabled; this foreground port binds that scan and setting change to one owner/revision/date-bound all-page receipt. Cleanup uses ninety-ID batches within D1’s parameter limit, and the entire preference/cleanup batch rolls back on failure. Review pages show twenty-five matches; approval covers every page. A forced failure in the last chunk of a 231-match API test preserves the prior preference, entries, associations, flags and revision exactly.

Schema 41 adds nullable `profiles.auto_transfer_disabled` without new tables, historical updates or revision changes. Null/absent uses Sure’s enabled default, without inventing a saved preference. Explicit enabled/disabled choices recover in financial JSON/ZIP format 29; formats 1–28 remain accepted. Restore retains imported pending records without scanning or cleanup, including when the restored preference is off. A recovery preview discloses a matching on/off change and requires acknowledgement. The pinned Sure export omits the family preference, so native import uses the enabled default. Older restore writers cannot discard a saved preference, and a database guard blocks older match writers from creating pending records while off. Ordinary writers remain 28, other automatic workflows 22 and reviewed contribution choice 26; pending matching/settings use 34 and complete recovery uses 33. All twenty-six statement/reconciliation recovery exemptions include the new recovery generation.

Verification: all 1,003 behavioral tests and 4,214 HTTP/API checks across 40 suites pass, including 60 new behavioral cases and 95 settings checks. Six hundred cases execute the actual pinned Ruby controller and cleanup against all four setting transitions, pending/confirmed records and both leg kinds. The isolated schema-40 → 41 upgrade passes 27 checks for unchanged legacy state, nullable defaults, old-writer/restore/disabled-scan protection, value/owner isolation and exact recovery flags/protections. Strict TypeScript, the final production build and clean-restart D1/R2 snapshots, saved preference and pending reviews, original receipt bytes and graph references pass. Initial harness coverage and a corrected invalid cleared-entry timestamp fixture remain documented in local verification logs; no financial assertions were weakened. No production financial records were used. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Unattended jobs, external calendar access and wider household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain in progress. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.


## Reviewed historical investment transfer repair

Portfolio now provides **Review older investment transfer reporting**. The owner may review all investment/crypto destinations or one destination, including archived accounts. The maintenance scan follows Sure’s actual `Transfer::InvestmentKindReconciler`: it checks existing associations into those account types, repairs only transfer-reporting kinds, and skips standard/one-time choices, excluded entries and protected kinds. It does not use the broad user-modified flag as a reason to skip. Preclassified pending legs are eligible without confirming their association or enabling automatic matching; standard pending kinds remain untouched. Categories are retained exactly, including default contribution categories, and the repair creates no categories or protections.

The complete review shows native entries and dates, before/after reporting kinds, skip/transition counts, pending/confirmed status, account balances, native monthly income/expenses, combined budget rollover, actual goal backing and separately recalculated expense-derived target proposals. Saved goal targets, allocations, checkpoints and progress bases stay unchanged. Combined report effects compare the whole proposed workspace, rather than adding independent per-row estimates. Change and effect pages contain twenty-five rows; one owner/revision/date-bound receipt covers every page. A stale or concurrent approval cannot partially apply another proposal. A second run is a no-op.

Original transaction IDs, import duplicate keys, names, notes, dates, amounts, categories, field protections, FX snapshots, transfer request identities/status/timestamps, fee expenses and receipt links/bytes remain. Financial recovery now also accepts legitimate confirmed associations retaining standard/one-time kinds or legacy kind drift; physical pairing, opposing signs, account separation, exact native snapshots and currency invariants remain mandatory. Native Sure import keeps its existing source importer normalization for confirmed transfers and retains preclassified pending rows for explicit review.

Schema 42 changes only repair guards, without backfill, new tables or changes to existing rows/revisions. Controlled writer 36 can change only eligible reporting kinds: the database independently protects excluded/chosen/protected rows and the destination-derived expected kind, and rejects changes to money, dates, categories, notes, ownership, links, protections or reconciliation fields. This narrow path preserves cleared/reconciled status, timestamps and statement references, while completed statements for changed account/date ranges require review. Ordinary financial edits retain their existing reconciliation-reset behavior. Workspace revision invalidates cached reporting, adapting the original repair’s transaction/entry clock updates. Ordinary writers remain 28, other automatic workflows 22, reviewed contribution choice 26, matching/settings 34 and complete recovery 33. Financial JSON/ZIP format 29 is unchanged.

Verification: 1,069 behavioral tests and 4,290 API checks across 41 suites pass, including 66 new behavioral cases and 76 real repair checks. The actual pinned Ruby class matches 1,636 cases, including destination/kind constants, per-leg exclusions/protections, dry-run/commit equivalence, cache invalidation and idempotence. The isolated schema-41 → 42 upgrade passes 23 checks for unchanged legacy state, the kind-only database footprint, exact reconciliation retention, ordinary reset compatibility and disabled/preclassified pending repair. A forced failure in the later 90-row write batch rolls back earlier changes. Strict TypeScript, the final production build and clean-restart D1/R2 snapshots, complete repair reviews and original receipt bytes pass. No production financial records were used.

The core suite repeatedly encountered Wrangler’s development HTTP proxy restart error after early rejection responses. Its 54 unchanged assertions now run through the pinned Wrangler/Miniflare SDK against the actual built workerd modules, without retries. A subsequent HTTP-worker restart reads the same persisted records and validates all saved snapshots. Other suites retain their HTTP checks. Failure and successful verification logs are retained. After building and applying local migrations, `npm run test:api` starts and disposes this direct local runtime; it does not need a separately running HTTP server. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Operator FX tolerance, unattended jobs, external calendar access and broader household/assistant/extraction/investment parity remain in progress. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.


## Guided transaction CSV mapping

Transaction imports now adapt Sure’s configurable separator/date/number/sign concepts to a foreground, reviewed Sites import. Select the target account, map arbitrary header columns by position, and explicitly choose ISO, month/day/year or day/month/year dates; comma/semicolon/tab/pipe separators; decimal-point, decimal-comma, space-grouped decimal-comma or comma-grouped integer numbers; signed amount direction or separate debit/credit columns. Optional mappings retain existing category/merchant/tag/notes/reporting/exclusion/account/currency/reconciliation validation. The preview covers every row in twenty-five-row pages with expandable metadata, and changes invalidate it. It shows parsed inputs before automatic rules. Server-side parsing, account opening-date checks, named metadata resolution and automatic rules remain mandatory and atomic.

Grouping is validated before separators are removed; malformed/mixed groups, symbols, short/impossible dates, excess currency precision, duplicate/missing/out-of-range/reused mappings and non-zero values on both debit/credit sides reject the file. No rounding or date guessing occurs. Only mapped columns are read. Headers must be unique, with at most 100 columns for configured imports; files remain limited to 500 rows/1 MB and one account. The existing fixed-column API remains compatible when csvOptions is omitted. General CSV parsing defaults to comma, preserving investment, rates and other import callers.

No new table, writer generation, historical update or backup format is required: schema 42 and financial JSON/ZIP format 29 remain. Normalized original fields retain existing occurrence-sensitive CSV keys; changing layout/date/number/sign format does not create duplicates, and automatic renaming does not lose retry protection. Late insert failures roll back earlier rows and revision. Owner isolation, same-origin protections, named metadata ownership and post-save savings hooks remain.

Verification adds 67 behavioral tests and 75 real mapped-import API checks. All 1,136 behavioral tests pass. Full regression and restart evidence is retained in the local publication report. Initial API fixtures incorrectly changed a reporting kind while asserting identical fingerprints, and expected a conflict response for a deliberately injected raw D1 failure; corrected fixtures preserve identical canonical input and assert the existing generic 500 response plus exact no-op state. No product validation or atomicity assertions were weakened. No production financial records were used. Hosted sign-in and browser/visual QA remain unverified. Reusable import templates, preamble handling, staged/large files, account mapping/merge, ZIP recovery extraction, PDF/XLSX extraction, household/assistant parity, unattended work and broader investment parity remain pending. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.


## Direct ZIP import and resumable original recovery

Settings now accepts the original Sites backup ZIP or Sure v2 ZIP without manual extraction. `lib/zip-read.ts` reads random-access Blob slices, validates central/local records and non-overlap, and bounds directory, entry count, compressed/expanded bytes and each selected extraction. STORE/raw DEFLATE, signed/unsigned data descriptors, bounded ZIP64 entry size/offset fields and ZIP64 end records are supported. The actual Rubyzip 3.4 OutputStream used by the pinned source writes ZIP64 size extras even for small exports; the retained real-library fixture verifies that layout and exact native NDJSON. The fixture’s SHA-256 is `f587e26eb2eb0a91907f21ff570e97f375faeefa007302dcd376ae7a3cc24e3f`; its original NDJSON hash is `b3e3bb039080539527410c77513357c7bbe82395a61794aa7660d1085ab327fe`. Rubyzip was installed only under a temporary test directory, with no application dependency or global change.

The bounded reader follows the [PKWARE ZIP format](https://pkware.cachefly.net/webdocs/casestudies/APPNOTE.TXT) and [Compression Standard](https://compression.spec.whatwg.org/). Raw compressed input is fed in at most 16 KiB slices; output size and CRC are checked during consumption. The archive never becomes one in-memory array or one server upload. Limits are 512 MiB compressed/expanded, 2 MiB directory, 2,000 entries, 8,000,000-byte financial content, 500 originals/500 MiB total originals, 25 MiB per statement original and 10 MiB per image. Selected originals are validated sequentially by size, CRC, SHA-256 and the existing content/type validators before financial preview. ZIP64 values must remain safely representable and inside these limits. Encryption, multiple volumes, symlinks, unsafe paths, duplicates, overlapping/prefixed/trailing records and malformed structures reject.

For Sites archives, manifest format/version, every metadata record and exact ready-file path must match the financial JSON. Unknown files reject; unavailable records have null paths and remain disclosed.
