For Sure v2, exactly one all.ndjson and its matching version.txt are required; the existing finance adapter still validates the full export and excludes bank/provider data. Native attachment metadata remains a separate parity gap. A single wrapping directory is accepted. Unsupported browser decompression provides an extraction fallback; hosted browser support remains unverified.

Financial restore retains its existing atomic revision/receipt-bound replacement, warning acknowledgement and typed confirmation. Original recovery follows separately and is resumable without another financial replacement. The new private originals endpoint authenticates each owner, locates only an existing checksum-matching file record, revalidates actual bytes and applies a revision-guarded pending reservation. It preserves file IDs, names, statement/receipt links, content metadata and creation times. Availability checks verify private R2 metadata without mutating records, skip valid existing originals, and detect missing objects behind ready rows. In-flight/deleting records block. Shared upload finalization retains attempt/key guards, failure cleanup evidence and retry semantics. No new table, migration, writer generation or backup format is introduced; schema 42 and format 29 remain.

Verification adds 45 behavioral cases and 75 actual archive API checks. All 1,181 behavioral cases pass. The archive suite exports real synthetic D1/R2 records, parses the streamed ZIP, restores to another owner, interrupts after one original, resumes without repeating finances, checks exact original bytes/links/IDs, retries a forced ready-finalization failure, heals a missing object, rejects cross-owner and malformed payloads, invalidates stale financial reviews and exports the recovered graph again. Native compressed ZIP input uses the pinned real-library fixture. Full regression and clean-restart snapshots/original bytes are retained in the local publication report. Initial typecheck fixes covered helper checksum scope, overlapping browser/Worker stream types and unknown JSON response types; no product validation was relaxed. No production financial records were used. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Staged/large finance files, reusable templates, account mapping/merge, native attachment-manifest conversion, PDF/XLSX extraction, household/assistant parity, unattended work and broader investment parity remain pending. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.


The final regression run encountered Wrangler’s development HTTP proxy restart error on early rejected-body requests in the migration and file suites. Their unchanged 47 and 102 assertions pass directly against the built workerd modules, using the same pinned Wrangler/Miniflare runtime and persisted D1/R2 bindings as the core and new import suites. No failed requests are retried and no rejection/status assertions are relaxed. Original proxy-failure logs and successful direct results are retained. The remaining suites pass through loopback HTTP. `npm run test:migration-api` and `npm run test:files-api` now use that direct local transport after a build and local migrations.


## Scoped investment flow reporting and signed reversals

Portfolio now exposes an owner-scoped, read-only monthly flow report and exact CSV. It adapts the pinned `Portfolio::FlowClassifier`: label precedence, signed income/fee reversals, internal buy/sell/reinvestment/sweep rules, cash transfer resolution by actual counterpart scope, and security Transfer evidence from opposite quantity on the same date/security in another scoped account. All report amounts use the recorded native currency. Cash and recorded security values have distinct gross/net totals; they are never added together or advertised as investment returns. Selected security values use stored native unit prices, not inferred transferred cost.

The normalized classifier handles every pinned label, exclusions, source pending-cash precedence and fee priority. The current-state adapter uses persisted Trade activities and Transaction kinds. Native v2 omits investment labels on transactions and trade classifications; unknown/generic rows retain the existing internal trade fallback, with a warning. The adapter maps actual `transferFees` associations, because the port’s principal `transferId` is not the source’s fee-only foreign key. Standard/one-time auto-match pending associations retain their kinds and signed cash fallback; preclassified transfer kinds resolve by scope. Those associations are different from source pending bank-extra flags, and no bank integration or pending bank metadata is introduced. Cash counterparts need only belong to scope; their excluded status does not change the source rule. Security counterparts are restricted by the actual date/security/opposite-quantity/account predicates. The current model has no per-trade exclusion field; that and richer transaction activity labels remain parity gaps.

Default scope is all non-excluded investment/crypto accounts in one native currency, including archived accounts. Selecting an account explicitly includes its own history, including a report-excluded account. Non-investment, wrong-currency, foreign-owner, malformed, future, duplicate or unknown options reject. Totals cover the full scope before the row filter and twenty-five-row pagination. The endpoint is authenticated/private and makes no writes; optional revision guards bind CSV or JSON reads to the loaded workspace. Integer/BigInt sums fail clearly on unsupported aggregate values. CSV uses exact minor-unit numbers and formula-safe text, with complete rows rather than a page. Balance/holding observations are disclosed but not inferred deposits; snapshots, splits and opening balances are not flow events.

The shared trade validator now permits signed dividend/interest charges and fee rebates, as Sure’s classifier and tests require. The activity form separates positive magnitude from money-in/out direction. Foreign conversion snapshots, manual edits, CSV import/export, original duplicate keys and JSON/ZIP format 29 preserve those signs. Nonzero cash, zero share/price/trade-fee cash shapes, quantity direction for actual buys/sells/movements and exact conversion evidence remain mandatory. This is a validation correction with no table, migration, writer generation or backup shape change; schema 42 and financial format 29 remain. Ordinary investment cash/balance effects and statement review hooks continue through the existing mutation path.

The source `Holding::LotBook` deliberately leaves inbound transferred cost unknown and tracks movement lots separately from its priced pool. The port retains that behavior. Automatic basis carry was incorrectly listed as an upstream parity requirement earlier; it is not supplied by the pinned source. The original native export also omits the labels needed to reconstruct imported activity automatically. Full return/performance analysis, broader activity/exclusion controls, per-account sharing, assistant and unattended workflows remain pending.

Verification: all 1,218 behavioral cases pass, including 37 new cases, plus 65 real flow API checks. A 4,032-case oracle executes the actual pinned Ruby class across labels, entry types, signs, kinds, fee priority, exclusions, pending flags and both scopes; only its two ActiveRecord lookup methods return fixture membership. It does not claim SQL execution parity. Adapter tests separately verify actual state/index predicates, native currency boundaries, fee relations, archived/excluded scope, original-kind pending semantics, unclassified/observation warnings, complete page/CSV totals, range/overflow errors and unchanged basis/state. The real API suite checks no-write reports, signed FX evidence, CSV reversals, duplicate/unknown options, cross-owner isolation, stale revisions, balances and version-29 recovery. Initial harness fixes supplied Ruby’s ActiveSupport presence predicates, an API boolean fixture and the exporter’s existing decimal formatting; no product assertion was weakened. Full regression and restart snapshots/reports/CSV are retained in the local publication report. No production financial records were used. Hosted sign-in and browser/visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.


## Investment cash labels and trade exclusions

The current model now preserves explicit cash investment activity labels and trade reporting exclusions. The prior flow-report section’s data-control gaps are superseded by this update. Cash labels use the thirteen literal values in the pinned Transaction::ACTIVITY_LABELS; the flow classifier consumes them with the original precedence and actual fee/counterpart rules. Trade exclusions suppress the row and its security-counterpart eligibility in flows and remove its disposal from realized/unknown-disposal summary totals. The physical holding/cost book, cash balances and detailed disposal history continue to include the trade, matching Holding::PortfolioCache and Balance::SyncCache, which do not filter excluded entries. No amounts, quantities, dates, FX evidence, import keys, receipt links or statement reconciliation fields are changed by reporting edits.

Owner/revision-bound reporting controls are available on flow rows, including archived history. Standard transaction and trade editors accept the fields; compatible commands that omit them preserve saved values. Cash labels can also be supplied by plain/mapped transaction CSV; trade CSV preserves explicit true/false exclusions. Economic fingerprints are unchanged, so repeating a file does not create duplicates or overwrite reporting choices. Exclusion validation now precedes the investment importer’s fast duplicate skip. Extended native export fields are preserved when present; the actual pinned v2 serializer omits them, so absent metadata is not inferred.

Schema 43 adds only two nullable columns, no backfill or tables. Legacy NULL is omitted from API state, preserving old snapshots and behavior; explicit trade 0/1 and cash labels survive. Four database triggers validate insert/update values. The historical repair writer 36 may still change only its eligible kind: its footprint now protects the new cash-label column too. Recovery moves from 33 to 37, with all twenty-six statement/reconciliation exemptions and both off-state pending-association exemptions extended. The owner-scoped old-restore guard rejects older recovery/unversioned generations when an explicit label or include/exclude choice exists. Ordinary investment/manual/automatic workflows retain their existing even generations and preserve metadata on column-limited updates.

Financial JSON/ZIP format 30 serializes the new optional data; all formats 1–30 are accepted. A complete restore remaps entity IDs, retains reporting values and all existing graph links, and preserves the reviewRequired value actually saved in the backup. It does not clear flags from later real financial changes. Metadata-only actions and unchanged financial form fields do not reset reconciliation or flag completed statements. Ordinary amount/date/kind changes retain their existing reset/drift rules. Explicitly clearing a cash label returns to NULL; an explicitly saved false trade exclusion remains 0. Repeating a saved value is a no-op, while choosing false for a legacy NULL trade records that explicit choice.

Verification adds 22 behavioral cases and 62 API checks; 47 isolated D1 checks prove unchanged legacy rows/revisions, nullable defaults, every cash label, invalid raw values, older restore rejection, repair footprint protection, preserved reconciliation/protection/statement evidence, disabled pending recovery and ordinary-edit compatibility. API tests cover owner/origin/stale checks, native footprint and lot preservation, archived/security counterpart behavior, legacy form commands, CSV metadata and duplicate identity, malformed repeated-file rejection, forced last-write rollback and full recovery. Future-version rejection assertions now use format 31; format 30 is valid. Initial harness corrections handled a split-export type, a pre-existing statement review flag after financial CSV writes and protocol-version expectations. No financial assertions were weakened. Full regression, typed checks, the production build, source comparison and clean-restart metadata/report evidence are retained in the local publication report. Broader performance/returns, household/assistant, large/staged imports and unattended work remain pending. Hosted UI verification is still outstanding. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.


During the final regression, the receipt suite’s unauthenticated DELETE rejection encountered Wrangler’s known development-proxy restart response. Its unchanged 119 assertions pass directly against the built workerd modules through the pinned Wrangler/Miniflare SDK, as do the core, migration, file and newer import/report suites. The receipt command now uses that direct local transport. Failure and successful logs are retained; no request retries or relaxed rejection assertions are used.


## Active-source period market change

The private portfolio performance report ports the pinned Balance::BaseCalculator#flows_for_date and #market_value_change_on_date identity and InvestmentStatement#period_return_trend denominator. Calendar-day holding differences telescope to closing minus pre-period holdings; adding actual signed cash of nonzero-share trades gives the source’s recorded market change without repeatedly replaying every calendar date. Zero-share income/fee cash is excluded from this measure. Embedded purchase/sale fees remain in actual cash. No trade notional, external security valuation, inferred transferred cost or contribution checkpoint substitutes for execution cash. Physical balances and market changes include excluded entries, as the source’s SyncCache does. Separate reported cash income/fees/external cash follow the existing scoped flow classifier and its exclusions.

The report has one native-currency scope, with archived history and explicit excluded-account selection, and omits accounts not yet open by the ending date. Opening values are strictly before the first requested day, including first-day trades only in the period. Source Trend percentages divide market change by the magnitude of pre-period total value and round symmetrically to one decimal. Zero baseline has no percentage. History issues conservatively withhold the displayed percentage for in-period account openings, holding observations, security transfers/sweeps/reinvestment, nonzero-share zero-cash entries and generic imported activity. The unqualified source percentage is retained separately for inspection rather than silently presenting those residuals as reliable performance. Cash/total observations are counted without turning them into market profit. Quotes carry forward with endpoint share/value/price/date and dated foreign-rate evidence. Missing required prices/FX or unsafe money aggregates reject. Unknown cost basis does not itself prevent a value-based market-change calculation.

Owner-authenticated, no-store GET /api/portfolio-performance validates unique strict options, owner account scope and optional revision guards. Complete account totals do not depend on twenty-five-row pagination; CSV exports all account rows with exact integer minor units, formula-safe names and review reasons. Portfolio exposes a monthly report with scope selection, calculation notes and quote/history evidence. The read-only feature adds no schema, tables, writer generation or backup fields: schema 43 and financial JSON/ZIP format 30 remain. The endpoint also supports arbitrary valid past date ranges.

This ports the active source measure, not a new total-return methodology. Portfolio::Xirr exists in the pinned source but has no active call site; its future UI and a broader reliable total-return analysis remain outside this milestone. The prior performance gap is now narrower, not closed. No bank or broker integration is introduced.

Verification adds 19 behavioral cases and a 605-case comparison executing the actual pinned Ruby daily flow/market-change methods and Trend percentage method. Only holding lookup, date/entry fixtures and framework construction boundaries are replaced; this is not a claim of SQL or Rails end-to-end equivalence. The comparison covers daily telescoping, cash signs, zero-share exclusion, negative/zero baselines and symmetric percentage rounding. Behavioral tests cover actual holdings, first-day buys and sale fees, quote chronology, split conservation, dated FX/missing FX, observations, movement uncertainty, exclusions, native scopes, archived accounts, complete safe CSV, paging and range/overflow errors. All 1,259 behavioral tests and 4,652 API checks across 46 suites pass, including 85 new performance API checks. Clean-restart D1/R2 state, original bytes, complete report pages and exact CSV are verified; the production build and strict TypeScript checks pass. Regression and restart evidence are recorded in the publication report. Initial fixture corrections fixed an 11-share closing-value expectation, immutable FX cache use, the existing integer-overflow wording, the securityPrice action name, integer holding flags and the required restore-before-edit rule for archived accounts. Typed fixture checks also supplied required FX notes and kept unowned backup DTOs separate from owner-scoped FinanceState. Source negative-zero percentage rounding is preserved. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual verification remain outstanding.


## Last completed transaction CSV configuration

The pinned Import#suggested_template selects the latest completed import for the same account and import type; Import#template_attributes copies its column labels and formatting, and the configuration page offers an explicit apply action. Transaction CSV now ports that behavior with one last-completed configuration per owner/account. The saved v1 mapping stores column labels and the port’s supported date/number/sign/amount-mode/separator settings. Applying it resolves trimmed, case-insensitive header names rather than old offsets, so reordered columns work. Missing saved columns or duplicate headers reject instead of silently guessing. Existing row validation and full paged review still run before financial import. Applying settings itself writes nothing and clears the previous review. Generic/header inference remains available when no saved template exists. This milestone covers transaction CSV. Investment CSV template reuse remains pending.

Schema 44 adds csv_import_templates, the thirty-ninth user table, with a unique owner/account key, a composite account foreign key with cascading deletion and a bounded valid-JSON constraint. There is no legacy backfill, FinanceState field, financial backup change or writer-generation change. Only supported header labels/settings are saved; transaction rows, imported filenames and amounts are not copied into the mapping record. Normal CSV imports append the template upsert to the same guarded financial transaction as new rows and rule results. A late template failure rolls everything back. Duplicate-only imports update the last completed mapping in a guarded batch without changing the financial revision or ledger. Failed validation and failed saves preserve the previous completed mapping. The existing economic fingerprints and duplicate behavior are unchanged.

Private GET /api/csv-template validates exact account options and owner membership and returns only that account’s mapping; it never reads another owner’s preference. The UI fetch is abortable on account changes and only applies settings through an explicit button. Financial JSON/ZIP remains format 30 and restore generation 37. Import-history templates are absent from the pinned native financial export; the port likewise keeps these preferences outside financial replacement files. Deleting or replacing the account cascades the old preference, including when recovery remaps IDs. The form documents this separation. CSV size/row limits remain 1 MB/500; large staged imports and broader mapping remain pending. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded. Hosted interaction and visual verification remain outstanding.

Verification adds 13 behavioral cases, 60 real API checks and 13 isolated schema-43 → 44 checks. New cases cover saved label/settings shape without row content, reordered/case-equivalent columns, missing and duplicate headers, split debit/credit formats, optional income labels, malformed/bounded settings, immutable application, owner/origin boundaries, null initial preferences, duplicate-only updates without financial revisions, failed validation, forced late-write rollback of finances and settings, account/type separation, plain canonical imports, financial recovery remapping and account deletion cascades. The isolated upgrade preserves existing graph rows/revisions, starts empty, enforces composite ownership, uniqueness and JSON bounds, retains unrelated owners and is idempotent. No assertions were weakened and no failed requests were retried. Full regression and clean-restart mapping/state evidence are retained in the publication report.

All 1,272 behavioral cases and 4,712 API checks across 47 suites pass, including the 60 new mapping checks; strict TypeScript and production builds pass. Tests use synthetic local D1/R2 records. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual checks remain unverified.


## Durable larger CSV staging and whole-file commit

The pinned Import permits 10 MiB/10,000 rows and TransactionImport#import! wraps the entire file in one database transaction. The port now provides durable private staging at those limits rather than committing independently recoverable financial chunks. Client UTF-8 validation and local converted-row review accept the larger limits explicitly. Files beyond the compatible 500-row/1 MB direct path upload into private R2 through an owner/request-bound reservation. Size, SHA-256, encoding and immutable mapping evidence are checked. Upload retries require identical bytes. A separate recovery action verifies an already-saved R2 source after a lost finalization acknowledgment, avoiding a needless re-upload. Sources remain owner-downloadable with their exact bytes and safe attachment filenames.

Private /api/csv-imports supports reservations, verified raw uploads, bounded review pages, whole-file approval, source download and resumable source/history cleanup. At most ten records per owner bound total storage, including completed history. The limit is enforced by a D1 trigger. Metadata is separate from financial state and backup files. Records deliberately retain original account IDs without cascading away their private storage keys; an account removed by financial replacement leaves an owned cleanup record. No remapped/replacement account is silently selected. Fresh reviews require the original active account. Source/history deletion retains imported financial records and uses a durable deleting marker so interruption after object deletion is retryable. Full import reversal remains separate parity work.

Schema 45 adds csv_imports, the fortieth user table, plus quota/account reservation and commit-precondition triggers. A csv-prefixed workspace guard asserts that the owned staged record still exists and is ready inside the same transaction as all financial writes; concurrent deletion or already-completed status rejects before money changes. The common mutation helper now supports that explicit guard key and a no-financial-revision variant for duplicate-only completion. Ordinary callers retain their existing default behavior. New financial rows use packed five-row inserts within D1’s 100-bind-parameter limit. Automatic rule changes, split children, last-completed mapping and the complete status/counts commit together. A failed final status write rolls everything back. A lost success response or concurrent approval returns the completed record without replaying finances. Pending-savings checks follow the ordinary import behavior and disclose a required manual check if they cannot finish.

Duplicate keys keep the existing full-file canonical date/name/amount/notes/kind occurrence index. Repeated identical rows across the old 500-row boundary receive distinct original identities; saved earlier occurrences skip without collapsing the rest. All row dates, amounts, currencies, kinds and named references validate before any financial write, including duplicate rows. Automatic rules apply only to new rows. Review receipts bind owner, draft, source checksum, immutable options, workspace revision and current day; changed state requires fresh review. Input rows and automatic rule summary/effect counts are available before approval, with twenty-five-row paging and complete totals. Financial JSON/ZIP remains format 30, restore generation 37, and existing writer generations remain unchanged. Staging is foreground work; no unattended job is installed. Existing workspace/restore bounds remain as documented. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

The CSV parser now retains contiguous spans and escaped-quote pieces instead of building a string node for every character. This is required to keep large unused fields economical before mapping discards them. A fixture preserves the actual version-48 parser from commit 5addef276bbd072999381516c05b46f9a48304f5. Eight thousand fuzz cases compare result/error behavior across all four separators; additional tests cover multiline/escaped quotes, million-character unused fields, legacy row limits, 10,000 rows, canonical occurrence identities and owner/account/reference constraints. New checks include ten behavioral cases, 103 API checks and eighteen isolated schema-44 → 45 checks. The real workerd/D1/R2 suite uploads an exact 10 MiB, 10,000-row file, skips 500 prior occurrences, atomically retains the other 9,500, verifies exact original bytes, concurrent/lost-response completion, recovered upload acknowledgment, stale review rejection, forced last-write rollback, interrupted cleanup, quotas, removed-account drafts, private boundaries, strict options and actual automatic split-rule persistence. Tests use synthetic local data only. Full regression and restart evidence are recorded in the publication report. Hosted interactive sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified.

All 1,282 behavioral tests and 4,815 API checks across 48 suites pass. Eighteen isolated upgrade checks pass; strict TypeScript and production builds pass. Exact source bytes, ready review pages, completed status, rule split children and existing financial/file snapshots are verified after restarting the local Worker. No production financial records are used.


## Reviewed CSV import reversal

The pinned Import#revert destroys its import-owned entries in a transaction, retains rows that belonged to earlier imports/manual history and returns the configured file to pending for another publish. Transaction::Transferable destroys the transfer join when a principal is removed; Transfer#release_transactions releases surviving principal kinds/protection and request identity, and dependent fee transactions are destroyed. The port now records new staged import ownership and provides a whole-import reviewed reversal with the same financial ownership boundary. All new UI transaction CSV imports use staging, including small files. The legacy direct API remains compatible and untracked. Legacy completed records without ownership are explicitly unavailable for automatic reversal; no IDs are inferred from source CSV or economic fingerprints.

Schema 46 uses additive columns on csv_imports and preserves its existing quota/account/commit triggers. Nullable reversal tracking leaves old rows unchanged. A new owner-scoped csv_import_entries table records each newly created root exactly once, with composite import/transaction foreign keys and cascading metadata cleanup. Rows skipped as previous imports are never claimed. New completions atomically insert ownership and set the tracked flag; a completion trigger requires the live owned count to equal the created-row count before a tracked completion can land. Existing ready uploads acquire exact ownership when completed by this writer. There is no backfill, new financial backup shape or writer generation. The user-table count is 41; financial format 30 and restore generation 37 remain. Ownership is import history outside financial backups, so replacement/remapped financial IDs lose that linkage. Deleting source/history drops ownership metadata without deleting financial records.

Reversal prepares the entire current graph from recorded IDs, including later edits and moved/archived history. Imported principal deletion removes its transfer join and dependent fees, releases only surviving principal links, resets confirmed transfer kinds to standard and releases their kind lock, while pending kinds/locks remain. The surviving native amounts, dates, categories, original import keys, receipts and unrelated transfer quotes remain. Reversing an imported fee without deleting a principal removes its fee association and clears only that parent fee total; the parent transfer/principals remain. Splits and receipt associations on removed roots cascade. Bill allocations, goal usage and recurring-price evidence are retained with ledger links nulled, and private original files remain. Rejection links involving deleted entries cascade; other choices, rule logs, labels/categories/tags/merchants, saved mapping preferences and goal settings are retained.

The review exposes all deletions and dependent-fee reasons, surviving entries, current account balances, completed-statement flags and complete budget/goal effects. Deletions, survivors and effects have independent twenty-five-row pages bound to one complete receipt. Ownership enumeration is normalized so query ordering cannot change the report or receipt. The private owner/origin-bound reversal action requires the reviewed revision/day/receipt and typed REVERT. A dedicated csv-revert workspace guard asserts a still-completed tracked import inside the same transaction as all financial changes and the ready/reset marker. The original private CSV and immutable mapping remain, allowing fresh review/re-import. Duplicate-only or already-removed ownership reversals change metadata without a financial revision. A durable last-reversal receipt makes retry safe after a lost response or concurrent approval and prevents an old reversal request from undoing a later re-import.

Verification adds eleven behavioral cases, 77 dedicated reversal API checks and eleven additional staged-import checks for larger reversal/re-import behavior. The actual 10 MiB/10,000-row test reverses exactly its 9,500 created occurrences while retaining the 500 prior matches, re-imports those 9,500, and proves an old reversal receipt does not delete the new import. Forty-eight cases execute the actual pinned Ruby Transfer#release_transactions with persistence/association fixtures and compare surviving kinds/locks; this does not claim full Rails SQL equivalence. Nineteen isolated schema-45 → 46 checks prove unchanged legacy financial/import rows, nullable defaults, all original staging triggers, composite ownership/uniqueness, complete-count guards, reverse-state guards, cascades, quotas and idempotent application. API graph fixtures use real public split/payment/goal/receipt/reconciliation paths and current D1 transfer constraints. Initial fixtures were corrected to include native payment conversion, allowed link-mode fields, cleared statement transactions and typed statement defaults; no assertion was weakened. Normalized membership order fixes report stability. Full regression/restart proof is recorded in the publication report. Tests use synthetic local records only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

All 1,293 behavioral tests and 4,903 API checks across 49 suites pass, including 77 dedicated reversal checks and the expanded 114-check staged-import suite. Strict TypeScript, production builds, the 19 isolated upgrades and the 48-case pinned-source comparison pass. Fresh-server checks verify reversal idempotency, live ownership review, ready/completed source status, exact staged/receipt bytes and existing D1/R2 financial/report snapshots. No production financial records are used.

## Multi-account transaction CSV mapping

The pinned Sure TransactionImport selects one import account or looks up each row’s Import::AccountMapping; missing mappings, including an unmapped blank label, prevent publishing the file. The Sites transaction importer now offers the same explicit existing-account selection boundary. In Import CSV, choose “Map accounts from a CSV column”, map the account column, read the distinct source labels and select an existing active manual account for each label. A unique exact account-name match is suggested for review; ambiguous names are not selected. Labels are trimmed and case-sensitive, with an explicit choice for blank labels. Multiple source labels may point to one account. Automatic creation of import-owned accounts and category/merchant/tag mapping or creation remain separate parity work; create manual accounts and matching entities first.

Mapped files retain the existing staging limits of 10 MiB, 10,000 rows and ten saved files per owner, with up to 500 explicit account labels per file. Schema 47 adds nullable account_mappings to csv_imports in place, retaining all financial/source/ownership rows, indexes and existing triggers. Reservation checks every mapped account’s owner and active status, including a database guard for secondary targets. Account choices are canonical and immutable within a saved draft; changing them requires a new draft. Upload/resume and full-file preparation validate every selected target. Removed or archived targets never silently remap. Source cleanup and original downloads remain available for removed-account drafts. The user-table count remains 41, financial backup remains format 30 and writer generations remain unchanged.

Each row is parsed using its target’s native precision before amount normalization. Missing currency cells use that account’s currency; explicit differing currencies reject the whole file. USD, JPY and KWD amounts remain exact without inferred conversion or mixed-currency totals. Debit/credit layouts follow the same native parsing. Converted and staged review pages show each source label, target account and currency. A complete account table shows row/new/skipped counts and before/after balances after rules through one reviewed day; future rows remain importable but do not enter that day’s balances.

Duplicate detection preserves the existing canonical date/name/native amount/notes/kind fingerprint, with occurrence counters scoped to each target account. Aliases into one account share its sequence; a matching row in another account cannot consume its occurrence number. Prior single-account imports remain recognizable. Rules apply only to newly created rows, with their existing currency and account scopes. Review and approval use stable new-entry identities for the current draft/reversal cycle, while a later re-import receives fresh IDs. The receipt binds owner, source bytes, column settings, canonical account choices, workspace revision, day and reversal cycle. All targets, rules, ownership and completion commit in one guarded transaction. Mapped imports retain their choices in staged history without overwriting a single-account last-completed column preference. Reversal tracks exactly the new rows across all targets and preserves prior duplicate matches; a duplicate-only second file claims no ownership of the first file’s rows.

The multi-account suite covers native precision, blank/alias labels, target isolation, existing single-account duplicate keys, currency-scoped rules, all review pages/counts/balances, stale/archived/removed targets, final-row validation, forced final-write rollback, concurrent approval, whole-file reversal/re-import with fresh IDs, old reversal no-ops, unchanged single-account templates, exact source bytes and the 500-label bound. The isolated schema-46 → 47 suite checks in-place legacy and ownership preservation, all prior triggers, nullable defaults, secondary-target ownership/activity, map constraints and immutability, completion/reversal guards, quotas and idempotent application. Fresh-process persistence includes mixed-currency draft metadata, complete ownership, full review and original bytes. Synthetic local records only; hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

Verification for this milestone: all 1,310 behavioral tests and 5,026 API checks across 50 suites pass, including 17 new behavioral cases and 123 dedicated multi-account checks. The 26 isolated schema-46 → 47 checks, strict TypeScript and production build pass. A restarted local Worker reproduces the mixed-currency pending review, immutable choices, complete 37-row ownership across three accounts and exact source bytes, alongside all existing financial/report/file/reversal snapshots. The expanded duplicate-only fixture confirms that reversing a second completed file cannot remove the first import’s rows. Hosted UI QA remains unverified.

## Import-created manual accounts and complete account reversal

Transaction CSV account mapping can now introduce new manual checking accounts in the same approval as their transactions. Import is available before the first manual account exists. For each source label, choose an existing account, create a named account in an explicit currency, or reuse a proposed account for another label. The default currency is the workspace currency. New accounts open at zero on their earliest imported row date; native precision and explicit row-currency validation apply before any write. A unique exact existing active name in the same currency is reused without acquiring account ownership, matching the pinned create-or-find boundary. Ambiguous, archived or differently denominated exact names require an explicit existing-account choice. A private draft stores immutable, canonical creation proposals; neither initial preview nor source upload creates financial accounts.

Approval resolves the complete graph, checks the 500-account workspace limit and validates every row, target, rule and reference. New account and transaction IDs are stable for one draft/reversal cycle. Packed inserts create accounts and exact account-ownership claims before the root/rule/entry-ownership writes and completion metadata, all in the guarded D1 transaction. Final-status failure rolls back the accounts too. An idempotent or concurrent approval cannot create a second copy. Reusing a pre-existing name never claims that account. A subsequent reversal/re-import generates fresh identities for newly created accounts and entries. Source/history removal deliberately retains the accounts and their financial data while ending their import tracking.

The pinned Import#revert runs accounts.destroy_all before entries.destroy_all. Reversal now follows that account ownership boundary: an import-created account is removed with ALL its current contents, including later manual transactions, later imports, edited/moved history within it, valuations, trades, holding/cash observations and contribution checkpoints. The review lists the owned accounts, additional account-entry deletions and all native balances. Transaction deletions retain existing complete paging and transfer behavior; surviving counterparts keep their amounts and receipt originals while transfer links/kinds/protection release. A changed surviving kind follows the existing database reconciliation reset, which the proposed graph now reflects. Pending kinds remain unchanged. Entry ownership in other imports clears only for actually deleted entries.

Account-scoped outgoing/inbound recurring schedules and their occurrences/history are removed. Payment records in surviving accountless schedules whose saved conversion or current source transaction identifies a deleted account are also removed, including valid legacy rows without a conversion snapshot; those schedules remain. Goal account links, account-specific usage rows and pending-savings history are removed. Goal settings, frozen completion data and declared consumed totals remain; a goal can have no linked accounts after account deletion, as in upstream, and the UI explains how to choose backing again. Goal creation/editing still requires a selection. Financial backup validation accepts this retained goal state. Budget selected-account scopes release the removed IDs. Account-referencing rules are disabled, clear their now-invalid predicates/actions and retain source JSON for rebuilding; historical rule logs, categories, merchants, tags, global securities/prices and other configuration remain.

The port’s account-bound structured statement records are removed and their private original files return to the independent library/inbox; shared surviving receipt links remain. This retains the originals but does not claim upstream’s accountless structured AccountStatement model, which remains part of wider statement-workspace parity. Other surviving-account payment/goal/price evidence retains its data with removed ledger links nulled. The reversal planner also now clears a removed imported savings transaction’s match pointer while preserving its historical matched evidence, matching the existing D1 unlink trigger. The account-removal review names schedules, statements, originals, affected rules/budget scopes and goals losing all backing, and reports every removed account-scoped collection before typed REVERT approval.

Schema 48 adds nullable creation proposals and a historical created-account count to csv_imports, plus csv_import_accounts, the forty-second user table. Additive migration preserves all existing financial rows, staged sources, entry ownership, indexes and unchanged triggers. Reservation guards recognize explicit new-account proposals while still rejecting foreign, missing or archived existing targets. Composite import/account ownership, unique claims, a creation-aware claim guard, completion count validation, immutable proposals and ready-reset guards prevent false completion or a legacy transaction-only reversal from dropping account ownership. The creation/account-reversal writer uses epoch 38; ordinary manual, investment, transfer and restore generations remain unchanged. Account/entry ownership stays outside financial JSON/ZIP format 30; restored/remapped records cannot be inferred as owned. Deleting financial accounts or source/history clears only the corresponding ownership metadata through composite foreign keys.

Dedicated API tests exercise creation with no prior accounts, a mixed USD/JPY/KWD file, existing duplicates, exact-name reuse without ownership, owner isolation, immutable proposals, native opening dates/balances, late rollback and concurrent commits. A real account graph is enriched with later manual and imported rows, a confirmed transfer, cleared surviving counterpart, goal usage and savings intent, assigned/accountless paid schedules, scoped rules/budget, investment trade/holding/cash/checkpoint, valuation, receipt originals and a statement PDF. Its reviewed plan is compared to the actual complete D1 graph after atomic reversal, including forced late failure and concurrency, then re-imported with fresh IDs. A separately removed empty created account permits a metadata-only reversal. Another test covers matched savings history in a surviving account. The upper-bound fixture creates 500 accounts with 10,000 total transactions (twenty repeated occurrences each), approves the full file and reverses every account/row together. Isolated schema-47 → 48 tests cover data/trigger/ownership preservation, proposal constraints, secondary foreign targets, creation-aware claims/epochs, completion/reset counts, cascades, retention on history removal, quota and idempotent migration.

Seven pinned-source checks execute the actual Ruby AccountMapping#create_mappable! and Import#revert methods with association/persistence stubs, verifying zero/default-native creation, existing-name reuse without ownership, and account-before-entry reversal order. They do not claim full Rails SQL equivalence. Synthetic local financial records only; bank and broker integrations remain excluded. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Entity mapping/creation, native account merge, investment import template reuse, broader import types and accountless structured statements remain wider parity work.

An additional behavioral and API case covers a valid inactive account-scoped rule: cleanup clears its now-invalid predicates/actions and retains its source JSON. An initial fixture incorrectly gave a blocked rule live predicates; the existing API correctly rejected that invalid configuration. The fixture was corrected to an inactive, unblocked rule without weakening the API guard or the complete graph assertions.

Milestone verification: all 1,323 behavioral cases and 5,171 API checks across 51 suites pass, including thirteen new behavioral cases and 145 account-creation API checks. The regression includes the focused corrected inactive-rule and legacy-payment follow-up. All 34 isolated schema-47 → 48 checks, seven pinned Ruby method comparisons, strict TypeScript and production builds pass. Fresh-process checks reproduce proposed account IDs/review/source bytes, three live created-account ownership claims after re-import, an old reversal receipt’s no-op, retained original receipts/PDF and all existing financial/report/file snapshots. The local upper-bound case approves and reverses 500 created accounts and 10,000 rows. Production financial rows are not used for testing. Hosted UI QA and the broader remaining port are still incomplete.

## Transaction CSV category and tag mapping

The pinned CategoryMapping and TagMapping create shared family labels, not import-owned financial entries. TransactionImport resolves these mappings before publishing and updates category, tags and nonblank notes on an existing duplicate without changing its original import. The Sites staged transaction CSV now supports explicit existing-label selection, creation/name reuse and skipping, combined with single/multiple existing accounts or import-created manual accounts. The UI reads distinct source labels, suggests exact existing category leaves/tags and new labels for unmatched names, and pages all decisions. Blank categories may be skipped or explicitly assigned to an existing category. Tags retain the port’s JSON-array input format. Source labels can be up to 255 characters when explicit mapping is enabled; created category and tag names keep the workspace’s 80-character limits.

New category names may use Parent:Child. The first colon separates the path; any later colon remains in the child name. The parent is created or reused as a root; when its label names an existing child, its root is used. A new child inherits its root’s color. Existing globally named children retain their current parent/color even if another path requests them, and an otherwise-unused requested root may still be created, matching the source create-before-find order. Tag aliases can merge into one target tag and destination tag IDs deduplicate per row. Existing names are reused exactly, without recoloring. New roots/tags have a reviewed color selection with a deterministic source-palette default; this does not claim the source’s random-root/default-null tag color behavior or icon metadata. All new labels appear in the staged review with their resolved root/color.

Mapped prior-import matches receive the source branch’s nonempty category/tag/note updates. Empty/skipped category or tag lists retain prior metadata on a match while leaving new rows empty. The review exposes every current before/after category, tag list and note, with independent twenty-five-row pagination and a receipt covering the entire file. IDs, native money, original import keys, protected fields, reconciliation evidence, split/receipt links and original import ownership remain. Rules run only on newly created roots. Budget/goal report effects and account-native balances are prepared from the complete proposed graph. Match detection itself retains the port’s canonical occurrence keys; source fuzzy/manual-entry duplicate matching remains wider parity work.

Approval atomically creates categories/tags, applies existing-entry metadata updates, creates any accounts and new financial rows, applies rules, records only new row/account ownership and completes the import. A failed last status write rolls back label creation and earlier-entry updates too. Duplicate-only imports may still create shared labels or change previous entries and then advance the financial revision once. A retry or concurrent approval does not repeat those changes. Reversal removes only its owned entries/accounts: labels created during the import and metadata updates to earlier entries remain, including on a duplicate-only reversal. This matches the source ownership boundary. Financial JSON/ZIP format 30 already includes the normal category/tag records; saved source label choices are separate import history and do not silently remap after financial replacement.

Schema 49 adds nullable entity mapping digest/count metadata and a bounded existing-update count to csv_imports in place. csv_import_entity_mappings is the forty-third user table. Choices are stored as individually bounded rows, with composite owner/import linkage, source/kind uniqueness, JSON/source consistency, owned target validation, immutable updates and a 2,000-per-kind limit. The canonical digest/count is checked again on full preparation. Reservation stores its source marker and all choices atomically. This supports up to 2,000 category and 2,000 tag labels without placing a multi-megabyte JSON object in one D1 row; reservation metadata is bounded to 6 MB while the original CSV retains 10 MiB/10,000-row limits. Removing source/history cascades only its choices, retaining shared labels and financial data. The additive migration preserves existing finance, file, draft and row/account ownership records and prior triggers. Epoch 40 binds mapped commits and permits their account-creation claims; older CSV writers cannot silently ignore choices. Ready/reset requires the existing-update counter to clear. Ordinary writers, account reversal, restore generation and the backup format remain unchanged.

Verification includes a mixed USD/JPY/KWD import that creates a manual account, two categories and a tag, maps five tag labels, deduplicates tag aliases and updates 27 previously owned entries without claiming them. Row, label and existing-change pages share the same complete receipt. Tests cover exact leaf/name reuse, unchanged hierarchy/color/protection, stale reviews, foreign references, forced final-write rollback, concurrent approval, original bytes, retained labels/previous updates through reversal/re-import, old reversal no-ops, and duplicate-only metadata/label persistence without reversal money changes. A separate real Worker fixture reserves mapping metadata over one megabyte, creates 1,900 categories and 2,000 tags from long UTF-8 source labels, imports 1,900 rows (including 100-tag rows), pages all 3,900 decisions, reverses all imported entries and retains every shared label. Isolated schema-48 → 49 checks cover legacy and ownership preservation, changed/unchanged triggers, mapping constraints, immutability, target isolation, per-kind quota, writer/reset guards, metadata cleanup and idempotent migration. Eighteen comparisons execute the actual pinned Ruby category/tag creation methods and duplicate-update branch with association/persistence stubs; they do not claim full Rails SQL equivalence. Tests use synthetic local records only. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

The first API fixture requested an unused root named Other, which already exists in the default workspace and was correctly reused. It was corrected to a distinct requested root to exercise creation without changing the source reuse behavior. Merchant mapping/import, native account merge, standalone/richer import types, native attachment manifests, accountless structured statements and broader parity remain pending. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified.

Milestone verification: all 1,340 behavioral cases and 5,279 API checks across 52 suites pass, including seventeen new behavioral cases and 108 dedicated entity-mapping API checks. All 33 isolated schema-48 → 49 checks, eighteen pinned Ruby comparisons, strict TypeScript and production builds pass. A focused final API run verifies budget effects against the original workspace snapshot (126 → 326), including newly proposed labels/accounts. Fresh-process checks reproduce pending label choices and stable review identities, exact original CSV bytes, completed mapping counts, prior versus new import ownership, retained metadata through reversal, old reversal no-ops and the existing financial/report/file snapshots. Production financial records are not used. Hosted UI QA and the broader remaining port are still incomplete.

## Merchant CSV import and export

Merchants now has an Import / export merchants action, available without financial accounts. It accepts Sure's separate MerchantImport CSV with name (required), color and website_url columns; website URL/website-url/website aliases, starred/case/space-normalized headers and comma, semicolon, tab or pipe separators are supported. Normalized duplicate headers reject the file. Names are trimmed and matched exactly, including case. Blank names are skipped. The first new occurrence supplies its color/website; later duplicate rows and already-existing merchants reuse their saved details without overwriting them. Older workspaces with duplicate merchant names retain all existing records and consistently reuse the lowest-ID exact match. Imported shared merchants do not belong to an import's reversal graph.

Every file is privately staged, checksum-bound and resumable, with up to ten saved merchant sources independently of transaction CSV history. Paged review shows all create/reuse/blank decisions, colors, websites and any normalization before a whole-file approval. New merchants, completion counts and the financial revision commit atomically. Forced completion failure rolls back all merchant creation; stale reviews and changed source metadata cannot commit. Concurrent approval creates one copy, and a completed retry returns its durable status. Duplicate-only or blank-name-only approval does not advance the financial revision. Lost ready markers can recover the saved original bytes. Exact originals download through the owner-bound source route; removal drops source/history while retaining every merchant and financial record.

Reviewing and typing REVERT resets a completed merchant import to ready without deleting or modifying merchants, transactions or any other financial collection, matching Import#revert's shared-configuration ownership boundary. Re-import reuses retained names, or recreates names later removed by the user with fresh cycle identities. Concurrent/retried resets are durable, and a previous reset receipt cannot undo a subsequent approval. Workspace revision guards and an independent stage-status guard protect duplicate-only approvals and metadata-only resets as well as normal imports. Financial replacement may remap merchant IDs; history remains external, and a new review resolves the current owned names rather than inferring old identity.

Existing manual merchant fields, native Sure migration and financial JSON/ZIP format 30 already support color and website data. Native-header merchant CSV export is owner-scoped and round-trips ordinary names and metadata; consistent with other port CSV exports, spreadsheet-formula-like names are escaped with an apostrophe, while JSON/ZIP remains lossless. No website is fetched and no logo/provider integration is introduced. Blank or invalid new colors use a visible deterministic source-palette default (#e99537), rather than Sure's random palette choice. Bare website domains gain https:// with an explicit review annotation; new websites must fit the existing 2,000-character HTTP/HTTPS-without-credentials rule. Names retain the existing 160-character and workspace 5,000-merchant limits. Invalid metadata on reused or blank-name rows is ignored, matching the source's skip-before-write behavior. These normalization and existing workspace limits are explicit differences from Rails' unrestricted website string/name length.

Schema 50 adds only merchant_csv_imports, the forty-fourth user table, preserving all existing financial/source/ownership rows and triggers. Bounded source/count/delimiter checks, immutable reservations, owner quotas, guarded completion/reset and allowed status transitions protect staged state. The dedicated merchant writer uses epoch 41; existing ordinary writers, restore generation 37 and financial backup format 30 are unchanged. Merchant history is separate from financial backups.

Verification adds twelve behavioral cases, 159 dedicated real Worker API checks, 28 isolated schema-49 → 50 checks and ten comparisons executing the actual pinned Ruby MerchantImport parsing/import methods with persistence stubs (not full Rails SQL). All 1,352 behavioral cases pass. The API fixture approves an exact 10 MiB source with 10,000 rows and 5,000 distinct merchants, validates its last page, rejects workspace overflow and proves reset retains the full graph. Other cases cover original BOM bytes, ownership/origin/authentication, quotas, interrupted upload recovery, stale review, final-write rollback, concurrent approval/reset, retention, export/re-import, and financial backup/restore with website/color preservation. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. A fresh local Worker reproduces the pending complete review and stable proposed IDs, exact source bytes, completed duplicate-only counts and original checksum, the retained 5,000-merchant graph, and all prior financial/report/file snapshots. The existing 5,279 API checks from version 53 are a prior regression baseline; this milestone reran the full behavioral suite, the 159 focused merchant API checks, upgrade/source comparisons and the complete restart snapshot checks. Tests use synthetic local records only; production financial rows are not used. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded. Category and other standalone imports, native account merge, attachment-manifest conversion, accountless structured statements and wider upstream parity remain pending.

## Standalone category CSV import, export and icons

Categories now has an Import / export categories action for Sure's name, color, parent_category and lucide_icon CSV. Starred/case/space/hyphen-normalized headers and the icon alias are supported with comma, semicolon, tab or pipe separators. Every nonblank source row needs a valid category name; invalid colors, overlong icons/names and normalized duplicate headers reject the complete source. Names match exactly and case-sensitively. Unlike merchant import, an existing category is updated in place. Blank color/icon cells preserve its current metadata, falling back to #737373 and shapes for new records or missing legacy icon data. Missing named parents become shared placeholders with those defaults. Names retain the existing 80-character and 2,000-category workspace limits; icon codes are bounded to 80 characters.

The planner executes the source's two-pass algorithm: first update/detach each named row and ensure its parent placeholder, then assign parent relationships in source order. Parents can appear after children. Duplicate rows retain the last supplied metadata, with source-order parent assignment and inherited-color callbacks; a later duplicate that detaches a category may retain a color inherited earlier in that pass. Children inherit their root color. Updating a root propagates color to its current children even when those children are omitted from the CSV. Self-parenting, cycles, assigning grandchildren or making a parent with children into a child reject the entire operation. Sixteen fixtures compare these results and failures with the actual pinned Ruby CategoryImport methods and Category inheritance/level/color callbacks using persistence stubs, rather than claiming full Rails SQL equivalence.

Every category file uses separate private, owner-scoped resumable staging with exact original downloads, checksums and ten-source quotas. Limits are 10 MiB and 10,000 source rows. Complete-file review has independent twenty-five-row pages for source rows, all changed categories (including unlisted children and placeholders), and budget allocation changes. Stable proposed IDs, the full source/separator/reset cycle, financial revision and reviewed day are bound to one approval receipt. The port's existing budget hierarchy policy retains direct allocations and reflows parent envelopes/automatic totals; new derived envelope IDs remain stable across review pages. Aggregate budget/goal report effects are prepared from the proposed graph. Approval saves categories, icons, budget changes, completion counts and one financial revision atomically. Forced final-write failure rolls back every earlier change. Concurrent/retried approval creates one copy. A no-change re-import completes without a financial revision.

Reviewed typed REVERT resets import history while retaining every category, color, icon, hierarchy change and budget/financial record, matching the source's shared-configuration ownership boundary. Old reset receipts cannot undo a later approval. Removing source/history likewise retains shared financial configuration. Native-header category CSV export round-trips ordinary names and metadata for Sure's two-level hierarchy. The older port's existing deeper hierarchies remain readable and recoverable through JSON/ZIP, but must be reorganized before re-import through this source-compatible category CSV. Spreadsheet-formula-like export names use the same apostrophe escaping as the other port CSVs; JSON/ZIP remains lossless.

Category icon metadata is now stored and included in manual editing, category cards, native Sure NDJSON migration and financial JSON/ZIP format 31. Formats 1–30 remain accepted. Null legacy storage is omitted from the public state instead of inventing metadata on untouched categories. Manual edits without an icon field retain imported icons. A bundled registry renders all pinned source icon codes plus template/default icons, with a shapes fallback while preserving any other valid code. No remote lookup is required. Existing transaction CSV mapping keeps shared metadata intact.

Schema 51 adds nullable lucide_icon to categories in place and category_csv_imports, the forty-fifth user table. Existing financial/source/ownership records and triggers remain. Category approvals use epoch 42; existing ordinary writers and restore epoch 37 remain. New restores identify their icon-preserving capability with a category-restore guard. An older replacement writer is blocked when owned category icons exist, and icon writes require a supported manual/import/restore guard, preventing silent loss or untracked metadata writes. Reservation immutability, bounded counts, owner quotas, guarded completion/reset and state transitions protect saved category sources. Import history remains outside financial backups.

Verification: all 1,366 behavioral cases and 5,618 API checks across 54 suites pass, including fourteen new behavioral cases and 180 dedicated category CSV checks. All 38 isolated schema-50 → 51 checks and sixteen pinned Ruby method/callback comparisons pass, alongside strict TypeScript and production builds. Complete API regression includes current format-31 archive assertions and positive legacy backup fixtures; previously unsupported-version assertions now reject version 32. The final focused category suite additionally verifies human-readable budget date ranges. Real Worker checks cover 64 category changes from 33 source rows, two budget-envelope changes with stable proposed IDs, unlisted-child propagation, financial identity preservation, interrupted upload recovery, final-write rollback, concurrent approval/reset, retained metadata after reset/re-import, original BOM bytes, owner isolation, quotas, export/re-import and format-31 JSON/ZIP recovery. The exact 10 MiB / 10,000-row fixture reaches 2,000 categories (1,989 newly created plus eleven updated defaults), pages the last source/change records, rejects overflow and retains the graph after reset. Fresh-process checks reproduce pending review/IDs/source bytes, completed counts/checksum, retained icon metadata and all prior financial/report/file snapshots. Tests use synthetic local records only; production financial records are not used. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded. Native account merge, investment CSV template reuse, other standalone/import formats, attachment manifests, accountless structured statements and wider upstream parity remain pending.

## Additive native Sure import with account targets

The native Sure migration form now offers Add to my workspace with account mapping. The source primitive is Family::DataImporter account_targets, not a general account-to-account consolidation operation. Each source account explicitly creates a new manual account or selects an active owned target with the same currency and account family (checking/savings share the depository family). Names alone never select an account. Multiple source accounts may share a target if the final graph is valid; mappings that turn a transfer into a self-transfer reject the entire import. Incoming activity must fit the retained target opening date.

The current workspace profile and all original financial identities are retained. Existing targets keep their type, opening balance/date, report exclusions, cash-planning choices and loan choices. Incoming names and archive status update selected targets. Sure's exported balance observations, including the reported Account balance at the selected export date, remain authoritative at their dates. They can override a target's calculated balance; later existing activity still follows the observation. Reviews expose the full account mapping, retained opening anchors and native before/after balances, all new/updated records with readable field values, existing budget/goal effects, loan terms and optional foreign execution evidence. Changed records, balances and conversions have independent pages bound to the same complete review receipt.

Shared category/tag/merchant names reuse current identities. Native category callbacks retain an existing parent when a source parent is null (unlike standalone category CSV), inherit root colors and propagate root color edits to unlisted children. Missing tag colors retain current colors, or use the first source palette color for new labels. An omitted merchant website retains its current URL; an explicit null clears it. Securities reuse ticker/exchange definitions without overwriting local definitions; incompatible cash/standard classifications reject the graph. Dated valuations, holdings, cash/contribution balances, quotes, splits and exchange rates upsert their natural keys while retaining existing observation IDs. Other imported records append with stable new identities. Imported trade sequences and rules follow current records, and rules do not run during native import. Imported primary budget plans become separate plans where a current primary currency plan exists. Existing category hierarchy changes reflow current budget allocations under the port's established direct-allocation policy. Affected completed statements retain their completion/snapshots and require renewed review.

Native writes and an immutable owner/request completion marker save in one guarded D1 batch. A stale revision, source, mapping or review day rejects approval. Failure of the final marker rolls back all finance writes. Concurrent approval creates one copy; the exact request can be retried safely, including after a later full recovery removes/remaps the original target IDs. A different request for the same file can append another copy of entries and is explicitly warned about. Once saved, the completion marker is checked before source parsing or target/rate resolution. Existing CSV import claims, keys and original files remain owned by their original imports; native entries do not acquire those claims. Pending-savings matching runs after the native batch for eligible recorded changes, consistent with CSV import. Matching may advance one additional financial revision; a follow-up failure reports that the native import is saved and asks for a Goals review, rather than replaying the native writes.

Schema 52 adds native_merge_commits, the forty-sixth user table, and three guards without replacing any previous table or trigger. Native approval uses restore epoch 37 with a category-restore:native-merge owner/request guard, preserving category icons and all previously supported graph types. Marker hashes, bounded JSON, owner/request uniqueness, guarded insertion, immutability and duplicate-request guards are enforced in D1. Financial backup format remains 31 (formats 1–31 accepted). Completion history is outside financial backup content and does not store original NDJSON, an R2 source, or a reversal graph. Keep the original file and a pre-import JSON/ZIP recovery copy. Resumable large native sessions, native import reversal/cleanup and native attachment-manifest conversion remain separate parity work; this operation retains the existing 8,000,000-byte native source, 250,000-character line, 100,000-source-line and 20,000 combined financial-record limits, plus all collection limits.

Verification: all 1,384 behavioral cases and 5,754 API checks across 56 suites pass, including eighteen new behavioral cases, 94 native merge API checks and 42 native graph preservation checks. All 34 isolated schema-51 → 52 checks and sixteen comparisons executing actual pinned Ruby account-target/shared-metadata methods with persistence stubs pass (not full Rails SQL). Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. The complete regression exercises the shared insert writer used by both additive import and replacement recovery. A fresh Worker reproduces the pending native review and stable proposed identities, completed marker and old-target retry after full recovery, original receipt bytes, the 20,000-record graph, and every earlier financial/report/file snapshot. Dedicated API cases exercise auth/origin/ownership, exact 8,000,000-byte input, malformed/oversized atomic rejection, whole-graph overflow, last-page review, authoritative snapshots plus retained later activity, primary-budget preservation/reflow, failed final-marker rollback, concurrent approval, immutable completion, retained CSV ownership through reversal, recurring evidence, disabled/no-run imported rules, reused security definitions and observation identities, appended trade sequences, opted-in FX evidence and pending-savings post-processing. Synthetic local records only; production financial records were not used. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified, and this milestone does not claim full upstream parity.

## Investment CSV mappings and completed configuration reuse

The portfolio CSV form now supports label-based saved mappings for investment activity, holding snapshots and dated prices. It follows Sure Import#suggested_template's completed-only account/type selection and template_attributes' configuration-only copy boundary. Trades and holdings have separate mappings per owned investment/crypto account; dated prices use one owner-scoped global mapping because quotes are shared by securities, with row/default currencies still selected explicitly. A successful import saves headers and format choices in the same D1 batch as financial writes. Validation failures or a failed final template save leave both the prior mapping and financial graph intact. Duplicate-only trade files and identical observation files can update their completed configuration without advancing the financial revision or rewriting observations.

Mappings store column labels rather than indices, so reordered starred/case/space/hyphen-equivalent headers can be reapplied explicitly. Missing or ambiguous saved labels require manual remapping. The form can map date, ticker, exchange/MIC, activity, exact quantity/price, fee/cash, currency/account, name/notes, ordering, exclusion, average basis/locks, foreign quoted currency/rate, exported conversion evidence and original duplicate keys. Comma, semicolon, tab and pipe separators, skipped physical preamble lines, the existing three date formats and four strict number formats are supported. Quantities retain eighteen decimal places and unit prices ten; formatting never uses binary floating point to normalize securities or money. Existing canonical exports still round-trip conversion snapshots and import keys.

Sure's default trade CSV layout is also accepted: date*, ticker*, qty*, price*, exchange_operating_mic, currency, account and name. Without an activity column, nonzero signed quantity infers buy/sell and native cash uses the port's currency rounding; positive quantity buys and negative quantity sells. Zero-quantity cash rows require an explicit activity column. Securities must already exist, and a provided exchange resolves ambiguous tickers. Selected account and currency constraints, conversion/observation consistency, fingerprint duplicate policy and the existing one-million-byte/500-row bounds remain. Multi-account trade mapping is added below. Staged larger investment imports, provider-based security resolution, reversed source signage choices, additional source date formats and liberal currency-symbol stripping remain wider parity work. The strict number parser intentionally rejects malformed grouping and symbols, consistent with the port's existing transaction CSV behavior.

The review pages all proposed rows in groups of twenty-five and shows exact quantities/prices, native cash/value, fees, activity, notes, exclusion, basis/locks, previous observations and retained FX ratios. It shows repeat counts for skipped trades. Changing a mapping/format/account invalidates the review, and the new UI sends its financial revision so rate edits and other concurrent finance changes require a new review. Legacy programmatic imports without that optional revision remain compatible and still validate the complete graph atomically at execution. The import remembers only configuration, not CSV contents, staging history or a reversal graph.

Schema 53 adds investment_csv_templates, the forty-seventh user table, without changing previous tables or triggers. Owner/account foreign keys and cascading account removal prevent orphaned or cross-owner settings; kind/scope checks, bounded valid JSON, immutable scope and active investment-account guards protect writes under existing investment epoch 6. Financial backup format remains 31. Account mappings disappear when the original accounts are removed/remapped during full recovery; the owner's global price mapping remains outside financial backup content. Native account-target import retains settings attached to existing IDs. No financial backup version or existing mutation capability changes.

Focused validation adds twenty-three behavioral cases, 88 real Worker API checks, 29 isolated schema-52 → 53 checks and fourteen comparisons executing actual pinned Ruby trade/template methods with persistence stubs (not full Rails SQL). The exact one-MB/500-row input imports its last row, rejects overflow/late malformed entries atomically and preserves prior configuration on failure. Other cases exercise original row numbers after preamble skipping, normalized header ambiguity, exact decimals, saved reordered/optional fields, concurrent-revision rejection, final-write rollback including duplicate-only files, account/kind/owner separation, reused observations, converted evidence after rate removal, format-31 recovery and account-deletion cleanup. All 1,407 behavioral cases and 5,842 API checks across 57 suites pass. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. A fresh Worker reproduces the account-bound trade mapping and global price mapping, every financial record from the latest import/recovery, pending native/CSV reviews and all prior financial/report/file snapshots. Tests use synthetic local records only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded; full upstream parity remains unfinished.

## Standalone manual account CSV import and owned-account reversal

Accounts → Import accounts accepts Sure's Account type*, Name*, Balance*, Currency and Balance Date template, legacy type/name/balance/amount headers and explicit column mappings. Source labels map explicitly to the port's manual account types; no bank/provider account or credentials are created. Every row builds a fresh account, including repeated or existing names, matching AccountImport#import!'s build boundary. A file contains 1–50 rows, within the existing 500-account and combined financial-record limits. Invalid or late malformed rows reject the complete file. CSV sources support the existing four separators, three date formats, four strict number formats and skipped physical preamble lines. Headers are star/case/space/hyphen normalized; ambiguous aliases require explicit mapping. Currency defaults to the current workspace currency at review; explicit currencies retain native precision. Blank dates use the source opening manager's two-year default, with leap-day clamping. Explicit dates must be valid and no later than the review date.

Each account receives an explicit opening_anchor valuation and a matching native opening balance/date. Sure-mode source balances use the existing native adapter convention: positive liability balances mean debt and reverse sign for the port's signed ledger. Asset signs remain. An explicit signed-ledger option preserves already signed debt balances instead. The source template itself contains a negative credit-card example, which becomes a positive overpayment in Sure mode; the form calls this out and reviews source and ledger balances side by side rather than silently changing the interpretation. Imported accounts have no inferred institution credentials, terms or investment positions. Existing accounts and all their IDs remain untouched.

The original UTF-8 CSV is privately staged in R2 with exact byte-size/SHA-256 validation and saved immutable format/type choices. Up to ten sources per owner are retained. Interrupted uploads can recover from saved identical bytes or retry the exact original; original downloads retain BOM/line endings and every byte.
