Approval covers the entire file and uses stable proposed account/anchor IDs, all type/currency/sign/date decisions, an owner/revision/day-bound receipt and one atomic D1 batch containing accounts, anchors, ownership and completion. Stale reviews reject approval, failed final writes roll back everything and concurrent approval creates one copy. The same completed request does not import again. A reversed source gets fresh deterministic IDs from its new reversal seed on reapproval.

Reversal follows Sure Import#revert's account-owned boundary: it removes the imported accounts and ALL their current contents, including later manual entries and entries from other imports. It does not merely delete opening anchors. A complete typed-REVERT review exposes every removed account, current entries and dependent fees, surviving transfer releases, native balances, budget/goal effects, statements and account-scoped cleanup, with independent twenty-five-row pages for entries, releases and effects. Account-scoped valuations, trades, holdings, cash/contribution observations, schedules/occurrences/payment records, goal links/usage/savings history and statements are removed; original files return to the library, global securities/prices, shared labels, goal settings/declared totals and historical rule logs remain. Account-referencing rules are disabled and budget scopes release removed accounts. This reuses the existing complete CSV account-destruction planner and extracted SQL writer; ordinary transaction CSV reversal retains its previous behavior. A failed final reset rolls back finance and ownership together. Old reversal receipts remain no-ops after later reapproval, preserving the new graph.

Removing source/history is a separate retention operation: it deletes the private source and claims while retaining accounts and their contents. Financial JSON/ZIP recovery carries financial records, not import ownership. Replacement remaps accounts and cascades away old claims; completed request retries and subsequent empty reversals cannot delete restored copies. Saved source history remains available until explicitly removed. This ownership-history retention policy is shared with the port's existing transaction CSV workflows.

Schema 54 adds account_csv_imports and account_csv_accounts, bringing the user-table count to 49. Existing tables, data and triggers remain. Owner/request/storage uniqueness, immutable reservation metadata, bounded JSON/counts, quotas, status transitions, guarded claim insertion/completion/reset and owner/account/import foreign keys protect the lifecycle. Existing mutation epoch 38 handles complete account cleanup; no financial backup version or existing mutation epoch changes. Financial backup format stays 31. The complete reversal review component is shared with transaction CSV imports.

Focused checks add twenty-eight behavioral cases, 168 real Worker staging/cleanup checks, 34 isolated schema-53 → 54 checks and 74 comparisons executing actual pinned Ruby account creation/template methods with persistence/opening-manager stubs (not full Rails SQL). The exact 10 MiB/fifty-row source reaches 500 live accounts, validates its final page, rejects overflow and downloads byte-identically. Tests cover source sign conventions and every manual account type, stable proposals, duplicate names, default/leap dates, strict formatting, all-or-nothing rows, upload/completion/reversal rollback, concurrent approval/reversal, current account contents, surviving transfers, global records, original PNG/PDF retention, stale/old receipts, reapproval, history retention, restoration ownership boundaries and source quotas. All 1,435 behavioral cases pass. The complete API regression passed 6,005 checks across 58 suites; the final focused run additionally verifies arbitrary-header mapping and all 168 account CSV checks after separating header inspection from required-field resolution. Together with the unchanged 5,842-check baseline from 57 other API suites, coverage totals 6,010 API checks. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. Fresh-process snapshots verify pending stable account proposals, saved source choices/BOM bytes, live ownership after reapproval, old reversal no-ops, original PNG/PDF bytes, the 500-account graph and every earlier financial/report/file snapshot. Tests use synthetic local records only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded; broader upstream parity remains unfinished.


## Rule CSV import, export and retained configuration

Rules → Import / export rules accepts Sure transaction-rule CSVs with name, resource_type, active, effective_date, conditions, actions and optional group columns. Separators include comma, semicolon, tab and pipe. The importer accepts standard quoted JSON, one wrapped JSON string, and Sure’s older loosely quoted JSON reconstruction. Valid JSON preserves literal backslash-n/r/t sequences; legacy cleanup follows Sure’s additional string normalization. NUL bytes, invalid Unicode, excessive nesting, unknown condition/action types, unsupported resources and empty action lists reject the entire file. Dates currently accept ISO calendar dates and day/month/year slashes; unsupported/invalid date text clears the date and is explicitly shown in review. Sure’s Ruby Date.parse accepts additional forms not yet ported. The supported template includes a grocery-category example.

Exact nonblank names update the first matching rule in run order, retaining its identity and position. This is deterministic for legacy duplicate names, where upstream find_by has no explicit ordering. New names append; repeated names in one file use their final definition. Missing group columns retain existing labels and blank group values clear them. Unnamed source rows always create fresh rules; the port supplies a display name while retaining the original null name for CSV export. A manual rename converts that exported name into a normal named rule. Missing categories, merchants and tags are created once and reused throughout the file with Sure’s default colors and root category icon. Existing label details remain unchanged. Conditions and split labels resolve to owned metadata, and raw split IDs must already belong to this workspace. Account conditions require an owned ID or unambiguous account name.

Supported text/type/amount/reference conditions, one-level any/all groups, rename/category/merchant/tag/exclusion actions and validated fixed/percentage splits become executable port rules. Tags merge rather than replace. Numeric conditions/fixed splits use the explicitly displayed currency selection, with the workspace currency as the API default. Existing text-only rules retain their local currency and stop setting. Blank native category/merchant actions are not converted into clears: they can only be retained disabled. Known unsupported AI, email, regex, transfer, investment-label and other actions can be preserved disabled with empty executable conditions/actions only after an explicit immutable opt-in. The original configuration and reason are visible in review, downloadable in the CSV source/editor and retained in JSON/ZIP financial backups. Unknown types still reject. No imported rule runs against existing history during approval; enabled rules apply to future transaction CSV imports or a separate explicit reviewed rule run.

Private R2 staging retains exact original UTF-8 bytes, including BOM/line endings, plus SHA-256, size and immutable separator/currency/disabled-preservation choices. Sources support 1–10,000 rows, at most 10 MiB, ten source records per owner and the existing 500-rule/workspace and metadata/20,000-record limits. Reviews independently page source decisions and every final before/after rule/label change in groups of twenty-five. Proposed IDs and owner/revision/source/options/reset-bound receipts are stable until the workspace or import changes. Approval saves all metadata, rule replacements and completion in one guarded atomic D1 batch. Stale receipts reject, failed final saves roll back and concurrent approval makes one copy. A second guard checks the exact completion/reset snapshot inside that batch, so history-only changes cannot bypass a finance revision that stayed unchanged. Interrupted uploads can recover saved identical bytes, or retry with the exact original file.

Sure’s rule-import reset retains rules and shared labels. The port follows that boundary: typed REVERT resets only import history, retaining every rule, category, merchant, tag, ledger entry and rule-run record. Named reapproval is idempotent; unnamed rows create additional rules after a reset. Old reset receipts remain no-ops after later reapproval. Removing source/history deletes only the original file and lifecycle record. Financial restore remaps rule references without importing source-history ownership.

Ordered CSV export uses portable names for supported conditions/actions/splits. Disabled rules export their preserved portable Sure configuration with active=false; a blocked rule without a portable source fails with a JSON-backup/rebuild instruction. An optional sure_sites_options JSON column preserves this port’s currency, stop-processing, explicit clear-label and include-in-reports settings. Sure ignores that extension and cannot recover those settings. Exported spreadsheet formula-like top-level text receives the established apostrophe guard; use JSON/ZIP backups for lossless recovery. Nested tag CSV values are RFC-quoted without a BOM or spreadsheet guard, since they are JSON text rather than spreadsheet cells.

Schema 55 adds rule_csv_imports, bringing the user-table count to 50. All 49 previous table snapshots and triggers remain intact. Immutable reservations, quotas, bounded options/counts, status transitions and owner/status-bound completion/reset guards protect the lifecycle. The existing category-capable mutation epoch 42 is reused; financial backup format 31 and all previous mutation epochs stay unchanged. csv-parse 7.0.3 supplies a portable browser/Worker liberal CSV reader; its MIT license is included at /csv-parse-LICENSE.txt.

Verification adds 26 behavioral cases, 164 real Worker API checks, 32 isolated schema-54 → 55 checks and 32 comparisons executing actual pinned Ruby parser/normalizer/boolean/date methods with minimal model stubs, rather than full Rails SQL. The exact 10 MiB/10,000-row source reviews its last page and saves 500 distinct rules atomically. Checks cover name matching, groups, reference ownership/creation, unsupported preservation/original export, local CSV options, stable proposals, upload recovery, final-write rollback, concurrency, stale/old receipts, reset/history retention, financial restoration and source quotas. All 1,461 behavioral cases pass. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. The full regression passed 6,169 API checks across 59 suites; the final focused rule CSV run passes 164 checks after adding lifecycle-race assertions. Together with the unchanged 6,010-check baseline from the other 58 suites, coverage totals 6,174 API checks. A fresh Worker reproduces stable pending proposals, original CSV bytes, disabled originals, the 500-rule graph, old-reset no-ops and every earlier financial/report/file snapshot. Tests use synthetic localhost records only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded; broader upstream parity remains unfinished.


## Multi-account manual trade CSV mapping

The existing Portfolio → Import CSV workflow now supports Map accounts from the CSV for trade/activity files. Read the source account column after choosing columns and formats; map every distinct label, including blank labels, to an owned active investment/crypto account. Unique exact names preselect existing targets, while aliases and ambiguous names require a choice. Several labels may share a target. Every mapped label must be present in the file; missing, duplicate, unused, foreign, archived and incompatible mappings reject the complete import. Holdings and dated prices keep their existing scoped workflows.

Each row uses the selected account’s native currency and opening-date boundary. Blank currency cells use that account’s currency, while explicit currency cells must match. This deliberately follows the port’s native-ledger contract; upstream CSV normalization can fill missing currency with the family default before TradeImport’s mapped-account fallback. Foreign quoted trades still use the explicit quote_currency/exchange_rate or preserved conversion-evidence workflow, with source prices/fees/cash and exact native rounding. Mapping an account never implies a currency conversion. Source signed quantities still infer buys/sells, and the established explicit-activity path remains available.

You can explicitly propose a new manual investment or crypto account, with a name, type and currency. New owned IDs and trade IDs are stable between review and approval for the current request. One compatible exact-name account is reused unchanged; an ambiguous, archived or differently typed/currency name requires an explicit existing selection. Several labels can use one proposal. Unused/colliding proposals reject. New accounts open with zero cash on their earliest imported trade date, rather than inventing funding or balances. Buys can leave negative uninvested cash until the user records funding. This adapts Sure AccountMapping’s zero-balance creation to the port’s investment-capable account types; Sure’s generic fallback creates a Depository. Existing account balances, types, opening dates and identifiers remain untouched.

The review lists every account mapping, new manual account, native currency, zero opening date and each activity’s resolved account. Source rows page in groups of twenty-five. All new accounts, trades and completed per-account column templates save in one owner/revision-guarded D1 batch. A failed final template write rolls back the whole batch; concurrent approvals yield one successful save and a stale-review response for the other. Batch inserts stay below D1’s one-hundred-parameter limit. New accounts and trades count toward the existing 500-account and 20,000-financial-record workspace limits. Duplicate occurrences are scoped to the resolved target: identical trades in distinct accounts survive, and repeated rows/aliases in one account retain separate occurrence keys. Repeat approval skips existing trade fingerprints, preserves reporting choices and leaves the finance revision unchanged when no financial data changes. Reusing a request identifier for a different existing trade rejects instead of overwriting it.

Completed column labels/settings are saved for every target account. In multi-account mode, choose an account under Load completed column settings from, then apply its previous mapping to the current file. This reuses column/date/number settings, not prior source bytes or account-target choices. Failed imports preserve the old templates. Saved templates stay outside financial backups and are removed when their account is replaced/deleted. JSON/ZIP financial recovery retains and remaps all new accounts, trades and original FX/reporting evidence. Existing single-account trades, holdings, prices and template APIs remain available.

This extension keeps the established 500-row/1,000,000-byte investment-file limit and the finance API’s 1,100,000-byte combined JSON request limit. Very large mappings reduce the source bytes that fit in that request. The following staged-trade milestone adds resumable R2 trade sources, import-owned reversal and a ten-thousand-row trade path. Automatic security/provider resolution and additional source signage/date/number formats remain pending. Create owned securities first and supply an exchange to disambiguate tickers. Those remain part of the broader port; bank and broker connections remain excluded. No schema change is required: schema 55, fifty user tables, mutation epoch 6 and financial backup format 31 remain in place.

Verification adds twenty behavioral cases, 78 real Worker API checks and 51 comparisons executing actual pinned TradeImport, AccountMapping creation and Row signed-amount methods with persistence/security-resolver stubs (not full Rails SQL or provider resolution). Tests cover mixed USD/JPY/KWD files, aliases/blank labels, new and reused accounts, independent/merged duplicate occurrences, ownership/active/type/currency/opening boundaries, stable IDs, unchanged existing balances, negative cash without fabricated funding, final-write rollback, concurrency/stale reviews, foreign quote evidence, duplicate-safe exports and financial restore. A real Worker saves 500 new accounts, 500 trades and all 500 scoped templates atomically, then rejects account overflow. A separate exact one-million-byte mapped source passes and overflow rejects. Strict TypeScript, production builds and the 1,481-case behavioral suite pass. The complete API regression passes all 6,252 checks across 60 suites. A fresh Worker reproduces the mixed-currency accounts/trades, all saved scoped template snapshots, the 500-account/trade graph, the exact-byte-limit account and every previous financial/report/file snapshot. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified; wider upstream parity is incomplete.


## Saved trade CSV sources and complete ownership-based reversal

Portfolio → Saved trade imports accepts UTF-8 trade/activity CSVs up to 10 MiB and 10,000 rows, with the existing single-account or multi-account column mapping and explicit new manual investment/crypto account proposals. The direct investment importer remains available at 500 rows/1,000,000 bytes. Holdings and dated prices still use their existing scoped workflows. Securities must already exist; exchange values disambiguate tickers. No bank or broker connection is involved.

Reserve a private source, upload its exact bytes to R2 and review before financial approval. Immutable canonical configuration, source SHA-256/size verification, stable cycle-specific account/trade identifiers and owner-local access protect resumed work. Check saved upload recovers a lost upload acknowledgment; exact reupload can repair a missing original without changing completed finance. Keep at most ten source/history records. The original download preserves BOM and all uploaded bytes. Deleting source/history retains finance and forgets reversal ownership.

The server review pages through all source rows, resolved accounts, current duplicate decisions, position/cost-basis changes, native cash/holdings/total balances and budget/goal effects. Review receipts bind the owner, exact source/configuration, financial revision, review date and import lifecycle. Approval writes all new accounts, trades, per-account completed column settings, ownership claims and completion in one D1 batch. A lifecycle guard also covers races that do not advance the finance revision. Failed final writes roll back every claim and financial change; concurrent approvals produce one saved copy. Duplicate-only approvals retain existing current trade fields and do not claim or advance finance. Repeated new supplied import keys reject before any write. The 500-account and 20,000-financial-record workspace bounds still apply.

Review import reversal uses recorded ownership and current data. It deletes remaining trades created by that source even after later editing or movement between accounts. Existing duplicate trades and unrelated activity in existing accounts survive. Accounts created by this import are removed with all their current contents, including later manual trades/transactions/holdings/cash anchors. The review exposes every current trade deletion and position change plus the shared account cleanup: affected ledger entries, transfer releases, files moved to the inbox, completed statement review flags, goal links and disabled account-specific rules. Global securities, prices, labels, goal settings and private original files remain. Type REVERT after reviewing every page to approve the entire atomic cleanup. Native balances, positions and budget/goal effects are recalculated from the remaining data.

Successful reversal clears claims and returns the original to ready. A fresh approval gets a new identity cycle; old reversal receipts remain no-ops after reapproval. Final-reset failures roll back all financial deletions and ownership changes. Financial JSON/ZIP restore remaps financial IDs and clears claims; it cannot reconstruct staged-source ownership, and completed sources cannot claim restored copies. Ready drafts whose original account IDs no longer exist need a new correctly mapped draft. Financial backup format 31 is unchanged and accepts versions 1–31; source files/history/preferences remain outside financial backups.

Schema 56 adds trade_csv_imports, trade_csv_entries and trade_csv_accounts plus a unique owner/id index on existing trades, bringing the user-table count to 53. All 50 previous table snapshots and triggers are preserved. Composite foreign keys and immutable claims enforce ownership; bounded counts, ten-source quotas and guarded status transitions protect lifecycle state. Approval reuses investment writer 6; full reversal reuses cleanup writer 38. Normal manual rule create/delete/reorder now uses existing metadata writer 28, fixing erroneous rejection when protected FX/category metadata was present. No financial backup or restore epoch change is required. Bulk statements remain below D1's one-hundred-bound-parameter limit; its documented batch invocation semantics support the complete 10,000-row write (https://developers.cloudflare.com/d1/platform/limits/).

Verification adds 16 behavioral cases, 194 focused Worker API checks, 43 isolated schema-55 → 56 checks and 48 comparisons executing actual pinned Ruby Import#revert/status behavior with association stubs. These comparisons do not run full Rails SQL, callbacks or background sync. The real Worker saves an exact 10 MiB source with 10,000 trades across 500 new accounts, reviews its last pages, reverses the full graph and reapproves with fresh IDs. Checks also cover mixed currencies/FX, source recovery, retained duplicates, current edits, complete later account contents, final-write rollback, concurrency, lifecycle races, old receipts, history deletion, restore ownership boundaries, source quotas and manual rules after protected metadata. All 1,497 behavioral cases and 6,446 API checks across 61 suites pass. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. A fresh Worker reproduces pending source reviews/original bytes, live 10,000-trade/500-account ownership, complete reversal previews, retained PDF originals, old-reversal no-ops and every earlier financial/report/file snapshot. Tests use synthetic localhost records only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Broader Sure parity, staged holdings/prices and automatic security/provider resolution remain in progress; bank and broker integrations stay excluded.


## Complete upstream CSV date choices

Transaction, account and investment CSV mapping now exposes all eleven pinned Sure Family::DATE_FORMATS plus the CSV-only DD/MM/YY choice. In addition to YYYY-MM-DD, MM/DD/YYYY and DD/MM/YYYY, choose MM-DD-YYYY, DD.MM.YYYY, DD-MM-YYYY, YYYY/MM/DD, space-padded D/MM/YYYY, YYYY.MM.DD, YYYYMMDD or DD MMM YYYY. Month names accept English abbreviations or full English names, case-insensitively. The selected format is explicit; ambiguous day/month order is never guessed. The existing strict ISO choice still requires zero-padded YYYY-MM-DD.

DD/MM/YY follows Ruby's fixed 1969–2068 window: 00–68 expand to 2000–2068 and 69–99 expand to 1969–1999. Every mapping screen displays that rule when selected, and review shows the converted four-digit dates before approval. It is an import-only setting, not a global date preference. Existing workflow bounds remain: supported calendar years are 1900–2199, account opening dates cannot exceed the reviewed day and activity cannot precede its account opening date. The existing port permits unreconciled future transaction dates; this update does not change that behavior.

All date choices use the same shared parser in direct and staged transaction imports, account-source staging, direct trades/holdings/prices and staged trade imports. Completed transaction/investment column templates retain the selected choice and resolve reordered headers as before. Private immutable staged configuration retains it across upload recovery and restarts. Original bytes and normalized duplicate identities are unchanged: the same trade/transaction expressed in another supported layout remains a duplicate. Reversal uses the normalized current data and existing ownership claims. No dates are inferred from source text or filesystem metadata.

Invalid calendar dates, inconsistent separators, unknown months, unsupported century widths and appended timestamps/text reject the whole import. Parsing consumes the complete trimmed cell; Ruby Date.strptime itself can accept trailing text, so this deliberately preserves the port's existing stricter validation. All-or-nothing financial approval, source privacy, existing import-size limits and lifecycle/receipt guards continue unchanged. No persisted field, migration, table, writer epoch or financial-backup format changed: schema 56, 53 expected tables and financial format 31 remain current.

Verification adds 88 behavioral cases covering all twelve choices through transaction, account, trade, holding and price parsing plus saved/reordered mappings, calendar boundaries and century expansion. All 28 comparisons execute actual pinned Row#date_iso and read both original date-format lists; they do not run full Rails row validations or SQL. All 431 focused real Worker API checks pass and exercise every choice through direct/staged financial writes, duplicate-safe reordered completed mappings, saved private sources, malformed-date rollback, two-digit historical transactions and complete reversal reviews. All 1,585 behavioral cases and all 6,877 API checks across 62 suites pass. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. A fresh Worker reproduces the normalized financial graph from all twelve choices, explicit historical two-digit centuries, saved completed mappings, exact private originals, pending full-month/two-digit reviews and three completed ownership/reversal reviews, along with every earlier financial/report/file snapshot. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Broader upstream parity remains incomplete; bank and broker integrations stay excluded.


## Custom amount-type CSV columns

Transaction CSV mapping now includes Amount plus income/expense type column, corresponding to Sure's custom_column amount strategy. Map a numeric amount column and a separate amount-type indicator column, choose one nonblank exact value, then explicitly treat matching positive amounts as income or expenses. Every other value, including blanks, receives the opposite treatment. Source cells and the selected identifier are trimmed; case and interior characters remain significant. The UI discovers distinct values/counts in source order and quotes labels so embedded line breaks and spaces remain distinguishable. entity_type and amount_type headings can suggest this control, while ambiguous aliases require manual selection.

This multiplies each original signed amount; it does not take absolute values. With Credit treated as income, Credit/+10 adds 10, Debit/+10 removes 10, Credit/-10 removes 10 and Debit/-10 adds 10. The signed-mode signage preference is inactive in this mode. The mapping screen explains these rules, and both initial and reopened saved reviews show each original type and its positive-amount treatment beside the converted native amount. No indicator becomes a reporting kind or a transfer automatically. The port's existing nonzero/precision/grouping/calendar and account-opening validations remain in force.

An explicit nonblank identifier and income/expense treatment are required together with distinct amount/type column mappings. Debit/credit columns cannot be combined with this strategy. Extra type settings in signed/split mode reject instead of being silently ignored. The UI removes inactive settings when switching modes. Identifiers and source type cells support up to 2,000 characters. A saved identifier absent from a later file remains valid: all current rows use the opposite treatment, and the UI warns before review. Unlike historical upstream configuration fallback, the new port format requires the explicit modern identifier/treatment pair.

Completed single-account column templates retain the identifier, treatment and source type header, resolve reordered headers and require remapping when that header disappears. Failed imports retain previous settings. A legacy signed CSV with an unused entity_type header still imports and remembers its signed mapping. Immutable private staged configuration retains the same settings across recovery/restart; changing them under an existing reservation rejects. The original CSV is retained exactly, including BOM and line endings. Financial JSON/ZIP recovery retains normalized finance, not source-history choices or ephemeral row-type review evidence.

Direct and staged imports, mapped existing/new manual accounts, entity mapping, native-currency precision and automatic rules share the same conversion path. Economic duplicate keys remain date/name/signed amount/notes/kind plus occurrence ordinal; source type labels do not create duplicate finance. Different source types that normalize to repeated identical transactions retain separate occurrence keys, and equivalent signed layouts skip later imports. Whole-file approval, stale/concurrent guards, failed-final-write rollback, import-owned reversal of current edits, retention of existing duplicates and old-reversal no-ops remain intact. Schema 56, 53 expected tables, current writer epochs and financial backup format 31 are unchanged.

Verification adds 38 behavioral cases and 384 comparisons executing actual pinned Ruby Row#signed_amount and its custom-column method with minimal import-accessor stubs, not full Rails validation or SQL. All 92 focused real Worker API checks pass, including both selected treatments, original positive/negative values, blanks/case/trim behavior, rule effects, duplicate occurrence identities, stale/concurrent approval, failed completion/reversal rollback, edited-row reversal, preserved unrelated activity, reapproval/old receipts and USD/JPY/KWD mappings with an explicit new manual account. An exact 10 MiB/10,000-row source saves 5,000 positive and 5,000 negative entries, retains zero net native cash change and reviews its last ownership page. All 1,623 behavioral cases pass. Final API coverage is 6,969 checks across 63 suites: the full run passed 62 suites and exposed an existing foreign-price assertion that selected the first position after a second security was added. That test now selects by security ID, verifies the second security independently and repeats the target assertion with reversed trade order; its 142-check rerun passes. No product pricing change was needed. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. A fresh Worker reproduces typed pending/completed sources and exact originals, saved identifier/treatment/header choices, current sign evidence, rules/native-currency finance, the 10,000-row graph and ownership/reversal previews, together with every previous financial/report/file snapshot. Tests use synthetic localhost records only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded; broader upstream parity is incomplete.


## Saved native Sure export sources

Settings → Saved Sure imports stores an original Sure export v2 ZIP, all.ndjson or JSONL source privately before any financial approval. Original files and extracted finance each support up to 8,000,000 bytes, with the existing 500-account/20,000-total-record workspace bounds. The established direct Import or restore workflow still accepts ZIPs up to 512 MiB while bounding extracted finance to 8 MB. This milestone adds saved additive sources; multi-chunk upstream sessions, larger saved ZIPs, native reversal and saved replacement restores remain unfinished.

Choose the export creation date, whether to keep/use current reviewed investment rates and an optional source currency for numeric rules. These settings, the original filename/kind/size/SHA-256 and reservation identity are immutable. Read source accounts, select explicit existing/new targets and use Review complete native import to save those choices and see the full existing native review. Reopening restores the last saved choices; missing/incompatible saved targets are shown as unavailable and must be remapped. Every target change advances a source-local configuration revision without writing finance. A concurrent change, including changing targets away and back, invalidates an earlier approval even if the financial revision is unchanged.

The exact uploaded original is retained, including NDJSON BOM/line endings or the full ZIP bytes. ZIP handling reuses the existing bounded UTF-8/CRC/structure/path/STORE/DEFLATE/ZIP64 reader and requires version.txt plus exactly one all.ndjson. Both raw-original and extracted-text hashes are verified. Check saved upload recovers bytes stored before a lost final status acknowledgment; retry exact original repairs missing bytes, including after completion, without finance writes. Keep at most ten native sources. Remove source/history frees that slot and its original while retaining all financial records and the permanent native request completion receipt. History/preferences/originals remain outside financial JSON/ZIP backups.

Catalog, review, approval, account balances, paged record changes, loan history, investment FX, existing budget/goal effects and warnings use the established native account-target merge engine. Source review matches the equivalent direct native review exactly. No entity graph is transformed by the storage wrapper. Financial approval, the existing permanent marker and saved-source completion share one owner/revision-guarded D1 batch under restore writer 37. Additional guards bind ready status, exact targets, input hash and configuration revision at the batch boundary and require successful source completion before the batch can commit. A failed source completion rolls back all native finance and the permanent marker. Concurrent approvals save one graph; lost-response retries preserve later changes. A legacy direct caller completing the same exact request can be reconciled into source history without repeating finance.

Completed sources remain completed after financial replacement and never claim restored copies or replay deleted rows. Ready sources with missing original target IDs require fresh explicit choices. Imported raw files do not activate bank/broker connections, price providers, external jobs or credentials. Source history currently does not track native reversal ownership: keep a financial backup before applying updates to existing accounts/metadata. Existing CSV source ownership and recovery remain separate.

Schema 57 adds native_import_sources, bringing the expected table count to 54. All 53 previous table snapshots and triggers are preserved. New guards enforce the owner-local ten-source quota, immutable reservations, fixed extracted hash, versioned targets, allowed statuses, marker-bound completion and deletion cleanup. Completion references the existing immutable native_merge_commits source/input/target identity; a deleted source cannot reuse that completed request. No existing financial field, writer epoch or backup format changed: financial format 31 remains current.

Verification adds 25 behavioral cases and 123 focused Worker API checks. All 33 isolated schema-56 → 57 checks pass. Existing 16 actual pinned Ruby account-target/shared-metadata cases with persistence stubs still pass; these do not run full Rails SQL or the upstream background chunk-session machinery. Focused checks cover actual Rubyzip originals, ZIP/NDJSON byte integrity, interrupted upload recovery, exact direct/staged review equality, persisted target changes, stale/back-and-forth choices, races at the actual transaction boundary, failed final completion, concurrent approval, completed retries, legacy-caller reconciliation, source deletion/permanent receipt retention, malformed uploads, quotas and native limits. An exact 8 MB source imports 19,987 transactions into an existing target, reaches the 20,000-total-record bound and reviews its last page. All 1,648 behavioral cases and 7,092 API checks across 64 suites pass. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. A fresh Worker reproduces exact ZIP/NDJSON originals, saved targets and configuration revisions, complete pending reviews, permanent completion receipts after source deletion and the full 20,000-record graph, together with every previous financial/report/file snapshot. Tests use synthetic localhost records only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded; broader upstream parity is incomplete.

## Reviewed reversal of saved native Sure imports

New approvals in Settings → Saved Sure imports record ownership of newly created accounts, transactions, trades and explicit Valuation entries in the same guarded D1 batch as native finance, its permanent completion marker and source completion. Ownership never guesses from generated IDs, economic fingerprints or restored backups. Existing mapped accounts, already-existing natural-key observations, exported Balance/history snapshots and current-export balance snapshots are not claimed as explicit entries. When several source accounts map to one account/date, the final winning observation determines provenance. The current export snapshot retains the existing adapter's precedence over a same-day explicit valuation; this limitation does not invent a separately reversible entry.

This follows the pinned Sure DataImporter result boundary: @created_accounts and @created_entries are attached to SureImport; Import#revert destroys owned accounts and entries. Imported metadata updates are not a general undo journal. Reversal removes current owned rows even after editing/moving them, and removes import-created accounts with ALL their current contents, including later manual records and activity from other sources. Splits follow parent transactions; dependent transfer fees are removed and surviving transfer principals are released through the existing full CSV/account cleanup. Schedules/payments, investment observations, statements, goal account links/usage/history and account-specific rule/budget references use that same reviewed cleanup. Private originals return to the library. Deleted valuation links are cleared in surviving savings-history evidence; the evidence itself remains.

Before approval, the review shows current account/transaction/trade/valuation deletions, current amounts and ownership reasons, split/receipt/evidence links, released transfers, completed statements requiring review, native account/investment cash and holding changes, positions/cost basis and budget/goal effects. Transactions, released transfers, trades, positions, valuations and effects have independent 25-row pages; the receipt binds the full plan, source approval identity/configuration, workspace revision and day. Type REVERT to approve the entire reviewed reversal. Updated reused accounts and shared categories/tags/merchants, global securities/prices, budget plans and general rules remain. Exported balances/history, holdings, cash observations and contribution checkpoints in surviving accounts remain unless independently owned explicit entries. Previous values are not restored.

Financial cleanup, claim removal and source reset share a writer-38 transaction. Source checks before and after deletion require the same completed approval cycle and successful reset. A failed reset or lifecycle race rolls back finance and ownership. Reversal retains the exact private original, options and target choices, advances the configuration revision and assigns a fresh effective financial request UUID. Reapproval proposes fresh entry/account IDs. All earlier native completion markers remain immutable, and a repeated last reversal receipt returns a no-op even after reapproval. Completed legacy/v64 sources and direct callers reconciled into saved history have no ownership record and cannot automatically reverse. Financial replacement cascades claims without claiming remapped copies; an empty later reversal preserves restored records and does not advance the financial revision. Removing source/history forgets its claims and original while retaining finance and permanent markers.

Schema 58 adds four owner-scoped ownership tables (58 expected user tables) and nullable source lifecycle metadata through bounded ALTER ADDs; it does not rebuild prior tables. Existing populated uploading/ready/complete/deleting drafts retain all prior columns with no invented claims. Counts, cycle transitions, owner-local request namespaces and claim capture are guarded in SQL. Financial format 31, restore writer 37, account/entry reversal writer 38 and existing file/record limits remain current. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.


Verification: all 1,661 behavioral cases and 7,179 real Worker API checks across 65 suites pass, including thirteen new behavioral cases and 87 focused native ownership/reversal checks. All seventeen isolated schema-57 → 58 checks preserve the prior 54 table snapshots and unrelated triggers, including populated source drafts in all four statuses. All 64 comparisons executing actual pinned SureImport#revert and Import#revert methods with association/verification stubs pass; these do not run full Rails SQL, callbacks or background synchronization. The sixteen existing pinned native account-target/shared-metadata comparisons also pass. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. Failed completion/reset and lifecycle races roll back finance and claims; concurrent reversal saves one result. Tests verify edited/moved ownership, complete later contents of created accounts, explicit-versus-snapshot provenance, natural-key updates, split cleanup, retained shared metadata/observations, permanent old requests, fresh reapproval identities, legacy untracked rejection, financial restoration without claimed copies, and full 19,987-entry reversal/reapproval. A fresh Worker reproduces exact originals, current full reversal receipts/live claims, old reversal no-ops after reapproval and every preceding financial/report/file snapshot. Synthetic localhost records only; production financial rows are not used. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Broader upstream parity remains unfinished; bank and broker integrations stay excluded.

## Security-level manual daily closes

Portfolio → Daily closes, and Enter daily closes from an individual position, now ports the dedicated Security::ManualPriceImport workflow. Select one manual non-cash security and enter or load a bounded UTF-8 date,price,currency table. Review up to 1,000 closes before typing SAVE PRICES to approve the complete batch. Every date must be a real YYYY-MM-DD date through the UTC review day, within the port's existing 1900–2199 calendar bounds. Duplicate dates reject across currencies. Prices must be positive, below 10^15 and numerically precise to at most four decimal places. Fixed-point normalization accepts finite BigDecimal-style decimal/scientific forms, optional plus signs and valid digit separators without binary floating-point money calculations or unbounded exponent expansion. Exact strings are recommended for long decimals.

Row validation retains original zero-based API indexes (displayed as one-based data rows). Date errors take precedence over price, currency and duplicate errors within one row; invalid rows do not reserve dates. Valid rows can contribute currency errors alongside other invalid-row diagnostics. The complete error set is returned before any write and is paged in the editor. A missing/foreign security is not eligible; cash instruments reject. All securities in this port are manually maintained; provider status/resolution is not activated by this workflow.

Existing recorded price currencies take precedence. With no prices, recorded trade currencies determine the first eligible currency, then holdings. Multiple trade/holding currencies or no evidence block a first bulk close; existing price history may contain several eligible currencies, but a new batch must use one throughout. Converted trade/holding records use this port's recorded account-native currency; their original quoted FX evidence stays intact and does not silently replace that recorded currency policy. General price entry/CSV and the explicit foreign-market valuation setting remain available for reviewed cross-currency price history. This dedicated daily-close route does not change their existing contracts.

Review includes every quote's proposed create/update/unchanged operation and prior value, date range and counts, affected current/archived account cash/holdings/total valuations, position changes with share quantity/price/value/cost basis, completed statements requiring review and budget/goal effects. Quotes, accounts, positions and effects have independent 25-row pages. Existing observation precedence, stock-split adjustments, native/foreign price preference, total/cash anchors and recorded cost basis use the established portfolio engine. Missing required foreign rates, unsafe calculated values and whole-workspace overflow block approval. Recorded trade cash, activities, holdings, goals, checkpoints and FX evidence are not rewritten; valuation changes can alter calculated backing and anchor-calibrated cash.

The receipt binds the authenticated owner, security, exact input rows, workspace revision and UTC review day. Approval revalidates the entire current graph before one guarded writer-6 D1 batch; natural-key upserts retain existing quote IDs. New proposal IDs are stable across pages and same-revision reviews. Late errors roll back every earlier price write and statement-review flag. Concurrent stale approvals reject; changing a security, currency evidence, input or reviewed day requires a fresh review. A no-change approval still checks its revision inside D1, writes no prices and leaves the financial revision unchanged. Counts distinguish value-changing updates from unchanged closes; upstream's upsert update count includes both. Retried lost responses can review current history without creating duplicate natural keys.

No schema, financial backup version, writer epoch, source-history table, external credentials or unattended schedule is introduced. Schema 58 (58 expected user tables), financial format 31 and existing JSON/ZIP recovery remain current. Prices remain editable/removable through the existing dated-price history. Source limits and broader upstream parity remain documented; bank and broker connections stay excluded.

Verification adds forty behavioral cases, 85 focused real Worker API checks and 74 comparisons executing the actual pinned Security::ManualPriceImport class with relation/lock/upsert stubs, not full Rails SQL or provider resolution. All 1,701 behavioral cases and 7,264 API checks across 66 suites pass; the final focused 85-check rerun also passes after CSV-loading and currency-message refinements. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. Checks cover exact decimal/scientific normalization, complete row errors and currency precedence, owner/origin boundaries, current/archived accounts, stable natural-key IDs, unchanged-only revision checks, typed/stale/concurrent approval, failed-last-row rollback, statement/goal effects, missing foreign rates, all 1,000 closes and the final independent page, malformed/oversized input and format-31 financial recovery. A fresh Worker reproduces the complete pending close review, exact saved quote values, cash/holdings totals, shares/cost basis and all preceding financial/report/file/source/ownership snapshots. Tests use synthetic localhost records only. No production financial rows are queried. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. Broader Sure parity remains unfinished; bank and broker integrations stay excluded.

## Zero-value ledger compatibility and QIF parsing groundwork

Pinned Sure permits amount-zero Transaction entries and split children. The manual transaction command/editor, CSV parser, native adapter, workspace validator and transaction/split SQL checks now retain those entries. The split command also permits a zero parent total. Exact integer/range, date, owner and reference validation remains in force; split positions stay unique and every complete child sum must equal its parent. All-zero entries preserve notes, labels, receipts, rules and reconciliation evidence without changing cash. Offset purchase/refund children legitimately affect the gross reporting lines even when their parent nets to zero. Native expense-sign inversion canonicalizes numeric zero.

Debit/credit CSV fields may contain explicit 0/blank, blank/0 or 0/0; both blank fields reject. Signed/custom modes still require a real number. Zero rows retain occurrence-sensitive economic duplicate keys and count toward 10,000-row and workspace limits. Creating them changes the financial revision despite unchanged cash, while duplicate-only completion/reversal stays a financial no-op. Saved original bytes, ownership/reversal, stale-review and permanent completion guarantees use the existing paths. The transaction editor explains memo entries, and CSV review counts them separately from inflows/outflows.

Transfer commands, principal details, actual bill payments, missing-entry amounts and goal consumption retain strictly positive requirements. Workspace transfer validation additionally requires two nonzero opposite-sign principals in distinct accounts, and equal magnitude for same-currency pairs. Zero roots and counterparts are excluded from transfer matching, including cross-currency manual suggestions. No account provider, external credentials, bank/broker connection or unattended schedule is introduced.

Schema 59 rebuilds only transactions and split_lines, removing their nonzero amount conditions and retaining their range checks. D1 deferral does not suppress ON DELETE CASCADE; see [D1 SQL statements](https://developers.cloudflare.com/d1/sql-api/sql-statements/). The migration snapshots the seven-table cascade closure, temporarily suspends the 59 triggers bound to those tables, recreates each ledger table immediately after dropping it, restores the seven exact row sets, and restores every original trigger. The other 51 user tables and 324 triggers remain in place. The 150 bounded statements total 36,950 bytes; no source data or seed records are embedded. Deferral stays enabled until commit so foreign keys are checked. Historical migrations remain immutable, and the generated schema snapshot/journal agree with the new model. The source-derivation check regenerates the exact migration from the immutable schema-58 history.

The isolated real-D1 upgrade gate covers all 58 user tables with 186,976 synthetic rows across 55 populated tables, 383 triggers and 110 indexes. All 75 checks pass, including failure at the final statement, exact rollback of every table/trigger/index fingerprint, successful preservation of the full graph, zero FK violations, zero parent/child inserts and rejected range/foreign-owner writes. Only the seven affected tables are copied. Main local migration also succeeds. Financial backup format 31 and writer epochs are unchanged.

`lib/qif-parser.ts` is a bounded, standalone parsing foundation. It handles the six offered account types, named and balance-only accounts, first category/tag and all security definitions, ordinary/investment/opening records, split order, raw decimal strings, cleared/check-number context, transfer labels, and skipped-record diagnostics. It offers exactly the eleven Family QIF date choices, source short-year normalization/current-year cap, and source auto-detection ordering. UTF-8 precedes Windows-1252, then Windows-1250, then the source undefined-byte omission fallback, using portable code-page tables. BOM and CR-only originals are supported. Limits are 8 MB original/decoded text, 20,000 records/sections, 500,000 lines, 65,536 characters per line and 100 split lines per record. The parser can expose raw historical dates outside the finance model's calendar bounds; a future financial planner must reject unsupported dates before writes.

All 1,914 comparisons execute the actual pinned QifParser module with blank/date helpers: fourteen actual upstream heredoc fixtures plus zero-entry fixtures, all eleven formats, a separate date matrix and code-page byte coverage. These compare parsing, not Rails persistence or a working QIF import UI. QIF mapping, opening-balance planning, investment conversion, saved approval cycles, reversal and UI remain pending. The parser is not wired to a financial endpoint. Broader upstream parity and hosted sign-in/browser QA remain incomplete; bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

Validation for this milestone passes all 1,728 behavioral cases and 7,382 API checks across 67 suites, including 118 focused zero-entry cases. Two older CSV atomic-rejection fixtures used zero as malformed input; they now use non-numeric input, and both complete focused reruns pass without weakening the rejection assertions. The real Worker checks cover manual/native-currency zero edits, exact zero and offset splits, labels/notes/receipts, clearing/statement review, expense rules, direct/staged/native imports, duplicate-only no-ops, stale/concurrent/final-failure approvals, current ownership reversal and fresh native reapproval, original-bearing format-31 recovery, rejected zero transfers/payments/consumption, and all 10,000 rows at the exact 10 MiB CSV boundary. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. The schema-58 fixture intentionally excludes schema-59-only zero proofs; it seeds the complete historical graph from compatible synthetic proofs before testing the upgrade.

## Saved QIF originals, mapping and reviewed financial import

This section supersedes the earlier QIF-parser-only pending notes. Settings → Import QIF files now offers exact-original staging, repair/recovery/download/removal, editable versioned mappings, paged complete financial reviews, typed IMPORT QIF approval, durable completion receipts, ownership and reviewed reversal. The source parser is now connected to an authenticated, uncached, same-origin Worker endpoint. No external bank, broker or security resolver is used.

The planner follows pinned QifImport's ordinary/investment sign rules, first-definition-per-section openings, named section order, unnamed first-opening behavior, hierarchy-aware selected labels, child-tag fallback/deselection, exact split totals, action-specific cash names and actual Trade.build_name fixed-point labels with Rails 8.1.4.1 BigDecimal formatting. Invalid split totals retain an unsplit parent with a warning. Bracketed transfer labels remain ordinary imported entries, without fabricated paired transfers. Unused category/tag/security definitions do not create finance. Invalid/extra opening definitions and skipped/unsupported records are diagnosed; QIF Prices records remain outside this source workflow. The exact original is always retained until source removal.

Date choices are the eleven Family QIF formats with the source normalizer, short-year cap and detection ordering. Financial rows must be through the UTC review day. The port retains its existing 1900–2199 model bounds rather than Source's rolling 30-year cutoff; parser-only raw dates can be older but cannot enter finance. Original/decoded text limits remain 8 MB, with 20,000 source records/sections, 500,000 lines, 65,536 characters per line and 100 split lines. Maps support 500 source accounts and 2,000 of each referenced security/category/tag kind, with at most 1 MB canonical choices. Existing workspace bounds still apply to the combined graph, including default categories and opening valuations. The exact 8 MB test imports 19,987 zero-valued rows into the 20,000-record fixture workspace; the next row blocks before choices or finance are saved.

Every source account has an explicit existing/create choice and a native currency. Targets must be distinct; requested new names cannot silently reuse an existing account. A referenced trade security requires an explicit existing/manual-definition choice; cash securities are ineligible. Existing security metadata is retained and equivalent new ticker/exchange definitions are reused. QIF has no currency: mapped account units are explicit interpretations without FX fabrication. This differs from blindly assuming one family currency for all targets. Money/quantity/price precision, safe ranges, owner-local references, loan/market-price constraints and the complete financial graph are validated before writes.

Ordinary and split signs follow the chosen convention; investment Buy/ReinvDiv/Cover and Sell/ShtSell cash/share directions follow their action codes independently. Original T totals are authoritative. A coherent fee is inferred only when quantity × price plus fee reproduces that total exactly; otherwise the original trade remains unclassified and requires review. The unclassified trade validator now permits zero shares, retaining original price/cash; classified security movements still require their correct nonzero directions, and cash activities keep their existing constraints. Position cost replay skips zero-share share movements and fallback quote selection excludes them. Cash posts exactly while the original price remains inspectable. Missing investment numbers follow Source's to_d zero interpretation with explicit warnings. Trade memo notes are retained as an extension to Source's QIF trade writer. Raw action, amount, quantity, price, check-number and cleared evidence appears alongside normalized records; cleared flags do not auto-reconcile entries.

Openings are valuations, not imported cash transactions or owned entry claims. Positive liability opening amounts follow Source's debt-positive convention and become negative net assets; the raw amount and signed result are displayed. Explicit openings must precede every non-opening entry. Named-account definitions are applied in source section order, retaining the last valid result; unnamed imports use the first definition. Automatic anchor moves use incoming row dates and honor existing older-entry validation. An unchanged later dated anchor does not rewrite an older local implicit baseline. If a retained later anchor would hide newly imported earlier history, a zero baseline is extended and the prior opening-day total is materialized, including preexisting same-day entries. Current dated observations stay authoritative. All opening/baseline and dated-anchor decisions are reviewed and are retained when reversing entries in a surviving account.

A shared prepareNamedEntities helper reuses the existing CSV category/tag resolver with the same identity scheme; its CSV wrapper preserves current behavior. QIF compares normalized before/after financial schemas so derived display fields cannot become accidental edits. Reviews include every changed record, source-row interpretation, target account and opening, investment cash/holdings/positions/basis/gains, budget/goal figures, closed-statement flags and complete warning sets through independent bounded pages. Rules are retained and not executed during import; reviewed rule proposals remain available separately. Eligible pending-savings matching runs after a successful approval, with any follow-up reported.

Schema 60 adds qif_imports, qif_import_commits and three account/transaction/trade ownership tables. All 58 historical tables remain in place. Sources have immutable reservation/original identity, checksums, encoding, versioned canonical choices and fresh active approval IDs after reversal. Source/financial guards, claim capture, immutable permanent marker and completion are in the writer-37 financial batch; forced final completion failure rolls back all of them. Completed receipt retries return without replay even after reversal, financial restore or history removal. Writer-38 reversal uses the existing full account/entry/trade cleanup graph. Source deletion retains finance and permanent receipts. Removing/restoring financial records cascades claims; restored copies are never inferred as owned. Opening changes and shared metadata in surviving accounts remain; created accounts are removed with all current contents. Sources/originals and ownership are outside format-31 financial recovery. Existing writer epochs and financial shape remain unchanged.

The isolated migration gate passes 574 checks against 186,979 synthetic schema-59 rows, including zero parents/children. All 58 original data fingerprints, 383 original trigger definitions and 110 indexes remain exact. A forced final-statement failure leaves no partial tables or metadata. New checks exercise immutable identity/configuration, ownership/receipt guards, status transitions, history deletion with retained completion and reused-request rejection. The historical schema-58 fixture is explicitly bounded to workflows that existed by its daily-price milestone; later zero/QIF proofs cannot seed values forbidden by that older schema.

Focused verification passes 179 real Worker API cases: tenant/origin/type/size isolation, exact UTF-8/BOM and legacy-code-page originals, upload acknowledgment recovery, mutable saved choices and stale/away-back guards, concurrent approvals, early lifecycle races, final completion/reversal faults, account/entry/trade ownership, current edits/later contents, original receipts, retained openings/shared labels, fresh reapproval and old retry no-ops, exact 8 MB and full-workspace limits, financial restore without ownership, completed-history deletion, closed statements, separate rule application and automatic pending-savings matching. All 1,914 comparisons execute the pinned actual QifParser; 1,461 further comparisons execute QifImport sign/split/name/type methods, actual Trade.build_name and OpeningBalanceManager date/default methods with the pinned Rails 8.1.4.1 BigDecimal conversion and persistence/relations stubbed. These are not full Rails SQL or provider execution. A 57-snapshot populated synthetic corpus retains unrelated records/metadata while planning a QIF memo. Broader upstream parity and hosted sign-in/browser QA remain incomplete; bank and broker connections remain excluded.

The complete regression passes all 1,776 behavioral cases and 7,561 real Worker API checks across 68 suites. Strict TypeScript and production builds pass. The final schema-59 → 60 gate repeats all 574 checks against the exact migration and historical fixture selector used for this milestone. Existing native/CSV, daily-close, zero-entry, receipt, portfolio, goal, budget, rule, recurring-payment, loan, FX and reconciliation suites remain green. All finance tests use synthetic localhost records; no production finance rows are read.

The offline name comparison loads the exact [Rails 8.1.4.1 BigDecimal conversion](https://github.com/rails/rails/blob/v8.1.4.1/activesupport/lib/active_support/core_ext/big_decimal/conversions.rb), matched to the pinned Gemfile.lock, with its MIT notice retained in tests/fixtures. This matters because Rails uses fixed-point labels rather than Ruby’s default scientific notation. Zero-share prices are retained for review but cannot replace a real position quote; focused checks verify unchanged shares, price and cost basis alongside their exact cash effect.

The final QIF rerun passes 179 API checks after warning-pagination and framework-format refinements; together with the unchanged 67 suites this verifies 7,561 checks across 68 suites. All 1,776 behavioral tests pass. A real noisy QIF generates 40,001 warnings: the final page remains available, with warning offsets bounded at 100,000. Catalog diagnostics show a bounded first page and counts, while the financial review exposes the complete set. This is tested in both Node planning and the real Worker. Transactions also has a direct Import QIF entry point. The earlier 75-check zero-value migration gate still passes after the QIF proof is added.

## Reviewed budget copying across currencies

Budgets → Copy previous plan now opens a complete review, including when the earlier and new period use different currencies. Choose any earlier initialized period in the same budget book and an explicit conversion date through today. The latest saved direct or inverse quote on that date or within five prior days supplies exact rational conversion; no provider or live fetch is used. Same-currency copying uses an identity rate without requiring saved quotes. The legacy finance command continues to support same-currency copying and rejects cross-currency copies that bypass review.

The pinned upstream Budget#copy_from! copies the source's numeric spending/income and matching category allocations without exchange-rate conversion. This adaptation explicitly converts native minor units to avoid interpreting a USD amount as a JPY amount. Each inclusive envelope and the manual spending/income targets round independently, half away from zero, to the target currency's precision. Independent rounding can make funded descendants exceed a parent. The review shows that excess rather than silently distributing a rounding residual. Zero allocations retain zero; if conversion rounds a child to zero, the resulting shared-parent behavior is shown. Automatic spending totals instead sum converted root allocations, with any difference from independently rounding the old total disclosed. Nullable expected income remains unset.

Approval replaces the target's allocations, retains an existing matching envelope's identity, copies source rollover preferences and clears target imported carry seeds. Source seeds never become copied money. Existing earlier periods, subsequent allocations/toggles, ledger entries, native balances, goals and FX records remain unchanged. Normal reports derive carry again; currency boundaries still break the carry chain. The review independently pages all source/replacement/removal decisions and every changed period or category report, including carry distribution changes hidden by an unchanged aggregate total. Missing transaction rates and other incomplete history remain explicitly pending. Copying is a plan operation and never creates account money.

Only uninitialized targets qualify. Receipts bind the owner, workspace revision, source, target, chosen conversion date, exact quote and UTC review day. Changes require a fresh review. The client requires all allocation/effect pages and typed COPY PLAN before approval. A guarded atomic D1 batch saves all budget changes and the revision; concurrent approval initializes once, and a failed final write rolls everything back. An acknowledged or ambiguous retry cannot append duplicate envelopes to an initialized target. Approval identities are not financial backup records. JSON/ZIP recovery uses existing format 31 and remaps the copied financial budget graph normally.

A complete review is bounded to 20,000 combinations of this book's periods and all workspace categories, to bound retained before/after report graphs in a Worker; larger books can still set period amounts directly. Review groups contain 25 rows and reject more than 40,000 derived effect rows. No schema migration, provider configuration, new credentials, scheduled automation or access change is introduced. Schema 60, the ordinary budget mutation writer 28 and financial format 31 remain current.

Verification adds seventeen behavioral cases and 161 real Worker API checks. All 1,793 behavioral cases, strict TypeScript and production builds pass. A further 462 existing budget, budget-FX, FX and goals API checks pass, including their recovery workflows. Focused checks cover JPY precision and half ties, exact inverse/direct selection and five-day boundaries, null/zero amounts, automatic totals, independent-rounding excess, source/target validation, stable identities, category carry effects and final review pages, ownership, stale receipts/rates/review dates, forced final-write rollback, concurrent approval, unchanged ledger/other periods, JSON recovery and bounded report graphs. A fresh Worker reproduces complete committed finance, the untouched other owner and the exact pending proposal/receipt. Tests use synthetic localhost data only; hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual browser QA remain unverified. Bank and broker integrations stay excluded, and broader parity remains unfinished.

## Auditable money-weighted investment returns

Portfolio → Money-weighted return offers a private, read-only date-range report with all same-currency reporting investment/crypto accounts or one explicitly selected account. Archived history remains available; an excluded account participates only when selected. Accounts not yet open at the end are omitted. The existing recorded-market-change and source-flow reports remain separate. Upstream's pinned Portfolio::Xirr is a solver foundation, with no current portfolio UI caller at this revision; this port supplies the report adapter and interface explicitly.

lib/xirr.ts ports the actual solver: 10% Newton start with 50 iterations, relative residual tolerance 1e-12, rate tolerance 1e-9, 200-step bisection, adaptive finite negative endpoint, 128 geometrically sampled sub-brackets and the 1e7 fallback ceiling. Newton may solve above that ceiling. Same-date amounts aggregate before sign changes, original nonzero dates control the no-duration guard, compensated sums protect cancellation and immutable public flow/unit state stays consistent with memoized results. More than one sign change is a conservative multiple-root warning. A solved rate is the root reached by this deterministic search, not proof that no other root exists. No-duration, one-way flows and failed convergence remain unavailable; no starting guess or zero rate is substituted.

Financial input uses exact integer minor units. The adapter aggregates same-day capital with BigInt, rejects unsafe nets and preserves the solver's original duration contract. Floating point is confined to fractional-power root finding and displayed rate estimates. The annual rate uses 365 days; the period rate uses the complete selected calendar-day span, including its strictly pre-period opening endpoint. Capital arriving later still uses its actual date within that unit. An extreme short-period annual extrapolation can be unavailable while its period rate is valid. The display rounds rates to two decimals and suppresses a minus sign on a displayed zero; CSV keeps solver precision.

Pre-existing accounts supply negative investor capital at their cash-plus-holdings value strictly before the requested start. An account opening inside the range supplies its recorded opening cash on its actual opening date, before that day's ledger entries. The final positive investor flow is the complete terminal cash-plus-holdings value, a hypothetical liquidation rather than an actual sale. External cash contributions reverse ledger signs; withdrawals are positive. External security movements use signed recorded quantity × stored native unit price plus their actual cash change, with investor signs reversed. Their original shares, recorded native price and saved source-price/cash/FX snapshot remain inspectable and exportable; request fingerprints are omitted. Those values never establish transferred-in cost basis. Internally matched security or cash transfers remain internal in a combined scope; movements across a single-account boundary become external. Purchases, sales, retained dividends/interest, classified reinvestment and fees stay in closing portfolio value instead of becoming external capital.

The report uses the current portfolio engine's dated native/manual market quotes and opt-in foreign-market FX behavior, with independent opening/closing quote evidence. In-period holding/cash/total observations can replace value without known capital, so they withhold both returns and remain visible as history issues. Effective excluded rows, unclassified/generic investment activity, unresolved security sweeps, externally moved securities without positive recorded prices and internally paired security transfers with cash also require review. Zero cash/share memo records do not block an otherwise valid return. Earlier observations can supply the pre-period baseline. No inferred deposit, live rate, provider lookup or price feed is introduced.

Four independent 25-row streams expose every original investor flow, every account's cash/holdings/capital values, every blocking issue and all endpoint quotes. A complete CSV contains raw integer investor/cash/security amounts, account opening/closing breakdowns, solver results/statuses, multiple-root warning, all original flow interpretations, quote/FX evidence and method notes. UTF-8 BOM and formula-safe names survive the download. Same-day cancellations stay inspectable as original rows even though the solver uses their exact aggregate. Revision-bound reads and exports reject stale workspace versions; unsupported/duplicate options and partial-CSV offsets reject. UI requests cancel on changed options/revision and cannot overwrite a newer result. Demo review computes from its in-memory sample, with export disabled.

This feature writes no financial records, cached returns, schema, preferences or approval markers. Existing financial format 31 and schema 60 remain unchanged. JSON/ZIP restore preserves the underlying financial facts with remapped IDs; the report is recomputed. Bank and broker integrations remain excluded.

Verification adds 38 behavioral cases and 240 real Worker API checks. All 1,831 behavioral cases, strict TypeScript and production helper builds pass. A further 555 existing portfolio, investment-FX, market-FX, portfolio-flow, investment-reporting and recorded-performance API checks pass. The actual pinned Ruby solver matches 2,008 cases, including captured source fixtures and deterministic varied-unit/scale/sign histories; its complete 34-test/94-assertion numerical file also passes with minimal test-declaration/assertion aliases and its one deliberate forced-failure stub shim, without loading full Rails. Checks cover ordinary/near-total losses, endpoint and sub-bracket roots, root ambiguity, scale/cancellation, immutable solver state, short-period annual failure, scope-sensitive capital and transfers, fees, observations, native/foreign quote evidence, all final pages, exact complete CSV, authorization, unchanged finance and JSON recovery. A real Worker handles 19,002 original flows/19,000 dated contributions in a near-limit restored financial graph with a 25-row response and complete export; the final local run took 378 ms, not a production latency guarantee. Fresh-Worker checks reproduce complete finance, the large graph, exact reports/CSV/final pages, blocked-observation history and multiple-root warnings. The full prior financial/source/file persistence corpus also passes. Tests use synthetic localhost records only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual browser QA remain unverified; broader parity remains in progress.

## Spending versus normal and a Reports workspace

Reports → Spending vs normal ports the pinned IncomeStatement::SpendingComparison model into a private, read-only workspace. Select a date range, historical/current as-of date, account scope and report currency. Active report-included accounts count; archived and excluded accounts do not, as in upstream Reports. The current Overview and budget screens retain their existing scope conventions. The report can use only native-currency accounts or convert all eligible account currencies with explicitly saved dated direct/inverse quotes and the port's existing five-day lookback. It does not request live FX, prices or bank connections.

A completed period compares net category spending with up to one year before its start, shortened to the true first eligible transaction and scaled by inclusive calendar days. At least 28 earlier days are needed. An unfinished period counts through its as-of date, then averages the same number of days beginning at each of the twelve earlier month starts. Month-end and leap-day subtraction clamps from the original starting date. Windows before available history or overlapping the current period are omitted. Long windows can overlap each other, and zero-spending windows still count in their average. A baseline with no complete matching window remains incomparable. An as-of date equal to the requested end closes the period and uses the completed-period method.

History starts from the first eligible transaction, including income or a zero memo. Opening balances, valuations, holdings and trades never establish spending history. Excluded entries/accounts, archived accounts, ordinary transfers, card payments, one-time entries, scheduled rows after the as-of date and Transfer/Sweep In/Sweep Out/Exchange cash labels are excluded before history and totals. Loan payments and included investment contributions use outward magnitudes regardless of their native signs. Count investment contributions as spending is an explicit report option, enabled by default like upstream; disabling it also removes those entries from the history-start calculation. Bank-pending flags and household sharing are not invented by this manual-workspace adapter.

Spending nets refunds within each category before clamping at zero. Parent totals include descendants; direct rows explain the remainder. If clamped child spending would exceed a refunded parent, its current breakdown collapses to that parent instead of overstating spending. Baseline-only categories stay visible at zero current spending. Historical child normals can exceed their parent normal because each is independently clamped; the parent remains authoritative. The existing port's deeper category trees apply these rules recursively, extending upstream's two-level totals. Parent changes enter the change list only at an absolute difference of at least one major currency unit, not one cent/fil. Ratios are absent for a zero normal.

Normal and change retain exact rational minor units, with displayed money rounded once and ratios rounded to four decimals. This deliberately removes Decimal precision artifacts observed in upstream: separate division of a parent and its children can leave about 3e-30 currency units as a phantom direct normal, then display a false -100% ratio. The port keeps the mathematically exact zero and omits that row. It does not redistribute rounded money to force child displays to add up. Individual FX/split conversions use the same exact minor-unit and grouped-split policy as existing budgets; this differs from upstream SQL's unrounded decimal FX multiplication. No missing quote is replaced with a one-to-one rate.
