Missing or out-of-range individual conversions leave affected current spending, normal and change figures pending, with original account, native amount, kind, root/split identity and counted-window evidence intact. The net of converted entries is not a lower bound: an unconverted refund can reduce it. Quotes outside the actual compared windows are not required, while zero foreign memos need no invented quote. Unsafe combined converted totals reject the report instead of producing an imprecise subtotal. A normal too large for a safe rounded numeric display retains its exact rational and decimal representation.

The interface shows the twelve largest complete positive current categories against normal, labels that chart's coverage, and pages every category/direct row, counted entry/FX interpretation, conversion issue and eligible account independently in groups of twenty-five. Every compared window is listed. Complete UTF-8 BOM/formula-safe CSV includes all streams, exact rational normal/change values, ratios, native and converted amounts, split conservation, original row IDs and baseline memberships. Reads and exports bind the workspace revision; duplicate/unsupported parameters and partial-CSV offsets reject. UI reads abort on changed options/owner/revision/demo state, while changing report currency retains the chosen dates. As-of reads recalculate the current edited ledger, not an immutable old snapshot. Demo comparisons stay in memory and export is disabled.

No financial data, preferences, computed report cache, schema or approval markers are written by this report. Financial recovery format 31 and schema 60 remain unchanged. JSON/ZIP recovery remaps the underlying financial graph and reproduces its comparison. Source-only import histories and bank/broker exclusions remain unchanged. Broader Reports sections, household permissions, assistant/extraction and other parity work remain pending.

Verification adds 30 behavioral cases and 259 real Worker API checks. All 1,861 behavioral cases, strict TypeScript and production helper builds pass. A further 462 existing budget, budget-FX, FX and goals API checks pass. The actual pinned SpendingComparison methods run against independently scoped native totals in 1,050 scenarios and 6,644 category/direct-row checks; the harness explicitly normalizes eight proven upstream Decimal-zero artifacts that exact rational arithmetic removes. It does not run full Rails SQL or bank-provider pending metadata. Fixtures cover month-end/leap arithmetic, partial months and quarters, refund collapse, exact ratios, minimum history, zero windows, sign conventions, deeper categories, missing/stale/inverse FX, split conservation, unsafe totals, every final page, exact CSV, ownership, stale reads and financial recovery. A near-limit restored graph exercises 19,000 baseline/current entries through the real Worker with a 25-entry response and complete export; the final local run took 524 ms, not a production latency guarantee. Fresh-Worker checks reproduce complete finance, exact comparisons/rationals/CSV, every final stream, missing/range FX evidence and that large graph. The full previous financial/source/file persistence corpus also passes. Tests use synthetic localhost data only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual browser QA remain unverified, and the full non-bank port remains in progress.

## Monthly transaction breakdown and complete exports

Reports now includes a monthly category matrix alongside Spending vs normal. It adapts the pinned ReportsController monthly export builder and transaction filters. The matrix uses recorded cash direction: money in is income, money out is expense, and zero memos count as income. Refunds remain incoming; positive loan-payment and investment-contribution entries likewise retain their original direction. This intentionally differs from the analytics outflow normalization in Spending vs normal. Internal investment cash labels remain eligible here. Trades, holdings, valuations and account opening balances never enter this transaction-only report.

Active report-included accounts count. Excluded rows, ordinary transfers, card payments and one-time entries do not. Investment contributions can be omitted explicitly. Select account, category with descendants, tag, native-only currency scope, exact original major-currency magnitude bounds, and within-period dates. Split parents are represented once through their eligible visible lines; category and tag filters apply to those lines. Magnitude bounds apply before FX in each account's own units. A full category path and owned category identity prevent duplicate-name merging, extending the source's flat name grouping and two-level category filter. Amount/count sorting is explicit. Filtering dates intersects the original period without dropping calendar columns.

The source query also includes future recorded entries. The port defaults to its as-of cutoff and offers an explicit include-future option, labeling every included future entry; it does not generate predictions. The full requested calendar includes empty months and respects partial-month boundary dates. Account subtype/tax-advantaged metadata remains unavailable in the manual adapter, so source retirement-account exclusions are not inferred from account names. Global account report exclusions or a selected account provide explicit scope; automatic subtype exclusions remain unfinished parity work.

Saved direct/inverse FX quotes use the existing dated five-day lookback and exact minor-unit/split conservation. The source instead requests Money.exchange_to and rescues conversion errors with unchanged native units. This fallback is deliberately not carried over: missing or out-of-range conversions withhold affected cells and totals, preserve original amounts and quote evidence, and appear in an issue stream. Zero foreign memos need no quote. Combined unsafe gross totals reject instead of silently losing precision. The report remains read-only and writes no settings, financial rows, report cache or approval markers. Schema 60 and recovery format 31 are unchanged.

Five independent streams page categories, entries, issues and accounts by 25 and calendar months by six. The chart covers only the six visible months. Internal category/month storage is sparse, so even the full 1900–2199 calendar does not allocate a dense matrix. Complete CSV exports stream one row at a time: the monthly matrix includes every category/month with decimal target-currency values, while the entry/FX audit includes all entries, account/native amounts, signed conversion evidence, tags, split identities, totals and notes. Aggregate income/expense values are gross nonnegative amounts; entry amounts retain their signs. Pending money is blank with explicit context, rather than zero. Both exports have a UTF-8 BOM, CRLF and spreadsheet formula escaping. They use the same options and owner revision as the reviewed report; page-limited exports, stale revisions and duplicate/unsupported query options reject. Option, currency, owner, revision and demo changes abort pending reads/exports. Demo calculations remain in memory and downloading is disabled.

Verification adds 18 behavioral cases and 237 real Worker API checks; all 1,879 behavioral cases and strict TypeScript pass. The actual pinned monthly builder and filter methods match 420 native-currency scenarios, 650 category/direction rows and 3,375 monthly cells through an independent relation shim. This does not run Rails SQL, live Money FX or unavailable retirement metadata. A further 524 existing spending, budget-FX and FX API checks pass, and the pinned spending comparison regression matches 1,050 scenarios after its documented Decimal-zero normalization. A restored 19,000-entry graph completes a real Worker report in 635 ms locally; this is not a production latency guarantee. The 500-category/two-direction, 3,600-month graph stores only 1,000 populated cells out of 3,600,000 possible cells and streams an 18,093,136-byte complete matrix. Ownership, revision guards, missing/range FX, all final pages, full CSV, unchanged finance and JSON recovery are checked with synthetic localhost data. Browser sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. The full non-bank port remains in progress.

## Income/expense summaries, monthly trends and existing-budget performance

Reports now ports the pinned ReportsController summary, previous-period and monthly-trends methods alongside the existing Spending vs normal and monthly transaction export workspaces. Select custom dates or month/quarter/calendar-year/last-six-month presets, an as-of cutoff, one active reporting account or all eligible accounts, report currency, native-only scope, and whether contributions count as spending. These controls belong to this summary; the other report sections retain their own options.

Summary amounts follow IncomeStatement analytics classification, rather than raw transaction export direction. Standard incoming entries and refunds are gross income; standard outgoing entries and zero memos classify as expense. Loan payments and included investment contributions are outflows regardless of their recorded signs. Unchecking contributions retains them as a separate invested classification with full evidence instead of hiding them. Internal investment Transfer/Sweep In/Sweep Out/Exchange cash labels, ordinary transfers, card payments, one-time entries, excluded rows/accounts, archived accounts, preopening entries and trades do not enter these analytics. Gross expenses are not the net category spending used by Spending vs normal and budgets. Opening observations, holdings and valuations do not create income or spending.

The previous period has the requested number of inclusive calendar days and ends the day before the selected start. In-progress current figures stop at as-of, while the complete previous period remains the comparator, as in source ReportsController. The adapter withholds a previous period falling before the port's 1900 lower bound rather than inventing partial comparison history. Percentage changes round to one decimal with exact integer arithmetic. Source's zero previous total convention remains 0%, with an explicit flag explaining that this does not assert unchanged activity. Savings rates likewise use the source zero-income convention.

Monthly trends deliberately begin on the first day of the selected start month, even for a mid-month summary. The last month stops on the selected end date; recorded entries after as-of stay excluded. Every requested calendar month enters the average, including empty and not-yet-started months. Unstarted and partial months are labeled, and this series is not a forecast. Exact rational minor-unit monthly averages round once for display. The twelve-month chart labels its visible-page coverage; all months remain available in a table and complete export. Historical as-of reports recalculate the current edited ledger, not an immutable old snapshot.

All individual conversions use the existing manually saved dated direct/inverse quotes, five-day lookback, exact minor units and normalized split conservation. Missing or individually out-of-range conversions withhold only the affected classification, period, month, change, net or average, with original native amounts, normalized signs, category paths and quote evidence retained. Zero foreign memos require no quote. Unsafe combined gross totals reject rather than losing integer precision. Entry/root/split identities and current/previous/trend memberships remain inspectable. No missing rate becomes source SQL's one-to-one fallback.

Budget performance is an explicit choice of an existing initialized current-UTC-month calendar plan in the report currency. Reports never calls source's find_or_bootstrap or writes a new plan. It uses the selected book's own active report-included account scope, all account currencies converted to the plan currency, net spending per root category and root envelope allocations. This scope is independent of the summary account and native-only filters. Inclusive child allocations do not add to their parent again. Unknown budget refunds can reduce the known subtotal, so it is not a lower bound. Zero allocation uses source's 0% convention only with complete actuals. A contribution excluded from spending can retain a budget conversion issue without blocking the spending percentage. Household selection/permissions and automatic tax-advantaged subtype exclusions remain unfinished; account names do not imply tax treatment.

The authenticated GET API binds the current owner/revision, rejects duplicate or unsupported options and partial-export offsets, and does not cache finance responses. Client requests and downloads abort on changed options, currency, owner, revision or demo state. Currency changes preserve chosen dates. Demo calculations stay in memory and downloading is disabled. Independent pages expose twelve months and twenty-five entries, issues and accounts; report and budget conversion issues retain their context. A complete streamed UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe CSV contains all summary and monthly classifications, exact averages and comparison flags, every entry/native/conversion/membership, selected-budget scope and issues, eligible accounts and method notes. Aggregate amounts are gross nonnegative values; entry amounts retain normalized signed cash. JSON/ZIP recovery remaps underlying financial identities normally. No schema, finance, settings, computed cache, approval marker, provider, schedule or sharing configuration changes; schema 60 and financial recovery format 31 remain current.

Verification adds twenty behavioral cases and 168 real Worker API checks; all 1,899 behavioral cases, strict TypeScript and production builds pass. The pinned actual IncomeStatement::Totals SQL executes in SQLite and the actual ReportsController previous-period/summary/percentage/trends methods match 240 scenarios and 2,000 monthly rows. Native amounts are uniformly rescaled to minor units, which preserves identity conversion, signs and sums; caller date scopes are independent shims. This does not execute full Rails relations, pending-provider flags, retirement metadata, live Money FX or budget bootstrapping. A further 855 existing monthly, spending, budget, budget-FX and FX API checks pass. The final focused Worker run handles 19,000 entries in 473 ms locally, not a production latency guarantee, with complete export and final pages. The same restored graph produces all 3,600 supported calendar months and a full 10,800-classification-row monthly export. Ownership, stale reads, native-only and budget account scopes, zero conventions, missing/range FX, normalized split recovery, full CSV and unchanged finance are checked with synthetic localhost data. Browser sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA and full non-bank parity remain unfinished.

## Net worth, physical balance history and classified snapshots

Reports → Net worth now ports the BalanceSheet chart-point and Trend behavior into a private, read-only historical report. Choose a period, as-of cutoff, selected-date evidence, account scope, native-only or dated-conversion currency scope, and explicit archive inclusion. The top snapshot is as of the cutoff, independently of the historical period. Period change compares the first and last historical samples; each point compares with its preceding sampled value, and the first complete point is flat. Monthly steps begin on the exact selected day, clamp iteratively at month ends and always include the exact end, which can add a second point in the final month. Requested future history stops at as-of instead of projecting future plans.

Classification follows account type rather than balance sign: an overdraft stays a negative asset and an overpaid debt can appear as a negative liability. Checking/savings share the depository group, followed by investment, crypto, property, vehicle, other assets, credit cards, loans and other liabilities in upstream accountable order/colors. Net worth is signed assets minus signed liabilities. Zero-value groups with eligible accounts remain visible for stable scope/page interpretation; this differs from upstream's all-zero series-group omission. English group labels are adapted to the manual account model. Changes divide by the magnitude of starting net worth, so an improvement from a negative base remains positive. Zero-base infinity has an explicit flag and label, while an exact change outside numeric display range remains a string in export rather than an imprecise number.

Native values follow physical financial facts, including excluded ledger entries and actual trade cash. Split parents affect balances once. Property/vehicle accounts use valuations; ordinary accounts use opening money and end-of-day observation resets. Reviewed loan principal replay retains full-account transfer pairing and contract/observation behavior. Investment/crypto accounts combine actual cash, total/cash anchors, quantities, snapshots, stock splits and selected native or opt-in foreign market quotes. Foreign execution/holding snapshots remain fixed. A total observation infers cash from the holdings value on that observation's own date; a same-day cash observation wins. The snapshot audit shows native and converted signed values, method, anchors and available selected-date holding/foreign-price evidence, without cost-basis lot/disposal graphs.

An indexed valuation reader uses exact cash prefix sums, per-position activity cursors, shared stock-split range products and bounded point caches. It answers dates in any order without retaining a dense account/date or position/split graph. Quote priority, native same-day preference and price-date manual FX reuse the portfolio core. History pages evaluate twelve selected points; complete exports stream points and groups one at a time. Currency minor-unit metadata is memoized per runtime and same-currency account values bypass FX setup. On the large fixture this reduces full-series export time from about 13.5 seconds to 2.3 seconds locally; neither measurement is a production latency guarantee.

A concrete fractional converted-holding regression also fixes an existing portfolio ordering bug. Its quantity comparison applied split rounding in saved/database row order while actual quantities replayed chronologically. Reversing two non-integer split rows could therefore switch an authoritative converted observation to a derived quote value. Both the existing portfolio engine and indexed reader now apply those splits chronologically, matching upstream PortfolioCache's oldest-first split order. No raw holdings, quantities, cash, execution snapshots, basis or statement records are rewritten; affected derived values now follow the same ordered physical history. The regression checks reverse and chronological input orders with the same financial result.

Account balances convert at each sample/evidence date with saved direct/inverse quotes and the existing five-day lookback. Security-price FX uses the price's own date and the saved pricing setting. Missing/range conversion or a native valuation failure withholds the affected side/group/net/change while preserving original values and explanations. Zero foreign balances require no quote. Unknown debts mean known net worth is not a lower bound. An unsafe aggregate display preserves its exact known integer sum; an individually unsafe conversion retains its original native amount. No one-to-one or nearest-future rate is fabricated.

At the original net-worth milestone, archived accounts were omitted by default and explicit inclusion retained their full recorded balances at every date. The archive-date milestone below supersedes that limitation with durable dates and explicit historical cutoff mode. Subtype catalog support is covered by the later account-details milestone. Account classification is generated from type in the pinned schema and already matches; household sharing and automatic prices/rates remain pending. As-of views use the current edited financial snapshot rather than an immutable record of what was known in the past. Overview keeps its existing archive conventions; this report's scope is explicit.

Independent pages expose twelve history points and twenty-five selected-date accounts, issues and quotes. The chart shows only its visible page, leaves gaps for incomplete values and provides group values in tooltips. Full series CSV includes every sampled net/asset/liability/group value and trend. Selected-date audit CSV includes every scoped account, available position quote and issue, rather than pretending to enumerate every historical account/quote in that snapshot file. Both exports stream UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe rows with owner revision, exact sums, scope and method notes. Requests reject duplicates, unsupported options, foreign/ineligible scopes, stale revisions and partial-export offsets. UI reads/downloads abort when options, currency, owner, revision or demo state changes; changing currency retains chosen dates. Demo calculations remain in memory and exports are disabled.

Verification adds 28 behavioral cases; all 1,927 cases, strict TypeScript and production builds pass. The actual pinned Trend and NetWorthBreakdownSeriesBuilder point assembly match 800 scenarios and 7,200 group values. Ruby Date stepping is an independent month-clamping oracle; full Rails validation/relations, PostgreSQL balance-query execution and unavailable archive cutoffs are not claimed. An additional 134 saved synthetic snapshots, 969 accounts and 2,225 dates match established native balance engines; the selector caps snapshots at 800 records and explicitly skips fifteen oversized snapshots. A separate valuation-only Node stress under a 96 MB heap limit handles 250 accounts sharing 19,000 splits without expanding 4,750,000 quantity records (about 34 MB reported heap). This is not a Worker/schema-validation fixture.

The new real Worker suite passes 130 API checks, including 500 accounts and 19,000 entries over 1,523 historical samples, complete series/snapshot CSV, every final stream, archived/negative/zero-base behavior, dated portfolio FX, loan principal, total observations, ownership, stale reads, display ranges, unchanged finance and JSON recovery. The final focused run took 179 ms for a bounded report and 2,274 ms for complete history export locally. Nine existing cashflow, monthly, spending, portfolio, investment-FX, foreign-market, loan-principal, FX and budget-FX suites add 1,388 checks. The original loan-principal test retains its loopback HTTP transport and runs separately from the direct-Worker suites. All test finance is synthetic localhost data. No schema, financial backup format, cached database metrics, approval markers, provider, credentials, schedules or sharing configuration changes; schema 60 and recovery format 31 remain current. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA and broader non-bank parity remain unfinished.


## Archive dates and historical account cutoffs (schema 61)

The net-worth UI now defaults to historical cutoffs. The pinned `BalanceSheet::NetWorthSeriesBuilder` and `NetWorthBreakdownSeriesBuilder` both use `(disabled_at || updated_at).to_date - 1.day` as the last participating date; `Balance::ChartSeriesBuilder` joins an account only while its last participating date is absent or the sample date is on/before it. This port includes the recorded physical value strictly before `archivedOn` and contributes zero on/after it. It keeps source-like account classification and report exclusions. The existing native-value engines, actual cash, loan principal, holdings/price/FX evidence and raw ledger facts are unchanged.

`account_archive_dates` is a separate owner/account-keyed, cascading metadata table. An actual account update from active to archived records today’s UTC date; editing an already archived account preserves its date, and activation clears it. This follows Sure’s current-status/last-date model; a multi-episode archive timeline is not reconstructed. The account editor has a separate revision-bound date command, so correcting metadata does not rewrite financial/opening fields. Clearing it deliberately returns historical interpretation to unknown. Invalid calendars, future dates, active parents, cross-owner/missing parents and identity changes reject. Database metadata writes outside a workspace guard advance revision; guarded operations retain their established single increment. Writer epochs remain 28 for ordinary mutations, 37 for restore/native/QIF finance and 38 for cleanup. No provider, additional credential, report cache, unattended schedule or access change is added.

There is no migration backfill. For archived legacy records with no known date, past historical values are pending with an `archive_date` issue. Current archived status proves exclusion only on the actual current UTC day, not on a selected earlier as-of date. A not-yet-open account contributes zero independently of archive timing. Retain-all mode explicitly bypasses cutoffs, active-only mode omits archived accounts entirely, and unknowns can be resolved by recording a date. Cutoff accounts have explicit excluded evidence, no fabricated native zero/FX quote, and no selected-date holding quotes. Full CSV includes scope, cutoff/date/participation metadata, unknowns and method notes. A scope change can move reported worth without a transaction.

Native full-replacement imports and mapped/saved-source merges preserve `disabled_at`; only a supplied `updated_at` is a fallback. Explicit source dates and offset-qualified timestamps are accepted; timestamps are normalized through UTC, representing the default Rails UTC storage round trip. Family-specific timezones/household scope are not modeled. Invalid, ambiguous, missing or future timestamps produce an unknown-date warning without using import time. Full-restore/native/QIF guards suppress automatic archive-date inference; existing archived status alone never proves a historical day. Financial JSON/ZIP format 32 carries known dates and remaps their parent account IDs. Formats 1–31 remain readable and absent dates stay absent. Financial recovery still does not restore source ownership. Account deletion and source-owned account reversal cascade metadata normally.

The report API adds `archiveMode=historical|active_only|all_balances`; the UI uses historical. Calls that omit this option retain the prior `includeArchived=false` active-only / `true` retain-all behavior. Canonical explicit reports also contain the consistent legacy flag; contradictory flags reject. This avoids silently changing old report/export clients. Independent report pages, owner/revision scope, cancelled client requests, demo isolation and complete streamed CSV remain in place.

Validation: 13 new behavioral cases (1,940 total), 98 focused real Worker checks, and 594 isolated schema-60→61 D1 checks preserving all 63 old table fingerprints, 186,977 synthetic rows and all prior trigger/index definitions. The fixture carries populated workflows through schema 58; schema-60 QIF tables are present but empty, so this is not a populated QIF-upgrade claim. An injected final-statement failure rolls back the whole migration with no new table; successful upgrade creates table 64 without inferred dates. Thirty-three additional SQLite checks exercise valid/leap/calendar/future constraints, parent ownership/status, immutable identity, revision behavior, real archive transitions, repeated edits/reactivation/rearchive, guarded import skips and cascade cleanup. Actual pinned source cutoff builders and chart serialization match 240 accounts/480 boundaries; both SQL join predicates are inspected, with UTC storage simulated. Full Rails relations/PostgreSQL query execution are not claimed. API checks cover ownership/origin/stale/invalid/no-op commands, unchanged raw values, cutoff-day boundaries, unknown past/today/unopened states, native full/merge/saved-source paths, format-32 JSON/ZIP and legacy-31 recovery, reversal and 500 archived accounts with complete 750-row snapshot account/issue evidence. All financial fixtures are synthetic localhost data. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA and broader non-bank parity remain unfinished.


Archive milestone regression also passes 2,524 API checks across 23 existing suites, the separate 116-check HTTP loan-principal suite, and the 130-check net-worth suite: 2,868 API checks including the 98 new cases across 26 suites. Eight suites run through the direct Worker runner; fifteen existing suites run against a single loopback HTTP Worker, preserving their raw-HTTP assertions. A direct-runner/raw-HTTP transport mismatch was corrected by selecting the required transport. Fresh Worker reproduction passes the complete prior finance/report/file/source/ownership corpus and exact current/final-page/full-CSV reports. Existing report warnings were revalidated by rerunning their full source workflows rather than rewriting expected financial data. The main finance persistence corpus now includes archive-date snapshots, and the worth wrapper also verifies complete archive reports/CSV. No production finance rows are read.


## Account subtypes and tax-aware report scope (schema 62)

Account creation/editing now retains subtype metadata for all accountable families and exposes the complete pinned catalog. Investment choices group by region, prioritizing the selected currency’s region and then generic choices exactly as the source does. There are 69 investment subtypes; the other catalogs cover depository, crypto, property, loan and card subtypes, with empty catalogs for vehicle/other assets/liabilities. Unknown source subtype text is retained and visibly identified. Investment treatment derives only from the pinned subtype metadata, with taxable fallback for absent/unknown keys. Depository `hsa` is tax-advantaged; other cash subtypes have no special badge. Crypto retains explicit taxable/deferred/exempt treatment with the source’s taxable default. Invalid/control/overlong subtype text and unsupported or noncrypto explicit tax treatment reject. These are application classification rules, not a tax calculation or current tax-law claim.

`Family#tax_advantaged_account_ids`, `TaxTreatable` and the actual Investment/Depository methods define the exclusion. Recorded tax-advantaged accounts are omitted from cashflow summaries/previous/trends, Spending vs normal/history, monthly transaction matrices, budget actual entries/FX issues, Overview income/spending, reserve-expense targets and payday cash. Report account selectors and authenticated API account eligibility use the same rule. Net worth, physical account values, holdings, recorded trade/cash/FX/principal evidence and raw ledger entries retain the accounts and their money. Taxable/unknown source defaults remain eligible. No account name or old manual cash preference is used to infer a subtype. Known HSA tax treatment overrides a previous included-cash preference in payday planning, matching the source; the preference API cannot re-include it while the recorded tax treatment remains restricted.

Budget cash deliberately keeps `Budget#cash_accounts`’ broader active report-included Depository scope, which does not apply the tax filter in the pinned source. If that figure includes a known tax-advantaged cash account, the UI identifies the restriction and distinguishes it from budget actual spending and payday cash. Existing unpaid-bill estimates, goal backing/reservations and manual planning targets are unchanged. Correcting metadata recalculates report scope but does not rewrite financial facts, fixed target allocations or stored reserve targets; existing explicit reserve refresh remains available. This separation is tested rather than silently applying one account filter to all monetary figures.

Schema 62 adds the owner/account-keyed cascading `account_details` table. It stores optional subtype and explicit crypto tax fields without adding columns to existing financial tables or backfilling old rows. Ordinary edits that omit the new fields retain them within an accountable family; changing family clears incompatible details. Checking/savings remain aliases of Depository and preserve subtype on alias changes. Explicit null clears a field. The form includes the current revision and stale writes reject. Old clients that omit the new fields remain compatible with the existing command. D1 guards enforce parent ownership, crypto-only explicit treatment, nonempty records, text limits, immutable parent identity and revision invalidation; guarded operations retain a single revision increment. Writer epochs remain ordinary 28, full restore/native/QIF 37 and cleanup 38.

Native full replacement and mapped/saved-source imports retain top-level/delegated or nested accountable subtype, and nested/top-level crypto treatment. The incoming native account metadata replaces mapped metadata as an explicit review change; omitted metadata becomes unknown/source default. Unknown subtype imports have a diagnostic; malformed treatment blocks approval. Additional metadata changes can increase review-page totals even when no monetary row changes. Financial JSON/ZIP format 33 preserves optional fields while remapping parent accounts; absent fields in versions 1–32 remain absent. Existing archive dates survive metadata edits/recovery. Source-owned account deletion/reversal cascades details; financial recovery still does not recreate source ownership. New manual HSA cash accounts also begin with the existing excluded-cash preference. No bank/broker provider, new secret, sharing grant, unattended automation or database report cache is added.

Validation adds 81 behavioral cases (2,021 total), 113 focused real Worker API checks and an isolated schema-61→62 gate with 603 D1 checks: all 64 prior table fingerprints, original trigger/index definitions and 186,978 synthetic rows survive, including existing archive dates. A late injected failure rolls back the entire migration. The historical fixture carries populated workflows through schema 58; QIF tables are present but empty. Thirty SQLite checks cover text/crypto/parent/identity/revision/guard/family-transition/cascade constraints and unchanged opening values. Actual pinned Investment/Depository/TaxTreatable/Family methods match 134 account cases and all nine complete catalogs through controlled relation adapters. Tax-account IDs are composed with the existing actual source Totals SQL/Reports controller harnesses: 240 summary cases/2,000 months and 420 monthly cases, with independent SQLite/relation adapters. This is not full Rails relation/PostgreSQL execution. Focused API checks cover owner/origin/stale/invalid writes, concurrent revision guards, retained old-client metadata, family clearing, archive retention, unchanged raw finance, all report/API scopes, broader budget cash, payday restrictions, native full/saved merges, format-33 ZIP/JSON and legacy unknown recovery, 500 accounts and 19,000 entries with every final evidence page and complete formula-safe streamed CSV. All financial test data is synthetic localhost data. Full upstream parity and hosted sign-in/keyboard/responsive/visual QA remain unfinished.


Subtype milestone regression passes 4,942 API checks across 42 suites, including 113 focused subtype cases and 4,829 existing checks. Direct-runner suites cover reports, native/saved-source merges and reversal, archives, budgets, goals, FX, portfolios, rules and contributions. Raw-HTTP suites keep their original requests/assertions; local Wrangler/direct-HTTP proxy rejection/connection-reset faults were resolved using `tests/http-worker.mjs`, a loopback Node adapter that drains input and dispatches the exact compiled workerd fetch handler. No product validation or assertion was removed and no automatic transport retry was added. This harness is not production HTTP/sign-in verification. Native large-import review counts now include an additional subtype change, with unchanged 20,000-record workspace/money/ownership assertions. Fresh Worker reproduction passes all subtype/legacy/native/ZIP metadata, tax-aware reports and final pages/exact CSV plus the complete prior finance/report/source/ownership/file corpus. The main persistence list and worth wrapper retain subtype snapshots and reports for later milestones.

## Manual credit-card terms and statement estimates (schema 63)

Accounts → Card statement adds optional recorded available credit, minimum payment, annual fee, APR, expiry, statement-close day and payment-due day. There are no issuer defaults or inferred legacy terms. Amounts use the account's native minor units; APR is a canonical nonnegative decimal with at most two places and eight integer digits. Days are independently nullable 1–31 and expiry is a supported calendar date. Changing the card currency requires clearing recorded monetary terms first, including explicit zeros, to prevent silent unit reinterpretation. Calendar/rate-only terms can survive a currency change. Leaving the credit-card family clears card metadata. Terms never change ledger values or execute payments.

Calendar/status/interest behavior adapts the pinned `CreditCard` methods. Each month's configured day clamps independently to its actual end; today counts as closed. A due day earlier than the configured close day wraps to the following month even when February clamping puts both on one date. Closed debt carries older unpaid activity. New charges build the next cycle; actual post-close credits/refunds/payments reduce the closed bill with a zero floor. Paid dates advance only after the old due date passes; an unpaid overdue date remains overdue and overdue takes priority over partial. Minimum-payment shortfall compares the entire closed statement with the recorded minimum, matching the source. Simple monthly interest is remaining debt × APR ÷ 100 ÷ 12, rounded once to native minor units using exact arithmetic. It omits daily accrual, grace periods, tiers and unrecorded charges.

The source sums raw account entries, including absolute Valuation entries. This port instead retains signed opening amounts, normalized transactions and replacing end-of-day observations; native export balance snapshots do not retain enough provenance to replay the source raw-entry sum safely. The explicit adapter takes the established physical debt at the last close, including the latest prior observation, and counts only actual transactions as post-close charges/credits. Split children replace their parent once, including mixed signs; reporting exclusions and kinds do not remove physical card activity. Observations are shown separately with current physical debt minus closed debt and post-close net activity. They cannot masquerade as payments. A current-only imported balance cannot establish a prior statement: the dated opening/snapshot and cycle evidence remain visible. Reports use current edited records/terms, not immutable historical issuer bills. Missing configuration and unsafe displayed figures remain unavailable, with exact aggregate evidence where possible. Existing recovery bounds continue to reject unsafe physical balances.

Scheduled evidence selects active owned incoming payment series. Per series, it chooses the earliest stored scheduled occurrence by saved due date, then displays its effective snoozed date, retaining overdue open records. The earliest effective candidate across series is highlighted. The source's cached `next_expected_date` does not exist in this data model: when no stored open record exists, the existing recurrence engine derives the next unclosed occurrence through 2199 and labels it derived. Closed markers/end conditions remain effective and no records are materialized. Native schedule currency, amount and confirmed allocation evidence remain distinct from actual card credits; manual paid markers cannot settle a ledger bill. Autopay is a label, not payment execution. Range failures in schedule aggregates withhold those amounts without erasing the card report.

Schema 63 adds owner/account-keyed cascading `credit_card_terms`, with strict seven-field JSON, decimal/calendar/amount/parent/identity constraints and guarded revision triggers. It does not rebuild prior tables, backfill terms or change writer epochs (ordinary 28, restore/native/QIF 37, cleanup 38). The dedicated command requires the current revision, checks ownership/type, canonicalizes terms and treats equivalent edits as no-ops. Native full/mapped/saved-source imports preserve supported accountable fields as reviewed metadata changes, including metadata-only updates. Missing incoming terms clear mapped metadata. Financial JSON/ZIP format 34 retains terms with remapped account identities; versions 1–33 remain accepted and absent metadata stays absent. Source deletion/reversal cascades new-account terms.

The authenticated uncached read-only report supports a date through today, independent 25-row transaction/observation/schedule pages and revision-bound complete streamed UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe CSV. CSV rejects partial-page requests. Archived/excluded cards remain individually readable; future actual entries stay out. The dialog cancels stale loads/exports on context change and requires edited terms to be saved before loading. Demo reads stay in memory; saves/downloads remain disabled. No bank/broker integration, provider balance inference, payment execution, sharing change, secret, background job or report cache is added.

Validation adds 43 behavioral cases (2,064 total) and 131 focused real workerd API checks covering ownership/origin/revision/no-op/concurrent saves, currency/type transitions, unchanged money, cycles/splits/observations/schedules, native/full/mapped/saved-source/retry/reversal, format-34 JSON/ZIP/legacy recovery and complete CSV at 500 cards/19,000 entries. Unsafe financial recovery is rejected atomically; valid large reports retain exact unsafe interest evidence. An isolated schema-62→63 gate passes 611 real-D1 checks preserving all 65 prior table fingerprints, 186,980 synthetic rows and original trigger/index definitions, including late-failure rollback and no inferred legacy terms. The historical fixture populates workflows through schema 58; QIF tables remain empty. Forty-six additional SQLite checks cover JSON/type/precision/calendar/parent/identity/revision/guard/currency/type/cascade constraints. Actual pinned `CreditCard` methods match 5,952 calendars, 720 ordinary-ledger summaries across USD/JPY/KWD and 100 stored schedule selections through controlled Money/relation adapters. This excludes full Rails SQL, provider logic, raw valuation-entry summation and unavailable cached hints; observation and derived-recurrence adaptations are tested separately. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA and full non-bank parity remain unfinished.

Card milestone regression passes 5,073 API checks across 43 suites: the 131 new card checks and all 4,942 preceding subtype/report/import/finance checks. Direct-runner and body-draining loopback HTTP transports retain the existing ownership, money, limit, rollback and complete-stream assertions. This remains synthetic local workerd validation, not hosted authentication/HTTP/browser verification.

## Property/vehicle details and vehicle purchase correction (schema 64)

Accounts → Asset details adds property year built, living area/unit and the complete optional address fields (line1/line2/locality/region/country/postal code/county), or vehicle make/model/year/mileage/unit. Metadata edits do not change financial values. Units remain explicitly recorded; changing one relabels the number, without conversion. Positive square metres also show whole square feet using the pinned `Property#area_in_sqft` factor 10.7639 and exact half-away rounding. Missing units stay unknown instead of guessing the source's form defaults. Values are bounded nonnegative 32-bit whole numbers; vehicle years are 1900–2199 and property years 1500–2199. Text is trimmed, at most 200 characters and excludes controls. Addresses are free-form, with no geocoding or valuation-provider connection.

The value-history view reports a chosen date through today, current physical value, purchase/original baseline, signed/infinite-base trend, and independent 25-row observation/transaction pages with complete formula-safe streamed CSV. These account types use observations/opening values rather than transaction sums. Exclusions and archive status do not remove individually requested physical evidence. Vehicle purchase price prefers its explicit opening anchor even when an older valuation exists, then its first valuation. Property follows its first valuation. With neither, the port adapts the source's current-value fallback to its independent normalized opening amount/date. First observations and imported current snapshots are baseline evidence, not proof of actual purchase cost or date. Future baseline dates withhold past comparisons. Multiple vehicle opening anchors surface an issue and block a correction while retaining history access.

Vehicle purchase corrections are reviewed separately from metadata, with exact old/new purchase date/amount, old/new current value, and every planned anchor/pin effect shown before saving. A date change must strictly precede all other entries, including zero-value transactions; price-only edits retain an existing explicit anchor date even when another valuation is older. With no explicit anchor, a price-only edit creates one at the source default: earlier of two years ago and the day before the oldest entry. The review explicitly labels that derived date; an explicit earlier date can be supplied instead. The supported calendar is 1900–2199 and new purchase dates must be through today. When a past purchase correction would otherwise change the present value and no intervening observation exists through today, a dated reconciliation retains the prior current value, matching `Vehicle#keep_current_balance`. A same-day purchase correction can change today's value. Later/future observations are retained and future ones do not substitute for a current-value pin. The normalized opening row is updated alongside a represented opening anchor when appropriate; separate older balance history is preserved.

The server recomputes the plan from current owned data, requires its revision, validates the complete proposed workspace (including payment/date/statement/size constraints), and commits the opening row, anchor and optional pin atomically. Equivalent corrections are no-ops. Archived vehicles must be reactivated. Completed statement snapshots remain retained and their existing review-required triggers fire when historical finance changes. No payment/transfer or observation is silently deleted. Financial correction and metadata commands are distinct; neither fetches a price or connects a bank/broker.

Schema 64 adds cascading owner/account-keyed `manual_asset_details` (67 expected user tables), with strict kind-specific JSON, nested address, text/integer/unit/parent/identity/revision guards and family-change cleanup. No old financial table is rebuilt and no details or purchase anchors are backfilled. Ordinary writer epoch 28, restore/native/QIF 37 and cleanup 38 remain unchanged. Existing generic account edits retain details within the same type and clear them when the accountable type changes. Currency changes do not reinterpret measurement units. Native full/mapped/saved-source recovery retains supplied accountable fields as explicit review changes; missing incoming details clear mapped metadata and metadata-only updates persist. The pinned Sure v2 exporter does not include the separate Property Address association; an absent address remains unknown. The adapter retains nested addresses when explicitly supplied. Financial JSON/ZIP format 35 remaps all details with account identities, while formats 1–34 remain readable. Source-owned new-account reversal cascades metadata. Provider IDs, credentials, valuation jobs and provider lock/enrichment workflows are outside this manual adapter.

The earlier pending label for custom account asset/liability classification was incorrect: the pinned `accounts.classification` column is generated from accountable type (Loan/CreditCard/OtherLiability are liabilities). Existing account-type classification already matches; no classification override is invented. Security taxonomy and other unfinished workflows remain separate work.

Validation adds 33 behavioral cases (2,097 total) and 139 focused real workerd API checks: ownership/origin/strict options, invalid/stale/equivalent/concurrent metadata, currency/type/archive transitions, atomic late-failure purchase rollback, concurrent corrections, same/moved date bounds, current-value retention, completed-statement review, native/full/mapped/saved-source/retry/new-account reversal, format-35 JSON/ZIP/legacy recovery, 500 assets and 19,000 records with complete last pages and CSV. Isolated schema-63→64 upgrade passes 620 real-D1 checks preserving all 66 prior table fingerprints, 206,554 synthetic rows and prior trigger/index definitions, including rollback after a final-statement fault. This fixture projects historical finance through schema 58 and seeds prior archive/subtype/card metadata; QIF tables are present but empty. Forty-two SQLite checks cover kind/JSON/text/integer/unit/address/parent/identity/revision/guard/archive/family/cascade constraints. Actual pinned Vehicle purchase/update/keep-current paths match 300 cases across USD/JPY/KWD; Property area conversions match 1,000 cases and strict OpeningBalanceManager boundaries are executed. Controlled Money/account/relation adapters are used, not full Rails SQL/transactions/asynchronous sync. Normalized-opening fallback, calendar/unknown-unit/multiple-anchor safeguards and complete proposed-workspace validation are port adaptations. All finance test fixtures remain synthetic localhost data. Full non-bank parity and hosted sign-in/keyboard/responsive/visual QA remain unfinished.

The asset milestone passes all 5,212 API checks across 44 suites, including the 139 new asset calls and 5,073 existing finance/import/report/subtype/card checks. Direct and loopback HTTP tests preserve existing assertions; the test adapter is not production HTTP/sign-in verification.

## Source-correct valuation-only other assets and liabilities

The source audit found a calculation gap: pinned `Account#balance_type` classifies Property, Vehicle, OtherAsset and OtherLiability as non-cash, and `Balance::BaseCalculator#derive_non_cash_balance` carries their non-cash value unchanged by transactions. Loan is explicitly different and retains its payment/principal accounting. The port previously handled property/vehicle correctly but applied transaction sums to other assets/liabilities. `isValuationOnlyType` now provides one rule for physical account balances, indexed net-worth history, workspace validation and native opening reconstruction.

For these four types, the latest end-of-day observation determines value, falling back to the opening amount. Cash/investment accounts and loans retain their existing engines. Liabilities keep the signed-NAV convention: money owed is negative and an overpaid credit can be positive. Transactions, including exclusions/kinds/transfers/splits/zero memos, remain recorded and retain the existing analytics/reconciliation rules. They do not change a valuation-only balance. Source-native opening reconstruction now uses the first known non-cash observation/current snapshot without subtracting transaction flows. Existing stored opening values are preserved, including legacy imports that may have derived them with the prior formula; review those against original records where inconsistent.

This corrects derived current/historical account values, asset/liability totals, net worth and statement comparisons for affected other accounts. It introduces no migration, preference, record backfill or adjustment. Original account/opening/ledger/valuation/statement snapshot rows and revisions remain untouched by report reads. Closed statement snapshots can consequently differ from the corrected calculation; the existing reconciliation drift detection shows Needs review without rewriting a snapshot or forcing a database review flag. Account cards/forms explain the rule and identify Other as Other asset, with guidance to use a loan account for payment-driven principal.

Accounts → Review value accounting provides a read-only comparison of the previous formula and the source-correct value using the same current recorded facts. It is an accounting-impact review, not an immutable capture of what the old release displayed. It shows signed differences by every recorded date, root transactions with retained split-child metadata, observations and completed statement opening/closing snapshot differences. Same-day observations reset both calculations; later transactions move only the old comparison. Before-opening values are zero. Future records remain stored, with separate counts outside the selected date. Archived/report-excluded accounts remain individually readable and report scopes remain separate. Old formula aggregates outside numeric range retain exact integer evidence while the corrected native value stays available.

The authenticated uncached `/api/value-accounting` endpoint binds owner and optional current revision, validates date/type/unique options, offers independent 25-row history/entry/observation/statement pages, and streams the complete UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe CSV. Partial-page CSV requests reject. Dates are supported through today; controls cancel stale loads/exports when account/date/owner/revision changes. Demo calculation stays in memory with downloads disabled. No exchange-rate lookup, bank/broker connection or valuation provider is added.

Schema 64, its 67 expected user tables, existing writer epochs and financial JSON/ZIP format 35 remain unchanged. All 65 migration SQL files, migration journal, Drizzle schema and hosting manifest (68 files) are byte-identical to published version 78. Old JSON/ZIP facts remain readable under the corrected source calculation. Source-only import ownership and the current saved-source/reversal boundaries remain unchanged.

Validation adds 29 behavioral cases (2,126 total), 97 focused real workerd API checks, 10 comparisons with the actual pinned account balance-type method, and 600 non-cash scenarios over 3,600 dates across USD/JPY/KWD. The harness executes actual BaseCalculator cash/non-cash/flow methods in forward and reverse directions through controlled entry/cache/valuation chronology; it does not run full Rails SQL. Physical balances, indexed history and review totals agree. API checks cover private ownership, strict options, dates/revisions, unchanged reads, net-worth/classification, native opening reconstruction, completed snapshot drift, archived/excluded scopes, format-35 ZIP/legacy recovery, 500 accounts/19,000 records, every final page/CSV row, and exact unsafe-prior-model totals. All financial fixtures are synthetic localhost data; no production financial rows are read.

User-confirmed scope is personal finances only. Household sharing and bank/broker integrations are excluded. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA and the remaining personal-finance port work are not yet complete.

This milestone passes 5,309 API checks across 45 suites: the 97 focused accounting calls and all 5,212 preceding finance/import/report/subtype/card/asset checks. Direct workerd and the existing loopback HTTP adapter preserve their assertions.


## Recorded future-entry balance projections

Pinned `Account#scheduled_entry_flows`, `#convert_scheduled_entry_amount`, `#scheduled_entries_total_money` and `#projected_balance_money` preview the account’s current physical value plus recorded future Transaction and zero-quantity Trade cash. Property, Vehicle, OtherAsset and OtherLiability ignore flows; Loan is the explicit non-cash exception. Nonzero-quantity trades, including their cash/fee effect, are omitted because they exchange cash and holdings rather than entering/leaving the account. Source asset signs are inverted into this port’s signed native NAV convention. Reporting exclusions, one-time/payment/transfer kinds and zero memos do not remove physical scheduled movements. The native adapter stores a split root and its children as metadata; that root counts once, equivalent to the source’s excluded split parent plus financial children.

Accounts → Scheduled projection, also reachable from Balance history, applies that rule to an actual base through today and a chosen inclusive horizon through 2199. The indexed physical-value reader supplies cash/holdings/current marks, loan principal and observed-value bases without cost-basis replay. Today’s entries belong to the base. The default horizon includes all recorded future entry/observation dates; an explicit end date is always retained. A sparse timeline includes the base, end and evidence dates rather than inventing daily records. Archived/report-excluded accounts remain individually readable. A not-yet-open account has zero actual value and does not silently acquire future opening capital. The selected old base still uses the current edited snapshot, not an immutable historical knowledge record.

Future valuations, investment cash/holding observations, security prices and splits stay separate ignored evidence. They can alter actual balances when their dates arrive; this source-style scheduled projection does not apply future resets or reprice holdings. Recurring reminders, payday plans and contractual instalments do not produce payments without actual recorded future ledger entries. There is no write, automatic occurrence, payment, interest expense, price/FX fetch, recurring task or report cache.

Transactions and trades have already been normalized to account currency by the existing command/import adapters. Immutable applied trade conversion evidence remains attached; a later FX edit does not reprice the booked cash. Unlike the source’s silent omission of unconvertible cash, an invalid/unnormalized cash-only trade keeps its evidence and withholds completeness while preserving known partial sums. Missing current physical valuations likewise withhold the projected headline. BigInt sums retain exact evidence when the display range is exceeded. Controlled invalid pure fixtures cover these recovery safeguards; ordinary API validation continues rejecting non-native trade money.

The main loan projection adds full recorded payments like the pinned account method. If principal replay has been explicitly reviewed/enabled, a separate estimate reuses the saved loan terms and existing principal/interest/insurance allocation, applying actual recorded future payments while excluding future valuations. Principal-only linked transfer assumptions remain confined to that estimate. No unrecorded instalment or future capitalization is invented. A gross payment can therefore reduce the source-style debt projection more than the principal estimate. The interface and CSV distinguish both.

Authenticated `/api/scheduled-projection` is read-only, uncached and owner-scoped with unique strict options, valid dates, optional revision binding, foreign-account rejection and independent 25-row date/entry/observation/base-position pages. Complete UTF-8 BOM/CRLF/formula-safe CSV streams all evidence; partial-page CSV requests reject. UI context changes cancel stale loads/exports. Demo calculations stay in memory and disable export. The full underlying financial graph remains in existing format-35 JSON/ZIP backups; formats 1–34 remain readable. No new field/table/migration/writer epoch is introduced. All 65 migration SQL files, journal, Drizzle schema and hosting manifest (68 files) remain byte-identical to published version 79.

Validation adds 31 behavioral cases (2,157 total) and 127 actual workerd API checks. The focused API corpus covers all ten types, actual/future boundaries, archive/report exclusions, retained split children, fixed current investment marks and ignored future observations, separate gross/principal loan estimates, private/strict/stale guards, no-write state/revision invariance, native imports, current JSON/ZIP and legacy recovery, 500 accounts/19,000 future records, independent last pages and complete CSV, exact unsafe sums, and 500 independently paged marked positions. Initial synthetic fixtures were corrected to use the existing zero-share cash-activity fee constraint and the current recovered account reference; no product validation or financial assertion was relaxed.

Six hundred scenarios over 2,400 inclusive cutoffs execute the actual pinned Account type/flow/conversion/total/projection methods and the ActivityFeedData future-date helper across all ten account types and USD/JPY/KWD. Source pending/split-parent/valuation exclusion and custom conversion forwarding/failed-conversion omission/nonzero-quantity omission are exercised through controlled relation/Money adapters. Actual bases use the separately verified port physical reader. These checks do not run full Rails SQL, live rates or the full activity feed’s precedence for existing daily Balance rows. The dedicated report does not replace activity-feed rendering.

All financial fixtures remain synthetic localhost data. Native production verification is limited to publication/access/schema metadata. Personal-finance parity and hosted sign-in/keyboard/responsive/visual QA remain unfinished. Bank/broker integrations and household sharing are excluded by the user.

Selected existing regressions pass 889 checks across nine suites (core commands, migration, ZIP recovery, portfolio, market FX, net worth, valuation-only review, card and manual assets), plus 116 existing loan-principal replay HTTP checks. Together with the 127 focused scheduled-projection calls, this milestone runs 1,132 API checks across eleven suites. The preceding release’s full 5,309 checks are historical evidence and were not all repeated for this additive report. The loopback HTTP adapter drains complete bodies and dispatches the exact compiled workerd handler; it does not verify production HTTP or sign-in.


## Security transfers and sweeps with actual cash

The audit found that pinned `Trade::CreateForm#create_trade` records sweep cash from signed quantity × price plus fee, while `Balance::ForwardCalculator#signed_entry_flows` applies that booked Entry amount to cash. `Trade#internal_movement?` classifies Transfer/Sweep In/Sweep Out for ownership/cost-basis purposes; it does not assert zero cash. The port previously rejected nonzero cash/fees for every one of these activities, so native unclassified records could not be reclassified without changing their money and a manual sweep could inflate the total by adding shares without exchanging cash.

`validateBaseTrade` now accepts retained net cash and separately recorded nonnegative fees for transfer_in/out and sweep_in/out, with existing nonzero share directions, nonnegative prices, safe amount/range and account/native-currency guards. A recorded amount is authoritative and is not inferred again from its label. Existing rows are untouched. Cash replay already posts cashAmount once; fees remain a separate fact and are not deducted twice. Inbound cost remains unknown; outbound movement relieves the running pool; neither creates a realized disposal. Actual/indexed account values, end-of-day observations and market-change reports retain their existing algorithms.

Portfolio activity offers calculated or recorded movement cash. New sweeps default to the source form’s calculated exchange, using signed shares × price and fee. Transfer selection defaults to a recorded zero, which can be changed to its actual net amount. Existing activities always initialize recorded mode from saved cash/fees, including legacy zero and original foreign currency. Changing activity deliberately exposes the choice. Foreign entry retains original source price/cash/fee and the immutable applied quote; a later rate-library edit does not reprice unchanged booked amounts. Cash/fee/price conversion retains existing minor-unit rounding rules. Metadata-only edits do not invent another cash charge.

Direct and saved investment CSV accept explicit signed cash_amount and fee for these movements. A blank/omitted movement cash_amount keeps the established zero default, including old saved originals, generated fingerprints, duplicate detection and pending import receipts. Calculated cash is a manual-entry choice, not an implicit change to historical import normalization. The UI explains that CSV cash_amount is net cash including fee. CSV exports preserve explicit cash/fee and conversion evidence; saved-source review shows the complete row, all final pages, ownership and revision-bound commit, original download and complete reversal/reapproval. Native Sure v2 still omits labels and explicit fees: imported activity remains unclassified with retained cash/inferred fee until reviewed. Reclassification can now preserve those facts and its original identity.

Investment cash is still distinct from spending analytics. Sweep flow labels remain internal in the source classifier, and transfer labels depend on report scope/counterpart matching. Embedded fees are visible in physical/recorded market change; they do not silently become standalone expense or fee-classified rows. Performance and money-weighted return keep their existing conservative movement-history warnings. Scheduled projections deliberately omit all nonzero-share trades, including cash/fee effects, as the pinned scheduled-entry method does. This update does not reinterpret an actual balance as a scheduled forecast.

No financial row is changed by deployment. Corrected cash requires an explicit manual edit or reviewed import. Old zero-cash records remain zero. Schema 64 (67 expected user tables), all 65 migration SQL files, journal, Drizzle schema, hosting manifest (68 files), writer epochs and financial JSON/ZIP format 35 remain unchanged from version 80. Current and legacy recovery retain the existing movement fields and remap parent identities. Existing guards, complete-workspace validation, private ownership, statement-review triggers and source-reversal boundaries remain in force. No bank/broker, household permission, price/FX provider, secret or unattended schedule is added.

Validation adds 22 behavioral cases (2,179 total), 90 focused actual workerd API checks and 600 scenarios across USD/JPY/KWD executing the actual pinned Trade::CreateForm sign/amount/fee methods, Balance cash/flow methods, Trade internal/no-realized-gain methods and Holding apply_trades inbound-unknown marker. Controlled account/entry/cache/lot-book adapters are used; full Rails SQL/provider sync and lot accounting are not claimed. Port actual/indexed cash and recorded market change match. The existing portfolio validation assertion that rejected recorded transfer cash now explicitly accepts it; invalid signs, zero shares, negative fee/price, non-native/mismatched snapshots and range checks remain tested.

The focused API corpus verifies owner/origin/type/sign/range safeguards, cash/fee posting once, actual/indexed/performance/scheduled methods, foreign rounding/applied-quote retention/concurrent retry, unchanged legacy zero cash, native reclassification, direct CSV duplicate identity, saved 62-row complete/final reviews, stale approvals, exact original bytes, reversal/reapproval and old retries, format-35 ZIP and legacy recovery. Initial test-adapter corrections added an Entry identity to the Ruby double, normalized a zero expected value, awaited the CSV exporter and matched the existing performance API’s supported options/concurrency responses. Product validations were not relaxed to accommodate those fixtures.

Fifteen related existing suites pass 1,488 API checks: core commands, migration, portfolio, investment/native/market FX, flow/performance/money-weighted return reports, investment metadata, saved/mapped/template trade CSV, native graph preservation and scheduled projections. With the focused 90 calls, this milestone runs 1,578 API checks across sixteen suites. The historical full 5,309-call corpus was not all rerun. Final strict TypeScript/build/package and fresh-Worker exact state/report/source/file persistence are publication gates.
