Remaining personal-finance parity and hosted sign-in/keyboard/responsive/visual QA remain unfinished. Bank/broker integrations and household sharing are outside the user-confirmed scope.


## Complete source trade activity labels and priced gains (schema 65)

Pinned Transaction/Trade expose thirteen activity labels. Cash-transaction reporting already supported all thirteen, but the normalized trade catalog omitted Contribution, Withdrawal, Exchange and Other. Portfolio activity and direct/saved trade CSV now support contribution, withdrawal, exchange and other alongside the existing aliases. All thirteen exact source labels map to a normalized trade activity; generic cash and unclassified imports remain separate port states. Native v2 still omits labels, so its trades remain unclassified until explicitly reviewed.

For the four new types, signed shares, unit price, nonnegative fee and signed booked net cash are recorded independently, including zero shares/cash where actually recorded. The label never forces share direction: source Contribution/Withdrawal flow direction follows the signed quantity. CSV requires an explicit cash_amount for these new types, including zero; it cannot silently infer a cash movement from a label. Existing CSV/default/fingerprint behavior is unchanged. Exports, immutable applied foreign snapshots, complete source reviews, guarded commits, retries/reversal/reapproval and current/legacy JSON/ZIP recovery retain the same fields. The actual retained version-81 format-35 reader was executed before source synchronization: it accepts its valid graph and rejects all four new enum values rather than silently stripping them. The format remains 35 because the record shape is unchanged and older readers fail closed on unknown values.

Pinned Holding::TradeCalculatorHelpers#effective_trade_price includes priced acquisition fees, while Trade#calculate_realized_gain_loss uses priced disposal proceeds less fees and prior basis. Those amounts are distinct from Entry cash. Positive new-type shares therefore use quantity × original unit price plus acquisition fee for basis; negative shares can realize priced proceeds less fee and prior basis even when booked cash is zero or different. A missing/short prior basis still leaves the gain unknown. Exchange and Other are not ownership-preserving Transfer/Sweep labels and do not suppress a disposal. Transfer/Sweep basis and no-realized-gain behavior remains as in the preceding milestone. No source label changes an existing row automatically.

The port retains immutable applied conversion evidence. New-type priced basis/proceeds use the original source quantity/price/fee and saved rational FX before final account-currency minor-unit rounding, independently of the booked cash conversion. This avoids a zero cash amount becoming a zero basis and avoids double-rounding normalized unit price/fee. Actual cash still posts once from cashAmount; source acquisition/disposal fees do not create another cash entry. Current portfolio values and indexed history keep their existing physical cash/holding algorithm. Existing trade types keep their previous acquired-cash calculation. The source comparisons use matching controlled rates and separately rounded basis/proceeds; they do not model live/exact-date Rails rate lookup, mutable rate drift or historical holding selection.

Flow reporting maps Contribution/Withdrawal to external security flows in signed share direction. Exchange remains internal in the source flow classifier while its disposal can realize a gain; Other uses the source internal-trade fallback. Money-weighted capital retains recorded share value plus actual cash, not priced gain or cost. Explicit Other/Exchange history retains a conservative review warning for economic meaning/paired events, with priced gains still visible separately. Spending budgets still exclude investment trades. Contribution-progress history safeguards and checkpoints remain separate. Source scheduled projection still ignores nonzero-share trades and counts zero-share booked cash, regardless of the label.

Schema 65 adds no table, column, row or label backfill: 67 user tables remain expected. Three owner-bound triggers protect insertion, financial replacement and deletion of the new-type rows from older investment editors; harmless metadata-only changes and old known activity rows retain compatibility. Manual/direct investment and saved trade-import writers advance from epoch 6 to 8. Six original saved-source/template/claim/completion guards accept epochs 6 and 8 while preserving their owner, immutable-scope, status, original, claim-count and completion conditions. Old epoch 6 still cannot financially edit new-type rows. Ordinary writer 28, recovery/native/QIF 37 and reversal/cleanup 38 remain unchanged. Completed-statement review uses the existing activity/financial triggers. All 65 prior SQL migrations, Drizzle schema and hosting manifest (67 files) and prior journal entries are byte-identical to version 81; the new migration and appended journal entry contain this change.

Validation adds 26 behavioral cases (2,205 total), 94 focused actual workerd API checks, 600 source priced-basis/proceeds scenarios across native/foreign USD/JPY/KWD and 4,032 actual pinned portfolio-flow classification cases. The harness executes source Holding price/fee/conversion and Trade internal/disposal/basis-currency methods through controlled Money/rate/holding adapters, not full Rails SQL or historical holding selection. It checks all thirteen source labels and distinguishes Exchange from Transfer. Focused API tests cover owner/origin/type/range, recorded cash versus priced gains, signed external/internal flows, reviewed return warnings, exact foreign rounding/applied quotes, old/current editor guards, native reclassification, explicit CSV cash, saved 62-row complete/final reviews, stale approvals, late-fault atomic rollback, retries/reversal/reapproval, exact originals and format-35 ZIP/legacy recovery.

The final isolated schema-64→65 gate passes 627 real-D1 checks preserving every prior user-table fingerprint (206,554 synthetic rows), all prior indexes and untouched triggers, the exact six intended guard extensions, and full rollback on a final-statement fault. Another 107 SQLite checks cover old-editor insert/update/delete protection, safe metadata edits, current investment/recovery epochs, owner-bound guards and old activity compatibility. Initial integration failures caught the saved-import writer and its six epoch-specific source/template guards; all were updated together rather than loosening the new-row protection. Test-adapter corrections covered CSV validation scope, CLI error text and a deterministic old-editor fixture row. No financial assertion or late-failure check was relaxed.

Related regression and fresh-Worker publication gates cover the prior movement workflow, finance/import/portfolio/FX/report/ownership corpus and exact saved state/source/file evidence. All financial data stays synthetic localhost data; native production checks are publication/access/schema metadata only. Remaining personal-finance work and hosted sign-in/keyboard/responsive/visual QA remain unfinished. Bank/broker integrations and household sharing remain excluded by the user.

## Reviewed owned merchant merging (schema 66)

Merchants now offers a reviewed merge into a chosen survivor. The complete private plan covers all source merchants and transaction roots, split lines, recurring definitions, supported rule rewrites and retained original-rule mentions. Each evidence stream has independent 25-row pages; selection supports search and 50 choices per page, including all matching choices. The full BOM/CRLF, formula-safe CSV contains every source and reference, all rule before/after configurations, original-mention notes and limitations. Root/split metadata is listed independently and is not summed as financial activity. The UI requires acknowledging the review and typing MERGE. No automatic undo or persisted merge-log table is added; retain a financial backup when original merchant identities may be needed.

The survivor retains its complete ID/name/color/website. Source references move even on archived accounts, excluded/reconciled entries, zero memos and transfer legs. Transaction and split identity, money, tags, categories, protection fields, import keys, reconciled evidence, statement snapshots, allocation links and original receipt bytes stay intact. Changed merchant fields gain the existing manual merchant protection; explicit merging can replace a previously protected merchant value while retaining other locks. Full proposed-workspace validation precedes the write.

Simple/compound merchant conditions, including stored null-test operands, compare selected IDs case-insensitively as in pinned `Rule.replace_merchant_references!`. Direct merchant actions and this port's split-action merchant fields move to the survivor. Active state, order, groups, blocked reason and source JSON remain unchanged. Rules are not executed. Disabled original-only rules remain disabled; original ID/name mentions appear as retained evidence without reinterpreting unsupported configuration. CSV/QIF/native originals, saved mappings and source ownership/reset/reversal history are not rewritten. Reapproving an old merchant CSV can create that original name with a fresh identity; no implicit alias is installed.

Pinned `Merchant::Merger` reassigns family transaction references, marks manual modifications, deletes family-owned sources, retains provider/global merchants and rewrites supported rule references. This port has only private owned merchants. Its recurring definitions are reassigned with saved occurrences, partial payments, allocations, matching settings, price and lifecycle records preserved. Existing D1 observation triggers refresh their edit clock at commit. That preservation deliberately differs from upstream `Merchant` destruction callbacks, which can destroy dependent recurring definitions. Future matching/detection can broaden through the shared identity; overlapping definitions need separate review. Global provider retention/sync flags, Rails destruction callbacks and rule notification baselines are not claimed. Split references and split-rule actions are explicit port extensions.

Reviews and exports are authenticated, owner-scoped, same-origin and private/no-store. A receipt binds owner, workspace revision, complete selection, survivor, effects, rule evidence and affected recurring state. Selection changes, stale revisions and tampered receipts reject. Commit rewrites rules, then transaction/split/recurring references, then deletes sources in one guarded D1 batch. The dedicated epoch-28 context skips only the ordinary merchant-deletion rule invalidator for this operation; ordinary deletion retains its prior behavior. Changed merchant fields still receive normal manual locks. An injected last-stage deletion failure rolls back the entire graph. A simultaneous merge commits once; repeating the old revision rejects.

Schema 66 adds three `(owner_id, merchant_id)` indexes to transactions, split lines and recurring entries, one merge-context epoch guard and a scoped predicate on the old ordinary-deletion trigger. No table, column or row backfill is introduced. All 66 prior SQL migrations and the hosting manifest are byte-identical to version 82; prior journal entries stay unchanged. Financial JSON/ZIP remains format 35 with 67 expected user tables. Three Drizzle index declarations mirror the SQL.

Verification: all 2,221 behavioral tests pass, including 16 new merge tests. The focused suite passes 99 API checks, including actual 5,000 merchants, 4,999 merged sources, 14,500 transaction references and 200 rewritten rules with complete final evidence pages and CSV. It covers exact survivor metadata, root/split protections, saved partial recurring payment/clock behavior, unchanged money, supported/blocked rules without execution, owner/auth/origin/type/range controls, stale/tampered reviews, late-fault rollback, concurrency, retained original receipt bytes, merchant CSV reset/reapproval and exact original bytes, plus remapped ZIP and legacy JSON recovery. Ten related existing suites pass 1,150 API checks: core, migration, ledger, rules, merchant/rule CSV, native graph preservation, recurring lifecycle/matcher and receipts. Together this milestone runs 1,249 checks across eleven suites; the historical complete corpus was not all repeated.

Three hundred scenarios with 12,000 transaction references across USD/JPY/KWD execute the actual pinned merchant merger, Entry manual-modification marking and Rule reference replacement using controlled relation/model adapters. Full Rails SQL, destruction callbacks, provider/global merchant behavior and notification baselines are not claimed. An isolated real-D1 schema-65→66 upgrade passes 630 checks preserving all 67 prior table fingerprints and 206,554 synthetic rows, all prior indexes and untouched triggers, with the exact one intended old-trigger predicate change and late-failure rollback. Eighteen SQLite checks cover ordinary deletion, retained originals, merge context, wrong-epoch rejection, owner isolation, manual protections/unchanged money and indexed lookup plans.

Strict TypeScript and the final helper build/package pass. A fresh Worker reproduces the complete merge graph, full-limit merge, recurring payments/rules/locks, ZIP/legacy remapping, current pending review/receipt/full CSV, retained source original/reset/reapproval and exact receipt bytes, plus the complete prior finance/report/import/file persistence corpus. All finance fixtures are synthetic localhost data; hosted verification uses publication/access/database metadata only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. The broader personal-finance port remains in progress. Bank/broker integrations and household sharing are excluded by the user.

## Reviewed category merging and budget collisions (schema 67)

Categories now offers a reviewed merge into a selected survivor. It retains that category's ID, name, color, icon and parent; selected sources are removed atomically after references move. Pinned `Category::Merger` hierarchy checks are preserved: an ancestor cannot merge into its descendant, and a source with children cannot merge into a subcategory. Remaining direct children reparent without color/icon changes, matching the source bulk update rather than normal color-inheritance callbacks. The port's existing valid hierarchies up to ten levels remain supported; the source's normal category editor allows two levels.

Every selected transaction/split/recurring category reference moves, including archived, excluded, zero-value and reconciled ledger records. Changed transaction and split category fields receive manual protection while other locks, money, identity, statements, tags, merchants, receipts and import keys remain. Saved recurring occurrences, partial payments, allocations, matcher settings and lifecycle/price history stay attached; D1 observation clocks refresh. The source transaction reassigner supersedes automatic category claims and touches affected entries. The port adapts that explicit categorization protection to its existing category-field locks and workspace revision. It does not claim Rails row locking/cache invalidation or automatic-provider claim models.

Budget envelopes merge separately within each period: inclusive allocated amounts are summed as the source sums `budgeted_spending`. Existing target envelope identity and rollover/imported-carry settings survive. Without a target, source categories are processed in stable ID order and the first supplies those settings. Colliding envelopes are removed, retaining every original setting in the review/CSV rather than adding retired carry snapshots. Unused retired carry totals beyond safe integer range remain exact audit strings and do not block an otherwise valid merge. Amounts never combine across periods, books or currencies. Automatic period totals refresh from root allocations; fixed totals remain fixed. No hierarchy-wide reserve reflow is silently substituted for the source's inclusive sums. Combining nested allocations can increase root funding or leave another ancestor over-allocated. Historical refund netting, shared availability and rollover recalculate through the existing budget engine; before/after stored allocations and root plan totals are reviewed, not a frozen projection of every future rollover. A test demonstrates changed refund-net spending/carry while account money remains identical.

Supported simple/compound category conditions, direct category actions and split-rule category fields move without running rules or changing active state/order/source JSON. This is an explicit port extension: the pinned category merger has no category rule-replacement call. It preserves this port's typed referential integrity and rule continuity. Blocked original-only configurations remain disabled and unchanged. Original CSV/QIF/native/rule bytes, saved mappings and approval/reset/reversal history stay unchanged. Reapproval can recreate an old category name with a fresh ID; no automatic alias is installed. A completed merge has no automatic undo or special merge-log table; download a financial backup if the old category/envelope/carry configuration may be needed.

Independent 25-row review streams cover sources, transaction/split/recurring references, rules, retained original mentions, reparented children, envelope collisions and affected plan totals. Selection supports search and 50 choices per page. The full BOM/CRLF, formula-safe CSV contains every stream, with full before/after envelope settings; root and split references are not financially double-counted. Confirmation requires acknowledgement and MERGE. Owner-scoped reads, same-origin commands, strict body/UUID/page bounds, private/no-store responses and a receipt binding the full selection/revision/evidence protect the operation. Rules and envelope collisions update before reference moves/source deletion in one epoch-28 batch. Late deletion failure rolls everything back; stale/tampered receipts and old retries reject, and concurrent commits apply once.

Schema 67 adds owner/category indexes on transactions, split lines, recurring entries, legacy budgets and budget envelopes, plus an owner/parent category index. One existing ordinary-category-deletion invalidator gains a narrow owner/epoch/prefix predicate; a dedicated merge guard rejects wrong epochs. Ordinary deletion and the previous merchant-merge context retain their behavior. No table, column or row backfill is introduced; all 67 prior SQL migrations, the hosting manifest and journal prefix remain unchanged. Six Drizzle declarations mirror the indexes. There are still 67 expected user tables and financial JSON/ZIP format 35; current/legacy recovery remaps merged references and envelope settings.

Verification: all 2,251 behavioral tests pass, including 30 new merge cases. The focused suite passes 107 API checks covering hierarchy restrictions/31 reparented children, 62 budget periods and all final envelope/plan pages, exact surviving settings, partial recurring payments, root/split locks, unchanged money and receipt bytes, supported/blocked rules without execution, owner/origin/type/body/page controls, stale/tampered reviews, last-stage fault rollback, simultaneous commits, source reset/reapproval/original bytes, ZIP/legacy remapping, and a complete 2,000-category graph merging 1,999 sources with 14,500 transaction references/200 rules. Fourteen related existing suites pass 1,621 API checks: core, migration, ledger, rules, budgets, budget FX/copy, category/rule CSV, merchant merge, native graph preservation, recurring lifecycle/matcher and receipts. Together this milestone runs 1,728 API checks across fifteen suites; the historical full corpus was not all repeated.

Three hundred scenarios (240 valid/60 rejected) and 12,000 transaction references with separate USD/JPY/KWD budget periods execute actual pinned `Category::Merger` and `Transaction.reassign_category!`: hierarchy validation, child movement, target exclusion, inclusive sums, envelope identity/settings and exact touched/superseded IDs. Controlled model/relation/SQL-execution adapters are used; full Rails SQL/locking/cache/destruction callbacks are not claimed. An isolated real-D1 schema-66→67 upgrade passes 634 checks preserving all 67 prior table fingerprints and 206,554 synthetic rows, prior indexes and untouched triggers, with the exact intended old-trigger predicate change and late-failure rollback. Twenty-four SQLite checks cover ordinary-delete behavior, retained originals, manual locks/unchanged money, owner/context/epoch isolation and all six indexed lookup plans.

Strict TypeScript, final helper build/package and fresh-Worker complete state/budget-review/final-page/source/receipt persistence are publication gates, together with the complete prior finance/report/import/file persistence corpus. All financial fixtures remain synthetic localhost data; native production verification reads publication/access/schema metadata only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. The broader personal-finance port remains in progress. Bank/broker integrations and household sharing remain excluded by the user.

## Private XLSX extraction into reviewed transaction imports (schema 68)

Files now offers **Import spreadsheet rows** for a ready XLSX original. One selected worksheet/range is converted to deterministic UTF-8 CSV, then fed into the existing single/multi-account, account-creation, category/tag mapping, rules, staged approval and source-ownership reversal workflow. Extraction is read-only with respect to financial records; it does not create entries, infer statement balances, match existing ledger entries to a statement, or mark transactions reconciled. The pinned Sure `AccountStatement::MetadataDetector` records XLSX extraction as unsupported; this deterministic converter is a port extension that reuses the already-ported reviewed CSV workflow, not a claim to port an upstream XLSX parser or PDF/AI extractor.

The parser reads standard STORE/DEFLATE OOXML parts through the existing ZIP directory/CRC/size checks, with a streamed chunk reader shared by backup recovery. SAX XML processing rejects malformed documents, DTDs/external entities, external worksheet/shared-string targets, duplicate sheet relationships, unsafe paths and missing required parts. Both transitional and strict worksheet namespaces, shared/inline/rich strings, sparse coordinates, explicit numeric/Boolean/ISO-date cells and hidden rows are supported. Misleading worksheet dimensions do not allocate absent rows/columns. Chart-only sheets are listed as excluded; a separate data worksheet remains importable. Text is not numerically coerced. Numeric scientific notation expands through decimal string operations, retaining precision beyond binary-float limits. Display formats/styles are deliberately not applied; financial currency precision/ranges still validate through the normal import planner.

Select numeric date columns explicitly. The workbook's 1900/1904 setting controls serial conversion. Nonzero time fractions stop by default; date-only conversion is an explicit choice and its count is retained. Selected ISO date/time cells follow the same policy without inferred timezone shifts. The fictitious 1900 leap day is rejected, and dates must remain inside the port's supported 1900–2199 calendar. Microsoft's [date-system documentation](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/excel/date-systems-in-excel) and [1900 leap-year explanation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/troubleshoot/microsoft-365-apps/excel/wrongly-assumes-1900-is-leap-year) describe these compatibility boundaries.

Formulas are never evaluated. Default extraction stops at formula cells/result ranges; explicit cached mode reads saved results, which may be stale, and rejects unavailable results. Shared/array result ranges cannot bypass the choice by placing their anchor outside the selection. Overlapping ranges use a row-event/column-coverage sweep and compact bitmaps rather than multiplying work by ranges × rows × columns. Cached empty string results remain empty; no missing amount is invented. Literal formula-like strings remain data. Hidden selected rows are included and blank data rows are omitted. Original worksheet row coordinates, options, selected sheet, parser version, checksums and conversion/cache/exclusion counts remain in the extraction evidence.

Limits: 25 MiB original, 100 MiB total expanded data, 2,000 ZIP entries, 100 data worksheets, 1 MiB each for workbook/relationship XML, 20 MiB shared-string XML, 10,000 populated data rows, 100 selected columns, 2,000 characters per cell/header, 1,100,000 scanned cell elements, 3,000,000 XML nodes and 128 XML levels. Derived CSV is capped at 10 MiB of UTF-8 bytes. Explicit row ranges may span sparse blank distances; the record limit applies to populated rows. Header cells must be distinct and nonempty. Selected merged ranges, XLS/XLSM, encrypted/macro workbooks, recalculation, rich visual formatting, PDF/OCR and automatic statement reconciliation remain outside this converter. Larger/unsupported originals may still be retained by the existing file vault within its own upload rules.

The server authenticates the owner, verifies the file-library reference/R2 owner metadata and complete original SHA-256, then parses only local package parts. Catalog/extraction/audit responses are private/no-store; POST extraction requires the same origin and strict bounded options. Before reserving a derived CSV, the server repeats extraction from that saved original and checks its exact derived checksum/byte count. Clients cannot submit an authoritative manifest. A new nullable `csv_imports.workbook_source_json` holds the immutable original-file snapshot, options, parser metadata and complete row map. SQL insert guards bind it to the same owner's ready XLSX and source/derived checksum/size/row-count context; update guards prevent replacing/removing the evidence or changing its owner/source fingerprint. A file metadata race during reservation rejects rather than rebinding evidence.

Existing plain CSV sources retain NULL metadata and unchanged public responses. No new table, financial field, source ownership model, writer epoch or financial backup generation is introduced. All 68 prior SQL migrations, the hosting manifest and prior journal entries stay unchanged. Every prior dependency record is unchanged; only pinned saxes 6.0.0 and xmlchars 2.2.0 are added. Full ISC/MIT license notices are distributed as public text files and corresponding source. XML parsing uses the pinned saxes API; no external entity resolver or network loader is registered.

Files opens the extraction choices and the normal import form with its generated file already attached; changing a target account retains that derived file. The file input does not require a second local file selection for this preloaded path. Stage reviews display CSV/XLSX row coordinates, original checksums and a complete formula-safe extraction/row-audit download. An uploading worksheet draft can recreate its upload from the saved original; the regenerated checksum and size must match the saved draft. A lost ready marker is recoverable through the existing R2 check. Commit/reversal/reapproval use unchanged whole-file atomic and idempotent source ownership. Removing the original leaves its verified snapshot and saved derived CSV usable; deleting source history retains financial records under the existing rules. File-library originals participate in financial ZIP recovery, while staged CSV/history/provenance remain outside financial backups. Cross-owner financial recovery remaps records without importing source ownership.

Verification: all 2,278 behavioral tests pass, including 27 XLSX cases. The focused suite passes 126 API checks: owner/origin/type/body/range controls, exact mapped USD/JPY values and dates, explicit formula/time choices, original/checksum verification, immutable source/row provenance, stale approvals, final-fault rollback, concurrency/retries, reversal/reapproval, regenerated uploads and lost-ready recovery, audit retention after original removal, ZIP/remapped financial records, malformed XML no-op, and complete 10,000-row extraction/import/reversal/final pages. Fifteen related existing suites pass 1,849 API checks covering staged CSV, core/migration, reversal, account/label mapping/creation, templates, all date/type choices, files/receipts, ZIP recovery, saved native sources and QIF imports. This milestone totals 1,975 API checks across sixteen suites; the historical full corpus was not all repeated.

An independent Python corpus verifies 600 calendar-to-serial cases across both date systems through 2199, plus 1,000 Python Decimal comparisons; the source format's phantom leap day is tested separately. The isolated real-D1 schema-67→68 upgrade passes 641 checks: every one of 67 original-column table fingerprints and 206,554 synthetic rows is retained, all old indexes/triggers remain exact, the new column is nullable with prior values NULL, and a late migration fault rolls the DDL back completely. Twenty-three SQLite checks cover legacy NULL compatibility, source shape/checksum/size/owner/availability, immutable evidence/fingerprint, harmless metadata edits and retained evidence after original deletion. These controlled OOXML fixtures/protocol tests do not claim full Excel rendering or universal workbook compatibility.

Strict TypeScript, final helper build/package and fresh-Worker exact original/catalog/extraction, converted finance, complete source/row evidence, pending reviews/final pages/full CSV/audit, removed-original retention and the prior finance/report/import/file persistence corpus are publication gates. All financial fixtures are synthetic localhost data. Native production verification reads publication/access/schema metadata only; hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. The broader personal-finance port remains in progress. Bank/broker integrations and household sharing remain excluded by the user.

The initial runtime audit used current [D1 limits](https://developers.cloudflare.com/d1/platform/limits/): batches count as one invocation query, while per-statement limits still apply. The existing guarded batch architecture already follows that contract; no speculative batch-limit rewrite or schema relaxation was introduced.

## Bounded regular-expression rules for manual finance

Pinned `Rule::ConditionFilter` executes `matches_regex` as PostgreSQL `~*` on unnormalized fields; `Rule::SafeRegex` limits patterns to 200 characters, rejects backreferences and uses database compilation/execution timeouts. D1 does not provide that PostgreSQL engine. This milestone explicitly adapts the workflow using pinned [RE2JS 2.10.0](https://github.com/le0pard/re2js), rather than treating JavaScript backtracking regex or RE2 as an identical PostgreSQL dialect. Patterns on transaction descriptions and notes search the stored text, preserve interior whitespace and default to case-insensitive, dot-matches-newline matching. Existing ledger/CSV input trimming still applies. Supported inline RE2 flags can change those defaults. Unicode simple folding, classes, escapes, anchors and empty stored strings follow RE2 semantics; PostgreSQL locale/ARE/NULL behavior is not inferred.

Rules can edit, enable, disable, reorder and preview these conditions individually or in all/any groups, including split-child matching. Existing first-writer priority, additive tags, stop-processing behavior, protected fields, explicit override and account/date/currency scopes remain effective. Saving never runs historical changes. Preview receipts bind current workspace/options; writes and run history commit atomically. Enabled conditions run within existing direct and resumable staged CSV approvals, preserving source-based fingerprints, ownership, retries and reviewed reversal/reapproval. A failed pattern or matching budget aborts the proposed graph before persistence.

Each pattern has a 200-Unicode-character limit, valid-text/null checks, conservative pre-compile counted-repeat expansion bound of 20,000 and maximum 2,048 compiled instructions. Even literal/quoted counted repeats contribute to the conservative expansion check. The workspace has at most 32 distinct patterns and 8,192 compiled instructions in total, including disabled rules. Pattern-count bounds are checked before compilation, both for a single proposed rule and the entire workspace; runs also check their reachable rule set. A request-local work counter bounds sum(program instructions × max(1, field UTF-16 length)) to 50,000,000 before matching. The compiled-pattern cache holds at most 32 entries and uses capture-state matching without retaining a potentially separate 8 MiB DFA per expression. These are deterministic port resource bounds, not a claim of PostgreSQL statement-timeout equivalence. Large history runs can narrow scope; CSV files can need fewer rows or simpler patterns. No user pattern executes with JavaScript `RegExp`. Lookarounds/backreferences and unsupported syntax reject explicitly.

Native Sure regex conditions remain disabled for explicit syntax review with their complete original configuration retained. Incoming unmarked Sure CSV behaves the same: reject by default, or preserve disabled when that option is selected. A rebuilt Sites rule can be enabled after review. Sites rule CSV exports use an explicit `re2-v1` marker and port-namespaced condition types `sites_re2_transaction_name` / `sites_re2_transaction_notes`; the pinned upstream condition inclusion list does not accept those types, so export does not silently reinterpret an active RE2 expression as PostgreSQL. Own dialect-marked CSV reimports normalize back to the same operator and exact pattern. Unknown dialects reject. Original blocked PostgreSQL configurations never gain a trusted dialect marker on export. Previously blocked rules remain blocked after deployment.

Financial JSON/ZIP format 35 retains regex conditions and remaps only actual record references, not literal pattern text. The actual retained version-85 rule schema/evaluator and format-35 reader were executed: valid legacy graphs remain readable, while new regex conditions are rejected rather than dropped or applied through a fallback. An older Worker can still read state, but mutations that validate a workspace containing new conditions may fail closed. Use this release or later to edit/restore those workspaces. No new record shape, migration, table, column, backfill, writer epoch or backup-format change is introduced: all 69 SQL files, the complete journal, Drizzle schema and hosting manifest (72 files) are byte-identical to version 85, with 67 expected user tables at schema 68. Existing dependency records are unchanged; only RE2JS is added, with its complete MIT license distributed in public assets and the source archive.

Validation adds 26 behavioral cases (2,304 total) and 155 focused real-workerd/D1/R2 API checks. Coverage includes Unicode/raw interior whitespace/default and inline flags, invalid syntax, escaped literal backslashes, nested repeats, compile/run/workspace bounds before effects, ownership/origin/stale/tampered requests, field protections/override, actual history-budget failure, injected late rule-log rollback and concurrent approvals, direct/staged CSV budget rollback, complete 10,000-row approval/reversal/reapproval and final pages, trusted/untrusted saved rule CSV originals and dialects, ZIP-remapped finance and preserved native disabled originals. Fourteen related suites pass 1,482 API checks (1,637 total across fifteen suites): existing rules, staged rule/transaction CSV, account/entity mapping/creation, source reversal/native recovery, category/merchant merging, workbook extraction, core finance and migration. The historical full API corpus was not all rerun.

Final strict TypeScript/build/package and the complete fresh-Worker persistence corpus are publication gates. The new probe checks five exact owner graphs, raw saved patterns and protections, native original configuration, remapped full-limit finance, pending history/source reviews, the complete last source page and exact original/export hashes. All financial fixtures are synthetic local data; native production verification is publication/access/schema metadata only. Hosted sign-in, keyboard, responsive and visual QA remain unverified. PostgreSQL regex dialect parity, other unfinished personal-finance workflows and the broader port remain in progress. Bank/broker connections and household sharing remain excluded by the user.
